*The front door bursts open, and from my room, I can hear the din of Tara entering.*
Tara: "-gotta be related to the stuff in the journals. There's no way they're not talking about the same thing."
Tara: "Just send all those pics to my email address when you can. This is gonna be soooo freaking good."
Morgan: "Sure thing."
*I sit paralyzed in my bed. Should I ignore them? Go out and act like nothing's out of the ordinary?*
*Part of me wants to just hide in my room, but the way Tara and I have always gotten over our arguments is by being open about our issues.*
*Plus, Tara would know that I'm at the cabin. So if she brought Morgan back here, I doubt she's trying to avoid me.*
*Maybe they came back specifically to include me.*
*Hoping for the best, I head out into the living room.*
*Tara's brewing a pot of coffee, while Morgan leans against the counter. A few books that she must have brought from home are stacked beside her.*
*She glances up as I enter.*
Morgan: "Hey, Maddie."
Madison: "Hey there."
*Tara nods without facing me.*
Madison: "What uh, what are the books?"
*I nod at the stack. Tara sets two cups of coffee in front of her and Morgan, and slides the books toward herself.*
Tara: "They're the journals she mentioned on the walk to the church. She was letting me take a look at them."
*She puts emphasis on the word 'me,' like she's the only one who's allowed to. Maybe that's the case.*
*This isn't off to a great start.*
Madison: "I see you've picked up a coffee habit too, huh? Morgan's really rubbed off on you."
Tara: "Yeah."
*She turns to face Morgan, being sure to exclude me from what she says.*
Tara: "Okay, we can chill in my room. Might wanna grab one of the spare chairs from out here."
Morgan: "Oh. Okay."
*It's a small consolation, but I can tell that even Morgan feels a little uncomfortable with the situation. She gives me a sympathetic look, but doesn't say anything.*
*Just why is Tara acting like this? If she would just tell me, we could work it out.*
*I stick out an arm, halting Morgan as she carries the chair across the cabin.*
Madison: "Tara, can I talk to you for a sec?"
*The two of them exchange a look, but I can't tell what it's supposed to mean. Then she shrugs. I gesture towards my room, and she follows me in. I shut the door.*
Madison: "Can you please like, tell me why you're mad at me?"
Tara: "I'm not mad at you."
*I scoff.*
Madison: "Whatever you wanna call it, why are you treating me like this?"
Tara: "Like what?"
*I can already feel myself starting to get angry, but I try to keep it under control.*
Madison: "Please don't play dumb like that. You barely talk to me for three days, you ditch me this morning, and now you're going out of your way to exclude me."
Madison: "I don't know how to interpret that as anything other than 'mad.'"
Madison: "I have no idea what I said or did to piss you off, so I don't even know what to apologize for."
*Tara runs a hand through her hair, and looks around my room. It's the first time she's been in here since we arrived in Eysenfeld.*
*It's more or less a carbon copy of hers, so I don't know what's so interesting about it.*
Tara: "I'm not mad. I'm just not wasting your time with this stuff anymore. I can take it from here."
*I'm not quite sure I heard her right.*
Madison: "Sorry, what?"
Tara: "I said I can take it from here."
Madison: "Take… what, exactly?"
Tara: "The vid. I mean, you're quitting anyway, so I've gotta learn how to do all this on my own, right?"
Madison: "But-"
Madison: "That's not-"
*I don't even know what to say to that.*
Madison: "Why not ask me at least? Or even tell me. That's so… So screwed up."
Tara: "How do you figure? Every day since we've got here you've talked about or at least shown how you don't wanna be here."
Tara: "You even said it yourself - you had to 'make an effort' to be less bitchy whenever we go out."
Tara: "So now that there's a solid lead, I can figure out the rest of it myself. Or at least with Morgan's help."
Tara: "If you want, I can buy you an early train ticket home or something. Next one should be coming through in a day or two. You don't have to keep pretending to care."
*The whole time, her tone never changes. It's flat and emotionless. I wonder if that's another result of Morgan's influence.*
*I wait, expecting there to be more. Hoping that there's more.*
*There isn't.*
*I stand there, dumbfounded. Tara shrugs again, and then leaves, closing my bedroom door behind her.*
Tara: "Okay! Sorry about that. Let's get those journals."
*I can still hear her talking to Morgan through the wall, bright and chipper.*
*My legs feel weak all of a sudden, and I collapse onto my bed.*
*Through everything - my own misgivings and concerns about my future, choosing to quit the show and telling Tara about it, and even witnessing the monster at the church - I've managed to keep my composure.*
*But for the first time in I don't know how long, I can't stop it any longer.*
*I cry.*
*All of this feels so unfair. Of course I didn't want to be here - it's been a waste of time, every single other time.*
*I get it. I was the skeptic, the nonbeliever, only humoring her in all her exploits and playing as her foil. I rained on her parade constantly.*
*And now, after everything that’s happened, she was right. I can't deny that. But why does that mean I don't deserve to be involved with it now?*
*Maybe she took my leaving personally, even though I swore that wasn't the case.*
*I was only doing what was right for me. I needed to move on and do something with my life, so is it really my fault that meant I had to stop playing around with paranormal stuff?*
*After these past couple days, I was even starting to reconsider that. Maybe staying on the show wouldn't be so bad. Once the episode goes live, everything will be changing.*
*But now I don't feel like that's an option. Like she'd even want me there anymore.*
*I feel like everything in my life has changed in just a few short days; like I'm lost at sea without anything to hold onto.*
*My best friend in the whole world is at the epicenter of it all, and I'm thousands of miles from home.*
*Through the walls, I can faintly hear the sound of Morgan and Tara discussing whatever it is. Not nearly loud enough for me to make out any words, but enough to be reminded of my exclusion.*
*I wonder if Tara knows how upset I am, or if she expected me to be grateful. Neither would surprise me.*
*I cry until my eyes are dry and sore, but I don't feel any better.*
*The walls of my bedroom feel claustrophobic, and the vague sound of Tara talking is insufferable.*
*I hurriedly bundle up, and slip out of my bedroom and out the cabin to get some fresh air.*
*The cold is just barely more manageable today, but it’s still preferable to the atmosphere inside. It stings my wet cheeks, burning reminders of my unhappiness.*
*I pick a direction at random and start heading into the forest. As long as I don't make any twists or turns, and just go in a straight line, I won't be able to get lost.*
*After what I've seen, the woods feel especially hostile now. I wonder what else could be lurking in the shadows or farther amongst the trees. Countless possibilities that I'd never considered are suddenly plausible.*
*But for once I don't hesitate. I just walk, my feet automatically stepping over roots and other forest debris.*
*Underfoot, the snow is slushy and messy. My head feels the same.*
*My heart hurts. Everything hurts. My nose burns from crying, the cold only exacerbating the effect.*
*What am I supposed to do? What am I supposed to say?*
*I just feel lost.*
*I reflect on the show, and on the years I've spent with Tara, and wonder how it all came to this.*
*The show had been her idea, of course, but I was the one who encouraged her to go through with it. She'd always had a flair for the dramatic. Whether it was the school play, her old blog, or even the over-the-top fanfic she used to write. And I'd always been there to support her, whatever it was. I'd go over her lines with her and help her rehearse, or even edit her fics. When she first had the idea for Taranormal, and combining her two biggest interests - cryptids and herself - I was the only person who said she should go for it. I even came up with the name, which I'm still pretty proud of.*
*At first, I thought it would just be another entry in a long list of harebrained ideas that Tara would start and quickly lose interest in. I gave it two, maybe three episodes tops before she got bored. But the reception was good, and the views started coming in. So did the ad revenue, and the sponsorships.*
*What had started as a weekend hobby turned into a weekend responsibility, and then into a day job. My role went from holding a cheap camera and stitching together the footage to actually learning proper video editing and investing in decent equipment. It seemed only natural that I'd be the one to handle all of the invites, fanmail, inquiries, and more. The professional side of things.*
*I put my life on hold for the show. Quit college halfway through my film studies degree, and moved out of my cheap student housing. I gave up my barista job, and the store manager promotion I'd been hoping for. I dropped everything in order to help Tara make the best show possible.*
*Sure, it wasn't like I had it rough. Tara's always split the royalties with me, and pays for the expenses herself. The two of us have done pretty well for ourselves; better than we had any right to expect. But at the end of the day, it was still always about Tara.*
*Her name in the title. Her name on the contracts. Her name in the fanmail. Tara, Tara, Tara. And when it's all over; when the final episode someday goes up and she moves on, she'll be able to take that name with her anywhere. That success. That popularity.*
*And all I'll have is the memory.*
*The more I dwell on it, the more unfair Tara's behavior seems. I can feel anger overcoming my sadness.*
*How could she say I'm only pretending to care? I gave up years of my life for her, and dedicated them to making her look good.*
*Without me, she'd have never been able to balance running the business side of the show while actually making it. Hell, she's not even close to being tech-savvy enough to do what I do.*
*Part of me wants to spin around right now and go back to the cabin, and demand an apology.*
*But I know how that would go. She'd just get defensive. And even if she did say she's sorry, she wouldn't mean it. It wouldn't actually fix anything. We'd just be back to where we started when we first got to Eysenfeld. Awkward and tense.*
*There isn't really a good solution that I can think of. But I have to do or say something. I can't just leave things as they are.*
*Her offer for an early trip home lingers in my mind. I'm sure she'd make good on that promise. It sounds pretty tempting, too. In less than three days, I could be comfortable, warm, and back in my own bed.*
*I'd never have to think about Morgan, or Eysenfeld, or forest spirits or weird churches or floating lights again. I could just move on with my life, the same way I've been planning to once I get home anyway.*
*As I think that, though, I know it's not really an option. I'd never be able to live with myself if I let it all go unresolved.*
*Two piercing red eyes shine in my memory, and I know that there's no way I'd be able to forget about them.*
*Remembering the monster makes me stop and take stock of my surroundings. I've been walking at a pretty good rate, unhindered by the weather and fueled by my bad mood.*
*There's a small clearing just up ahead, and it looks like it might be a good spot to take a short break.*
*It's not very big, I realize when I reach it. I could toss a stone from one side to the other. It doesn't feel as stifling as the rest of the forest, though.*
*I clear my throat. It's the only noise I can hear. The sounds of the village don't travel this far. They barely reach the cabin, for the most part.*
*Overhead, the sky is a crystal clear blue for the first time that I can remember. It's still freezing, but it isn't cloudy. Not even an airplane or vapor trail disturb the tranquility.*
*I rub my eyes and defog my glasses. I must look like a real mess out here. I do feel a little bit better though. The fresh air alone has helped to clear my head.*
*The snow underfoot is well-trampled from my stomping around, and mud mixes with the slush to form a thin paste that sticks to my boots.*
*If there was somewhere to sit down, this place would be perfect. I make do with leaning against a tree that's mostly dry.*
*I'll need to remember how I got here. It would be nice to have a place of my own to retreat to when I need it, away from the other two.*
*Just as I'm enjoying the tranquility, there's a rustling sound overhead, and I look up just in time to get a face full of snow as it slides off a branch.*
*Perfect timing.*
*I cough and splutter, wiping away as much of it as I can, though a lot of it still falls into my clothes. I flap my jacket like wings, trying to knock it out.*
*Right then, from the corner of my eye, a quick motion catches my attention. It's gone too fast for me to notice more than a glimpse of fabric and I turn around to see who it was.*
*But there's nothing there, and no sign of anything having been there.*
*Heart pounding, I walk over in the direction it came from, looking for footprints or something.*
*There's nothing.*
*I rub my eyes. Maybe it was the snow, messing with my vision.*
*SNAP*
*The harsh, unmistakable sound of a branch breaking cracks behind me.*
*I spin around, expecting to see Tara there with a dumb smile on her face for having spooked me.*
*But again, there's nothing.*
*I pull my coat tighter around myself and press my back against a tree.*
Madison: "Hello? Is someone there?"
*My voice resounds through the clearing but goes unanswered.*
*In the middle of the clearing, a mysterious woman appears for just a moment. She has long pink hair and a lavender nightgown. Despite the snow, she's barefoot.*
*Again, a flash of movement from the corner of my eye. This time I'm sure I see it: a glimpse of purple, darting behind a tree.*
*I start towards it, hand reaching, but without a clear plan in mind. Just like a dog chasing cars, I don't know what I'll actually do if I catch it.*
*I walk into the middle of the clearing, turning a slow circle, hoping to spot whatever it was.*
Madison: "Can you hear me? Hello?"
*It already knows I'm here. There's no point in trying to hide.*
*Snow crunches off to my left and I pivot, just in time to see a girl disappear behind yet another tree.*
Madison: "Hey, wait!"
*I may as well not be saying anything, for all the effect it seems to have.*
*I hurry to the tree where I saw her, but she's gone. I'm just running in circles, chasing after who-knows-what.*
*My heart beats like a kick drum. A million possibilities race through my head, each more fantastic than the last.*
*Maybe she's from the village. Maybe I imagined her. Maybe she's another forest spirit. Maybe she's the same forest spirit we saw before, in a different form.*
*Any of them could be true. I've seen now that nothing is impossible.*
*I look all around, searching for a clue and finding none.*
*This is a bad idea, I know. Every single fairy tale I've ever read warns that this is a bad idea. Chasing after mysterious girls in the woods never ends well.*
*She could be luring me to her secret lair, or could even be a disguise for some kind of terrible monster. Maybe it's the forest spirit, come to exact revenge for trespassing in its territory.*
*I should turn and run, and race back to the cabin. If I told Tara and Morgan, I'm sure this is the kind of thing that would override Tara's annoyance, at least for now.*
*It could be our chance to patch things up; pursuing some new mystery.*
*Or…*
*I could not tell Tara. And I could solve the mystery myself. Whatever it is.*
*For the first time, something happened to me that doesn't involve Tara. I could keep it that way.*
*My hand is trembling, but I reach out in the direction I last saw the girl, more as a sign of openness than anything else.*
Madison: "Are you still there?"
*Silence.*
Madison: "Um, if you can hear me… can you let me know?"
*No response.*
*My fingers close over empty air.*
*This is stupid. I feel like an idiot, talking to no one.*
*If she wanted to be caught, she would've let me, I'm sure.*
*But then, if she didn't, then what was the point of even showing herself?*
Madison: "I'll come back tomorrow, okay?"
Madison: "If you'll be here again."
*It feels desperate. It is desperate.*
*But I can't let this chance go. Not when this could be all I have.*
*A light breeze blows through, as if to answer.*
*Maybe it is. Maybe it isn't.*
*I stand there, hoping for another sign.*
*As I give up and turn back towards the path home, I see it, for just a split second: a bright pink light, crystal clear and the color of a sunset, flashes just once.*
*It's the same as the one we chased through the forest several days ago.*
*My breath catches in my throat. My muscles tighten, poised to follow, but my common sense overrides.*
*As much as I want to, I can't just go running off into the forest on my own. I'd probably never find my way back.*
*I swallow. If it can understand me, like it seems to, then I'll see it tomorrow. Once I've had more time to prepare.*
*I face the direction the light came from, even though it's gone now.*
Madison: "I'll be back tomorrow."
Madison: "I promise."
*Morgan and Tara never notice me come home. They might not have even noticed that I left. I don't make any efforts to talk with them. I'm still too angry with Tara. Instead I spend the rest of my day in my room, watching films or reading. All of the research that I was so eagerly working on is put aside, stuffed into a folder that's out of sight on my hard drive. I have my own project now; my own lead to explore.*
*The thought of that light, and the mysterious girl in the woods, stays on my mind through it all. Even late into the night, I can hear that Morgan's still over, in Tara's room with her. At some point, the sounds go from conversation to something else. I turn up the volume on my computer. They can do whatever they want. It's well past midnight when my eyes finally get heavy enough to sleep.*
*The next day, I head straight to the clearing without telling anyone, nearly 24 hours to the minute since I left it. From the thick clouds overhead, I can tell it's going to be a short trip. Still, I have to go, at least to see.*
*My heart thumps with anticipation, and more than a little nervousness. It's an odd mix. I force myself to keep a calm demeanor, and not get too worked up.*
*It's difficult.*
*I'm hopeful, but I don’t know what for. I don’t have a camera, or even my cellphone - there’s no way I could prove anything that happens. And it’s not like there’s anyone who could vouch for me; say that I saw what I saw.*
*For now, it's important that I keep it that way. This encounter, whatever it may be, will be mine and mine alone.*
*Arriving at the clearing, I brace myself for what I might find.*
*Nothing's changed. I'm met with silence. There’s the faint sound of trees rustling in the wind, and nothing more.*
Madison: "Hello? Are you out there?"
*I give it a moment, waiting and hoping for a response that never comes.*
*Maybe it really was my eyes playing tricks on me. The light could've been a reflection of the sun, and the girl could've been…*
*The girl could have been… *
*There isn't a doubt left in my mind that the girl was there. There's no way I didn't see someone.*
*Frustrated, I kick at some of the mush on the ground. It splashes up and splatters my legs.*
Madison: "Are you there? I'm waiting!"
Madison: "Please…"
*I feel like a fool. I'm pleading with someone that may or may not exist to manifest. If someone had told me a week ago that I'd be here doing this today, I'd have laughed at them.*
*Everything about this situation is unlike me.*
*My irritation builds up. With myself, with Tara, with the stupid ghost light. With everything!*
*Whatever was there is gone now. And really, what kind of an idiot convinces herself to come back for an imaginary date with a weird breeze?*
*I clench my fist, looking without seeing.*
*Slowly, I turn circles, hoping for something. Anything. I traipse back and forth at an increasingly desperate pace, not really sure what kind of clue I'm searching for, but just knowing that I can't sit still.*
*If I sit still then I'm giving up. And if I give up then I really don't have anything left for me out here.*
*For the second day in a row, I feel the sting of tears in my eyes, and I pinch my eyes shut to stop them.*
*Not here. Not today.*
*I hold my hand there, and suck in a deep breath. The cold air stings my throat. I steady my breathing, fighting back the urge to cry.*
*After a moment, once the urge has passed, I lower my hand and open my eyes.*
*And she’s there.*
*So silently and so suddenly that it's like she was never not there.*
*The mysterious girl stands before me, barefoot in the snow but seemingly unfazed. She regards me with a curious look, her eyes wide, like I’m the one who's out of place.*
*Now that she's here, right in front of me, I realize that I don't know what to say.*
Madison: "H-hello."
*… is all I could muster. My voice comes out weaker than I expected.*
*Her eyes go wide at my word. Her lips move, but no sound comes out.*
*She mouths something else again, still without a voice.*
Madison: "I can’t hear you."
*A frown.*
*She turns away. Have I disappointed her? Is she going to disappear again?*
Madison: "Please don’t go!"
*I don’t know why I want her to stay. Something is clearly different; unnatural. This isn’t a normal girl.*
*But that’s exciting, in and of itself.*
*The girl turns back around to face me again. She looks just as confused as I feel.*
*There’s a nervous sort of energy to her. Beneath the gown she’s wearing, far too thin for the weather, the girl’s legs are tense and ready to bolt.*
*I take a step towards her, holding out my hand the same way I’d approach a kitten or lost puppy.*
Madison: "My name’s Madison. Can… can you understand me?"
*She still looks a bit nervous, but nods.*
*So we speak the same language. That’s good. Is she from Eysenfeld? I can’t imagine she’s from anywhere else. There’s nothing else around.*
Madison: "You were talking, right? Just a bit ago. But I couldn’t hear you."
*Another nod.*
*That means that for whatever reason, her words can’t reach me. Maybe she’s naturally mute? Or maybe her voice was magically stolen? *
*Every idea that comes to mind is more ridiculous than the last, but I consider them all equally.*
*To my surprise, I’m able to remain calm and, by my standards, pretty level-headed.*
*It seems like the girl is more afraid of me than vice-versa.*
*I drop my hand down, offering a handshake.*
*She stares at it for a moment, maybe expecting it to turn into a snake or for me to shoot magic bolts from my fingers. When neither of those happen, she gingerly, slowly reaches for my hand.*
*It passes right through me. Her fingers glide through the palm of my hand like a breeze disappearing into the air.*
*With a sad smile she looks down, seemingly expecting this result.*
*Horrific realization slowly dawns on me. With the same tinge of certainty that guided me out here.*
*This girl isn’t really here, in the same way that I am.*
Madison: "You’re a ghost."
*A slow, sad nod. She looks apologetic about it; for inconveniencing me with her lack of a corporeal form.*
Madison: "I’m sorry."
*The words feel weak and inadequate, and nearly like a non-sequitur. But really, what do you say to a ghost?*
*She shakes her head vigorously, hair flying about, trying to rid herself of some bad thought. She gives me a big smile and stretches her arms wide, turning about to gesture to the trees around us.*
Madison: "What is it? I don’t understand."
*The girl bites her lip, wondering how to better convey her message. Then with a decisive nod, she points at me, and then points at the ground between us.*
*Pausing a moment, she holds that pose. Then she points at the trees, in particular the path I’d come from, and shakes her head.*
*A flash of fear knifes its way under my skin. Is she threatening me? She definitely doesn’t look like it.*
Madison: "Um, something about where I came from?"
*She nods, and then repeats the pointing. First at me, then at the ground.*
Madison: "Me… right here…?"
*Another nod.*
Madison: "You’re asking if I’ll stay here?"
*She nods and claps, hopping up.*
*Except she doesn’t come down. She hovers there as if suspended by invisible strings, then slowly lowers herself back to the ground.*
*I’m not sure if it’s what she was going for, but I smile.*
Madison: "Are you showing off?"
*She laughs and nods, glad to be understood.*
Madison: "I can stay for a bit, sure. If you want me to, I mean."
*The girl practically zooms the short distance between us, moving with what might be supernatural speed. She stands so close that her gown phases through me, uncomfortable in its lack of sensation.*
*And she nods again. This time solemn and hopeful.*
*I can’t fight the feeling that she could easily be setting some kind of trap or leading me astray, but I still don’t get that impression from her. So I agree.*
Madison: "Okay. I’d be happy to."
Madison: "But I can’t stay too long, or… Tara will start to wonder where I am."
*Tara again. For the first time since we arrived, I was able to fully push her from my thoughts. I was so overwhelmed by meeting this girl that Tara didn’t even register on my consciousness.*
*But now, there she is again. She’s more haunting than the ghost.*
*The ghost in question mouths Tara's name - one of the few words I can actually lip read.*
Madison: "The person I came here with. She’s um… Back in the village."
*The girl’s eyes go wide and she points in the direction of the forest, then makes a dragging motion back to where we are.*
*This one’s easy to understand.*
Madison: "No, she’s not coming here. She has no idea."
*For the first time, I’m glad the girl can’t speak, because it means she can’t ask me to elaborate.*
*I don’t want to think about Tara right now.*
Madison: "Anyway, were you here yesterday too? Watching me?"
*A nod, wholly unapologetic.*
Madison: "I thought so. I also thought I might be crazy, though."
Madison: "At this point, I wouldn’t be surprised by either one."
*She looks surprised. I wonder if she knows about the other supernatural occurrences in the forest? Maybe they’re connected somehow.*
Madison: "The other day, my friend and I were out in the forest."
*Nod.*
Madison: "And suddenly, there was this huge snowstorm. Out of nowhere. And we saw this… This giant… thing."
*I stretch my hands to indicate just how enormous it was. But it the enormity and terror of it are impossible to convey.*
Madison: "I don’t even know how to describe it. It was a monster of some kind. Like a huge tree had come alive and was walking in the snow. Do you know what I’m talking about?"
*Not only does the girl nod, but she laughs, finding something funny about my story.*
*I’m not sure if I should be worried or not.*
Madison: "So you’ve seen it… Is it like… evil?"
*A firm shake of her head.*
Madison: "That’s good. It was honestly the scariest thing I’ve ever seen in my life."
*After that, we taper off into silence. Well, I do.*
*I don't have a reason to believe her, but so far she doesn't seem threatening at all.*
*I have a hard enough time holding up my end of a conversation under normal circumstances. This just makes it even harder.*
*The two of us just stand there, looking at each other.*
*Now that she’s not moving around so much I’m able to get a better look at her.*
*She seems to be around my age, if not a bit younger. Her hair is long and slightly unruly, reaching well down her back. She reminds me a bit of Rapunzel, all alone in the forest. A cute bow midway down helps keep her hair in place. Her wide eyes are curious, but slightly sad. Still, she smiles so easily that I wonder if I'm just imagining the sorrow.*
*As green as emeralds, her eyes kind of remind me of Morgan's. I was so taken aback by her sudden appearance and well, ghostliness, that at first I didn't realize… she's beautiful.*
*Her choice of attire makes a lot more sense now that I know she can't feel the cold. The gown she’s wearing has a few dangling threads and scuffed spots, which I assume haven’t changed since she…*
*Died.*
*I shudder, trying not to think of my own mortality. What was it like? Why is she here, instead of whatever usually happens when you die? Does she know what she's missing? An afterlife, or lack thereof? I have a million questions, about her life, and about life itself. There’s so much I want to ask, but it’s impossible.*
*Plus, we’ve only known each other a few minutes. It feels like that would be rude to ask right away.*
*A thick snowflake lands on my cheek, startling me. I'd pretty much forgotten about the weather. Overhead, the clouds are getting worse, and the snow is starting to fall more heavily.*
*As much as I want to spend more time with her, I really don't want to be caught out in a blizzard a second time.*
Madison: "I need to go back now, but will you be here again?"
Madison: "I'd like to… to see you again. If you'd like that too."
*She nods emphatically, with a grin to match. I can't help but smile in return. It's nice to know that someone is looking forward to seeing me.*
Madison: "Okay. I'll come back as soon as I can. I promise."
Madison: "Although… I'm not sure when the weather will let me do that."
Madison: "On the next sunny day, I'll be there, okay?"
*She nods.*
Madison: "Okay. I'll see you soon then, I hope."
*The girl waves goodbye as I turn to leave, remaining in the center of the clearing.*
*As I get my last look at her, I know that I'm not imagining the sadness in her eyes now.*
*I glance behind several times on the walk back, each time expecting her to have disappeared once more.*
*But she stays there, waving, until the trees obscure her from sight.*
*A splash of purple fabric against the brown and white of the forest.*
*A little spark of light to look forward to.*
*With almost every step I take, I cast a glance behind me. I keep expecting to hear the crunch of snow, or spot a flash of red hair as Tara follows me. But there’s nothing.*
*I’m alone.*
*I hurry through the woods practically on autopilot. Less than a week ago I’d have never set foot in the forest on my own, but now I already have the path to the clearing memorized.*
*It gives me extra time to worry.*
*What am I even doing? Everything about this situation is extremely not-me.*
*Heading off on my own into a dark forest, without telling anyone, to go meet up with a ghost…*
*Even thinking the word 'ghost' still makes my heart lurch. I’ve seen her with my own eyes, and yet I still have trouble believing it.*
*And I’m just trusting that she’s not going to lead me to her lair and suck out my soul or something like that.*
*I feel like Gretel, and I’m not even cautious enough to leave a breadcrumb trail.*
*But still, I keep walking.*
*There's something exciting in being on my own. In doing something without Tara.*
*I can't remember the last time I had a secret from her; at least not a big one.*
*Not telling her about the ghost girl feels a little bit like rebellion. I feel like I deserve something exciting that's all my own.*
*She and Morgan can go… do whatever. I doubt Tara will even notice that I'm not around.*
*And even if she does, it's something that she needs to get used to. I've tried to show her how to cut a video, or frame a shot, lots of times.*
*It's her own fault if she still doesn't get it.*
*Just like it's her own fault that she's missing out now.*
*As I approach the clearing, I slow down a little.*
*My usual trepidation overtakes me once again as I contemplate just how reckless this is.*
*There's absolutely no guarantee that the girl isn't dangerous. That I'll walk out of these woods alive.*
*Normally I'd chastise Tara for following her gut instinct, which dictates probably 70 percent of her decisions.*
*Yet that's all that I have to go off right now. The feeling that I'm safe with the ghost girl. That she won't harm me.*
*I cross the threshold of trees and into the clearing. Just like the other times, it's serenely quiet.*
*Even my past footsteps are gone, erased by the wind or snowfall.*
Madison: "I'm here."
*Somehow, I feel like she already knows.*
*The forest swallows my words, returning to frozen, pristine silence.*
*Soundlessly, she steps out from behind a tree.*
*A small smile sits on her face, though her eyes are slightly downcast.*
*She waves, meeting my eyes for just a moment before glancing away.*
Madison: "Hi there."
*My words hang in the still air.*
Madison: "Were you waiting for me?"
*We lock eyes again, and she nods. Quickly; earnestly.*
Madison: "I hope it wasn't for too long."
*She shakes her head and then she laughs. I’m not sure what the joke is, though.*
Madison: "What? What's so funny?"
*Once she catches her breath, she bites her lip as she ponders. After a moment, she shrugs, looking apologetic.*
*I guess explaining a joke through gestures would be difficult.*
*She sidles up next to me, and I fight the urge to step back. Though I've decided to trust her, my instincts still belie my nerves.*
*But what she seems to be most interested in is the bag slung over my shoulder.*
*She simulates opening the bag, so that she can see into it.*
Madison: "Nosy, huh?"
*Immediately, she backsteps, waving her hands in a frantic, apologetic motion.*
Madison: "Sorry, I was just kidding. You can look."
*She regards me like a timid deer as I hold the bag open for her to see. Inside are a couple books that I picked up in the village this morning.*
*I hold up the first one, small and square and baby blue.*
Madison: "I got a really strange look from the person who runs the bookstore because of this one. It's a book of baby names."
*Her eyes go wide, and she grasps at her belly then points at me. It takes me a second to understand what she means, and for some reason, I feel my face flush.*
Madison: "No! I'm not pregnant. But I thought… maybe you could use it to show me your name? If it's a name that would be-"
*Before I can finish my sentence, she's zoomed back over to me, all sense of personal space once again discarded.*
*She clasps her hands, a fiery look in her eyes, and… hops up and down.*
*Frankly, it's adorable.*
Madison: "Guess you're a fan. Okay, cool."
*She eyes the book hungrily. She must be really excited to share her name with me.*
*It's a little exciting for me, too.*
*It's nice to see someone so happy about being with me.*
*I fight to keep an overzealous grin off my face.*
Madison: "Okay, let's see… "
*I look around. Aside from trees and snow, there isn't much. I wonder if there's something about this place in particular that the girl likes.*
Madison: "Is there somewhere nearby where we could sit down? It feels a little weird to just keep standing."
*Finger on her chin, she ponders. Then she turns and points towards the woods, even further in.*
*I can tell from the look in her eyes that she's uncertain.*
*I swallow a lump in my throat. I've come this far, after all.*
Madison: "Sure, that's fine. Lead the way."
*She does so, but never gets too far from my side. Every few steps she glances over at me, as if I'm making sure I'm still here.*
*That feels a little backwards to me, but in a nice way.*
*I don't really feel compelled to break the silence, either. Of course, part of that is because any conversation would be one-sided.*
*But also, she looks content. Like she's just happy to be here. I am too.*
*The silence doesn't feel vast and overwhelming, like it has with Tara. It feels warm.*
*Before too long, I realize what our destination is. This is nearly the same path that Morgan took us down just a couple dusks ago. I recognize some of the more grotesquely warped trees.*
Madison: "We're going to that church."
*Eyes wide, she nods probably surprised that I know of it.*
Madison: "One of the villagers showed it to us a few days ago. Me and my friend."
*It feels weird to just call Tara 'my friend.' Usually if I'm talking to someone about Tara, they've met her.*
*The girl just nods yet again.*
*As we reach the edge of the trees and the church itself comes into sight, I look around for traces of the monster we saw days before.*
*There are none to be seen, though. No snapped or toppled trees.*
*No footsteps as deep as I am tall.*
*Much like the girl beside me, there's no trace of its existence.*
*I look over to the door of the church, where she's patiently waiting for me.*
*Once again, I fight back all my fears and hesitations and head for the door, too.*
*It's too late to turn back now.*
*The door is still slightly ajar from our visit days ago. A thin trail of snow powders the entryway, but otherwise the church is dry.*
*It’s still unnaturally warm. I wonder if that has to do with the girl, too. From the way she walks, I can tell she’s familiar with the place.*
*She heads to the front, stopping just shy of the altar, where she turns around to face me.*
*Being back here is a little unnerving, and I slowly approach her. Though she hasn’t given me any reason to be suspicious, the image of those monstrous eyes is ingrained in my memory.*
*But this time, there’s no howling wind or earth-shaking footsteps. Just the one girl, waiting patiently.*
*I give her a smile as I sit down on one of the pews near the front. It’s hewn from stone, smooth and rigid. She takes a seat right beside me.*
*Normally, being this close to strangers makes me uncomfortable. But with her, I don’t really mind.*
*Part of it is, of course, the fact that I can’t feel her at all. But the other part is that… it feels kind of pleasant to sit together like this.*
*I hastily push that thought out of my head. She’s a ghost. She is literally transparent and insubstantial.*
*I rummage through the bag and grab the book of baby names.*
Madison: "Okay, so first…"
*She bounces up and down with anticipation as I tear off the plastic cling wrap and stuff it in the totebag.*
Madison: "Is the first letter early in the alphabet?"
*She nods and holds up one finger.*
Madison: "I? L?"
*Two shakes of her head. She touches her index fingers together and then uses her middle finger to cross between them.*
Madison: "Oh, an A."
*Enthusiastic nodding.*
*I flip to the first couple pages in the book, right in the middle of the A’s. The first name I spot is ‘Amber.’*
*Impatiently, she leans over me and motions to go further back, towards the start.*
*I turn the pages slowly, giving her time to scan.*
*On the very first page she holds out a hand to stop, and points. She’s practically hanging over my shoulder to show me.*
Madison: "Abigail."
Madison: "Your name is Abigail."
*She gives me the biggest, most radiant grin I’ve ever seen as she nods.*
*Her joy is infectious, and I can’t help but smile too.*
Madison: "Abigail."
*I repeat it, and she looks even more joyful.*
*A beam of light breaks through the glass window, illuminating dust motes that make it look as if she’s sparkling.*
*We lock eyes, and I feel my breath catch in my throat. The look in her eyes - pure, unadulterated happiness - is something I’ve never seen before.*
*They shine with tears. The good kind.*
*The kind that bring out the color of her eyes. The radiant kind.*
*I didn’t know I could make someone happy like this.*
*I quickly look away, back to the now irrelevant book in my lap.*
Madison: "A-anyway, Abigail."
Madison: "Um, I had a couple questions. Is that okay?"
*Abigail nods.*
Madison: "So, maybe this is kind of rude to ask, but… are you a ghost? Or are you something else?"
*Abigail closes her eyes and, with a sad smile, nods.*
Madison: "I’m sorry."
*I’m not sure what the appropriate response to something like that is.*
Madison: "Then, are you from Eysenfeld?"
*Nod.*
Madison: "Do you know a girl from there called Morgan?"
*She shakes her head. That makes sense. I’m sure Morgan would have mentioned her before if so.*
Madison: "So have you like… been a ghost for a long time then?"
*I cringe a little as I ask. It seems gauche, although I’m not entirely sure what proper etiquette is for this kind of ‘conversation.’*
*In answer to my question, she nods again.*
Madison: "Do you know how long?"
*A shrug.*
*She holds up two fingers, then two fists.*
Madison: "Two hundred…?"
*Another shrug, and a nod.*
Madison: "Two hundred years…"
*I murmur it to myself, in slight disbelief. It could be more. I doubt she’s been keeping track of every day.*
*The world out there, the world I come from - it may as well be another planet for her.*
Madison: "I’m sorry."
*It’s incomprehensible to me. I can’t fathom that much time passing, unable to talk to anyone. How lonely that must be.*
*She’s closed her eyes yet again. Though I’ve been interpreting her silence as serene, I wonder if that’s how she feels.*
*These yes or no questions are practically the extent of what I can ask her. There’s still such a gulf between who she is and what I know of her. Centuries’ worth of solitude.*
Madison: "In all that time… Have you ever spent time like this with someone else?"
*Without opening her eyes or looking at me, she shakes her head again.*
Madison: "I’m the only one?"
*Nod.*
*I fall silent too, my head still reeling. There are so many things that I want to ask and want to know. How did she die? Why did her spirit stick around? Does she know about the thing we saw in the snow?*
*Why am I the first person she’s shown herself to?*
*But there’s no way she could answer. And even if she could, I wonder if she’d want to. Is it impolite to remind a ghost that they’re dead?*
*It’s morbidly funny to me that these are questions I have to ask myself.*
*Tara would love this. Tara would…*
*I push that thought out of my head too. Tara’s not here right now. It’s just me and Abigail.*
Madison: "Would you like me to read that other book?"
*Her grin returns, the moment of melancholy dispelled.*
*Seems I made the right call in changing the subject.*

Tara: "I was going to bring it up today, but then all this stuff happened."
Morgan: "At least the two of you are talking a little. She must be feeling bad, too."
Tara: "Maybe. You should've heard her before you got here, though."
Morgan: "I'm sorry."
Tara: "Nah, don't be. It's like you said, this is on both of us."
Morgan: "You really don't know what happened to the footage? I promise I won't tell Maddie anything."
Tara: "Nope, it's a hundred percent legit. It was working fine, and then it wasn't. I didn't even drop my computer or spill water on it this time."
Morgan: "That's so strange."
Tara: "You're telling me. Mads is convinced I did something to screw it up, though."
*I have a niggling suspicion in the back of my mind, but I don't want to bring it up yet. I don't want to alarm Tara if I can help it.*
Morgan: "Has anyone-"
*Right in the middle of my sentence, the heater in the corner of the room makes a sizzling noise, and then dies.*
*Tara and I both stare at it.*
Tara: "Did that… Did you see that?"
Morgan: "I did."
Tara: "Goddddd."
*Clutching her head in her hands, Tara gets up and goes to inspect the heater. She looks around and underneath it, although it's clear she has no idea what she's actually looking for.*
*While her back is turned, I take a moment to peer out the window, scanning for anyone or anything out of the ordinary. I don't see anything.*
Madison: "What happened?"
Tara: "Heater died. Check it out."
Madison: "Wait, you're kidding me."
Tara: "I wish."
*Already, the cold is starting to set in.*
Madison: "Was it a short circuit or something?"
*I'm glad that she doesn't default to blaming Tara for this.*
*My suspicion gets a little stronger, too.*
Tara: "No clue. Morgan and I were sitting here when it suddenly died. Didn't give any warning or anything."
Tara: "It was a lot like the…"
*She trails off. She doesn't need to finish the sentence. I'm sure we're all thinking the same thing.*
Tara: "Like the footage."
*She says it anyway.*
*None of us say anything. Then, Maddie chuckles nervously.*
Madison: "That's a pretty weird coincidence."
Tara: "Do you two think that like, they're connected somehow?"
Madison: "What, the heater and the footage? There's no way that could be. It's just bad luck."
Tara: "Maybe."
*She gives me a look that says she doesn't think it's random. I'm inclined to agree.*
Tara: "Any luck with saving the video so far?"
Madison: "Not yet, but I just started. Give me a bit."
Tara: "Right, right."
Tara: "I'm gonna go grab a blanket. You want one, Morgan?"
Morgan: "No thanks."
Tara: "What the hell?"
*Two seconds after she's entered her bedroom, Tara yells and pops back out.*
Tara: "The heater in my room is dead too!"
Morgan: "What?"
Madison: "Are you sure?"
Tara: "Of course I'm sure! Come look if you want."
*Maddie and I both hurry into Tara's room. Sure enough, the heater in here is as dead as the one in the living room.*
Madison: "Were they on the same breaker? This cabin must have ancient wiring, right?"
Morgan: "No. It was renovated a couple years ago."
Tara: "Is the one in your room still working, Mads?"
*Without waiting for an answer, Tara pushes past and goes to look, ignoring Maddie's feeble clamor against barging in. We follow behind Tara yet again.*
*In Maddie's room, the heater is running fine.*
Tara: "That's so unfair!"
*Maddie frowns too, looking perplexed.*
Madison: "I don't get it. Why just in here?"
Tara: "That's a pretty weird coincidence."
*Tara does a poor imitation of Maddie's voice.*
Madison: "It is! What else do you want me to say?"
*Grumbling, Tara leaves the room without giving an actual answer. Maddie doesn't try to stop her, and stares at her perfectly functional heater.*
*I shut the bedroom door behind me as I leave.*
Tara: "Okay, tell me I'm not crazy. Something weird is going on."
Morgan: "You're not crazy. This definitely isn't a coincidence."
Tara: "You say that like you know what's going on."
*I can feel a pit opening up in my stomach. I've never been good with words, and I'm not sure how to phrase everything I'm about to tell her. Much less how she'll take it.*
*I must struggle for too long, because I can see Tara's expression go from mopey to concerned.*
Tara: "Hey, you okay? I was just kidding but like… do you actually know what's up?"
*I press a finger to my lips, and point to Tara's room. Her eyes go wide, but she nods. I lead the way, with her following close behind.*
Morgan: "We can talk now."
*Tara looks like she's going to explode.*
Tara: "Now? What the hell? That was like something out of a spy movie!"
Morgan: "Shh, not so loud."
*I listen for any indication that Maddie's followed behind us, or is eavesdropping, but her door never opens.*
*Tara watches, waiting for me to elaborate, but I can tell that it's taking every ounce of self-control she has to not barrage me with questions. It's understandable.*
Morgan: "Okay. Bear with me, because I'm not sure how to say some of this."
*Nearly vibrating with excitement, Tara nods. I take a deep breath.*
Morgan: "I don't think that all these malfunctions are a coincidence. I think they're intentional sabotage. And that my mother's to blame."
*Her eyes get as wide as saucers.*
Tara: "Your… your mom?"
Morgan: "Yes. And I also think that Maddie might be involved, too. It's not her fault at all. I'm afraid that my mother might have gotten to her first. She might not even know it."
Tara: "Gotten to her… how?"
Morgan: "As in she's not in control of her actions. She might not even be aware of them."
Tara: "You mean like… mind control?"
Morgan: "More or less, yes."
*She stares at me, and then starts to laugh. She gets up and paces around the room, her hands flying in useless motions as she laughs.*
Tara: "Okay. Okay, you're screwing with me, right? Mind control? Maddie?"
Morgan: "I'm not messing with you. I'm dead serious."
*I can't blame her for being incredulous. It's a lot to take in at once. And the rest of what I have to say is even stranger. But if there's anyone who will believe me - who will believe the truth - it's Tara.*
*Her laugh becomes a whistle as she calms down. She takes a second to compose herself, and then sits on the bed next to me.*
Tara: "Okay. Mind control. Go on."
Morgan: "Like I said, I doubt Maddie's even aware. It's probably more like sleepwalking, for her. But it would make sense. You said she's been spending a lot of time away from the cabin, right?"
Morgan: "And that she doesn't like to talk about where she's been. Plus the footage being damaged, and then the heaters dying. It makes sense."
Tara: "You're gonna have to sell me on this more. I wouldn't say that makes sense. What's the point of it? Why would your mom… do that? And how?"
Tara: "Like we haven't even approached the point of making sense."
Morgan: "I know. I'll explain."
*I'm trying to remain calm myself, but there's a nervousness that I've never felt before. This is my first time ever talking about this with someone else.*
*No matter how many times I'd imagined this conversation, and rehearsed it in my head, it's nothing like real life.*
Morgan: "My mom… isn't really my mom. To be frank, she's not even human. Not anymore."
*I pause, waiting to see how Tara takes it. No reaction. I doubt that anything I'm saying even registers as a possibility to her. My only choice is to go through it all, and hope that she believes me in the end.*
Morgan: "The person that you've met - the creature - is something else, using a human woman's body as a host."
Morgan: "She has the whole town under a glamor that makes them loyal to her, and she has for years. Longer than I've been alive. I think she has Maddie now, too."
Morgan: "The reason that I sent you an email and invited you to Eysenfeld isn't because of the forest spirit or any of that other stuff, although it's related, too."
Morgan: "The reason is because I need your help. Evelyn, or the thing inside her, is going to try to kill me soon, to use me as a new host. Within the next few weeks."
Morgan: "You were the only people in the world who I could think of who might believe me. There wasn't anyone else I could ask."
Morgan: "She knows that, too, which is why she's trying to get rid of you now. Ruin your trip. Make you want to leave."
Morgan: "She wouldn't ever hurt either of you. Too many people would notice if something happened. There'd be way too much unwanted attention on her."
Morgan: "But if she could get the two of you to leave Eysenfeld then she'd be able to proceed with her plans. I'm pretty sure that's what she's trying to do, through Maddie."
Morgan: "That's it, I guess."
*As I finish speaking, I look down at my legs. It feels like a weight has been lifted off of my chest. If nothing else, I'm glad that I got to tell someone.*
*Even if nothing changes, at least I got to share my story.*
*If she doesn't believe me; if she thinks I'm as crazy as Maddie does, then… Then I'm out of options.*
*The silence stretches on, and I glance up at Tara's face.*
*For someone usually so expressive and easy to read, her face is immutable. I can't tell what she's thinking. She just stares at me intensely. More scrutinizing than I've ever seen before.*
*I meet her eyes, willing her to search mine and see that I'm telling the truth. I want to reach across and grab her hand. More than that, I want her to grab mine.*
*Tara huffs, blowing a loose strand of hair that's fallen over her face. She blinks, pops her knuckles, scratches her leg. Then finally she half-smiles.*
Tara: "Okay, so then what do we do now?"
Morgan: "Does that… mean that you believe me?"
*My breath catches in my throat, scarcely daring to believe it. She hasn't confirmed yet. She could just be playing along.*
Tara: "Of course I do."
*I can't help myself. I lean forward and wrap my arms around her in a very selfish hug.*
Morgan: "I'm so sorry for dragging you into all of this."
*She doesn't deserve any of what's going on. I brought her into my own world of problems.*
*Her arms wrap back around me without hesitation.*
Tara: "Hey, I'm glad you did."
Morgan: "Really?"
Tara: "Of course! If some other paranormal blog got this scoop, I'd be jealous forever."
*We both laugh.*
Tara: "But… I'm not gonna lie, it's a lot to take in. Especially the stuff about Mads."
Tara: "But if never coming here would've meant you having to deal with all this on your own, then it's a damn good thing we're here."
*For the first time that I can remember, I feel warm and comfortable. It's a moment that I wish could last forever.*
*I don't deserve her support. But I'm infinitely glad that I have it.*
Tara: "Now let's go kick your mom's ass!"
*I laugh again. I'm glad that she's able to keep a light heart through all of this. We're going to need it.*
Tara: "Really though, what do we do now? Pack our stuff and get out of here? You could totally crash with me and Mads, I'm sure she wouldn't mind."
Tara: "We'd have to get past that whole mind control thing first, though."
Morgan: "No. Running away isn't an option. She would just take someone else instead. She has to be dealt with."
Tara: "You said it'll happen in a couple weeks, right? How do you know?"
Morgan: "It'll happen on the winter solstice. It's something to do with the moon. I don't really know the specifics, myself. There's not a whole lot of information to go off."
Tara: "You're in luck, because investigating stuff without a real lead is my specialty."
*She's incorrigible, and her smile is infectious. Her optimism and confidence makes me think that this might work out okay after all.*
Morgan: "There'll be plenty of time for that, I promise. Right now, I should head back home."
Tara: "Already? Feels like you just got here a bit ago."
Morgan: "I know. I wish I could stay longer. But I need to start preparing right away. There's a lot that I'll need to fill you in on, but it'll take some time for me to get ready."
Tara: "So like, later tonight?"
Morgan: "Probably tomorrow or the next day."
Tara: "Okay. I'll lie low until then. What should we do about…"
*Her voice trails off, and she nods in the direction of the rest of the cabin, and Maddie.*
Morgan: "Nothing for now. Just try to act normal around her, if you can."
Tara: "So avoid conversation as much as possible. Easy peasy."
*We stand up together, and Tara walks me to the front door.*
Tara: "So, see you soon, I guess?"
Morgan: "Right."
*She leans forward suddenly, giving me another quick hug. My second one today. It's over far too quickly.*
Tara: "Cool. I'll be waiting."
*I smile.*
Morgan: "Me too."
*It's always scary going home, and facing the prospect of seeing Evelyn face to face.*
*Today though, it doesn't seem as bad. There's still a long way to go. But with Tara's help, there's a chance that everything will work out.*
*That's more than I've ever been able to say before.*
*The skies are grey out when I wake up. I check my phone for the time, thinking that it must still be early. But no, it's almost 10.*
*It's the clouds that are making it so dark. We're definitely in for a lot of snow today. It looks like it could be more than any of the previous storms.*
*Sighing to myself, I get out of bed and get dressed without wasting any time.*
*There's no way I'm going to be able to go see Abigail today. Not with the weather as it is. I hope she won't wait up for me.*
*It's frigid in the living room. I spent a while trying to fix the heater, but to no avail. I don't even know what's wrong with it.*
*Tara must have had it pretty rough too. At least she could bundle up in blankets to keep warm. The door to her room is shut, so she's either gone or still asleep.*
*I'm glad that things have improved between us. She seemed to have a change of heart or something, because a couple days ago she started making small talk again.*
*Even our argument over the footage wasn't as explosive as it could've been. If it had happened three days prior, we probably wouldn't be on speaking terms at all anymore.*
*I make myself a bowl of cereal and have a quiet breakfast, alone.*
*Although I've gotten used to not interacting with Tara much, there were still my visits with Abigail to look forward to.*
*Now there's no telling how many days it'll be until the weather's cleared up enough for me to go see her again. Hopefully it'll blow over quickly. It's already starting to snow a little.*
*Just to pass the time, I move my laptop out into the living room and start an episode of a show I've been meaning to watch. I keep one headphone out so that I'll be able to hear if Tara leaves her room.*
*She never does. After two episodes, I get up and knock on her door. Unsurprisingly, there's no answer. I take a quick peek inside - empty. She must be at Morgan's again.*
*At this point, it's pretty obvious that they're… more than friends. I'm not sure how I feel about that. It's none of my business, of course. They're both adults and can do whatever they want. But, I can't help being a little envious.*
*It's been more than a year since I've been on a date. Things ended sort of messily with my last girlfriend, and since then I haven't really been looking. Working on the show kept me busy enough. Still, it wasn't that I was trying to avoid meeting anyone new. It just didn't happen. I'm not like Tara - I can't talk to strangers and carry on a conversation straight away. Business stuff, sure. Small talk? No way. I've always been a bit jealous of that.*
*So, seeing how quickly and how naturally Morgan and Tara ended up together feels like a painful reminder of my own social inadequacies. I guess there's an expiration date on their relationship, whatever it may be, though. It's hard to believe that we're already halfway through our trip in Eysenfeld.*
*It occurs to me that I'm in a similar situation with Abigail. Although our friendship is nothing like Morgan and Tara's, it's also time limited.*
*That thought… hurts. More than I expected it to.*
*I wish there was some way she could come back with us. I wonder how difficult it is to smuggle a ghost internationally. That's the kind of problem Tara would be great at solving, if she was around.*
*In fact, I wonder if maybe I should take this opportunity to try to mend things with her. Assuming Abigail was okay with it, I could take Tara to meet her.*
*While not quite as dramatic as the forest spirit, having a genuine ghost on film would still be a huge win for the show.*
*It's a little funny to me that an actual ghost is mundane compared to what else we've seen.*
*I'm not entirely convinced on the idea. There's still some appeal to keeping the news to myself, for at least a little bit longer.*
*A knock at the front door interrupts my TV-watching.*
Madison: "Coming!"
*I undo the latch and come face to face with Evelyn again.*
Evelyn: "Good afternoon."
Madison: "Hi there."
*My previous encounter with her, on the way back from the church, flashes through my mind and sets me on edge.*
*There's a man I don't recognize with her, too, adding to my unease.*
Evelyn: "We heard there was an issue with the heater, so we've come to fix it. Don't want you freezing to death out here."
*Her demeanor is a 180 from last time. I wonder which of them let her know? Maybe Tara mentioned it earlier today.*
Madison: "Oh, thanks. That'll be a big help."
Evelyn: "Aren't you going to let us in?"
*I realize that I'm blocking the doorway, and step back to make room. It's technically her cabin. I don't really have a right to say no.*
Madison: "Yeah, sorry."
*The guy with her brushes past me and heads to the heater, getting right to work.*
*Meanwhile, Evelyn and I both sit down in the living room. I stare at the floor, hoping that she'll start the conversation. But she seems content to just look around the room, admiring the decorating.*
*When she clears her throat, I flinch.*
Evelyn: "I'm very sorry for the discomfort it must have caused. Even a single night without the warmth can be most unpleasant."
*She stares at me intently, like she's hoping for me to catch some hidden meaning. Whatever it is is lost on me.*
Madison: "It's fine. It was just one night. Thanks for taking care of it so fast."
Evelyn: "Of course."
Evelyn: "Are you the only one home today?"
Madison: "Yeah. Tara's… out."
*I can't imagine that Evelyn doesn't know about her and Morgan, or at least that the two of them have been spending so much time together.*
*She and Morgan live in the same house, after all.*
*But Evelyn's eyebrows raise.*
Evelyn: "Is she, now? My daughter said your friend was here."
Evelyn: "She wouldn't be foolish enough to venture into the woods on her own, would she? The weather's looking quite dire."
*My mouth goes dry.*
*Even Tara wouldn't be that reckless… right?*
Madison: "I… I don't think so."
Evelyn: "Good. Only a fool would go out in this."
*As if on cue, a heavy gust of wind blows, making the whole cabin creak. Through the window, I can see the snowfall has already gotten heavier. Thick, heavy flakes mask the glass and obscure the view outside.*
*The workman finishes up and moves closer to the couch. He still hasn't said a word since he arrived.*
Evelyn: "Seems we're finished here. Do take care, Madison, and don't hesitate to call if you need help with anything else."
Madison: "Will do. Thanks."
*The two of them leave as abruptly as Morgan always does.*
*The moment the door shuts behind them, I leap up and head for the landline phone.*
*I don't know what that was about, at all, but it's obvious there's something more going on than I'm aware of. Something sinister.*
*There's a small scrap of paper jammed underneath the phone, with Morgan's phone number scribbled on it. I dial, and wait, praying that the weather won't render the phones useless.*
*It rings and rings and rings, but doesn't pick up. Eventually, it goes to a generic voicemail message delivered in an automated voice.*
Madison: "Dammit!"
*I slam the phone back to reset it and then dial again.*
Madison: "Please, please, please…"
*If she's not there; if she's not with Morgan, then I don't know what I'll do. I don't know where else she could be.*
*On the third ring, the phone clicks as someone answers.*
Morgan: "-lo?"
*It's garbled and hard to hear her, likely because of the storm.*
Madison: "Morgan?"
*The person on the other line says something unintelligible.*
Madison: "Hello?"
Morgan: "Maddie?"
*It's just barely clear enough for me to hear my name.*
Madison: "Yeah, yeah it's me. Hey, listen, is Tara with you?"
*There's another pause, and some staticky noise. Then a beep as the line goes dead.*
*Frantically, I hit the redial button.*
*This time, she picks up on the first ring.*
Madison: "Hey, it's me. Is Tara with you?"
*There's a noise, but I can't tell if it's her answering me or just interference.*
Madison: "Sorry, what?"
*Again, the only response is garbled nonsense.*
*I try to talk to her several more times, but nothing is intelligible. I don't even know if she can hear me.*
*The call drops again, and this time I don't bother calling back.*
*I could try to send her an email, but it'll take forever before the webpage loads enough for me to do that.*
*I try to make myself calm down. Logically, she's got to be with Morgan. There's nowhere else she'd go.*
*Evelyn must have missed her somehow.*
*I go to one of the windows, using my sleeve to brush away the fog that veils the glass.*
*It's already a blizzard out there, snowing harder than anything I've ever witnessed.*
*That's when I see her.*
*A flash of red hair, over by the treeline.*
*Tara.*
*She's just… standing there?*
*When she notices me, she waves. What the hell is wrong with her?*
*I pound on the glass, pointing downwards to indicate that she should come in.*
Madison: "Come inside, you moron! What are you doing?"
*I know she can't hear me. But she keeps waving.*
*Then, she turns and walks into the forest.*
*What the hell? She's lost it. She's absolutely lost it.*
*I fight against the wind just to open the door.*
Madison: "Tara!"
*My voice is swallowed up by the wind; there's no way she could have heard me. I try yelling again, but the cold stings my throat.*
*Is this some idea of a joke to her? It's a terrible one if so.*
*Stepping out the front door, I can barely see her amongst the trees and swirling snow. She's just standing there waiting for me.*
*I linger, not sure what to do. I can't just leave her there, but going out in this would be asinine.*
*She waves again, more urgently.*
*She clearly wants me to follow her. Maybe she needs my help. Maybe someone's been injured.*
*Still, the storm…*
*Every second I'm out here, the cold seeps a little bit further into me. I need to decide.*
*I clench my fists.*
*If nothing else, I know that Tara would come for me.*
*Bracing myself, I start walking towards her.*
*It's nearly impossible. Just keeping my balance and remaining upright is a chore. Every step I take feels like a battle against nature.*
*Slowly, though, I do it.*
*Once she sees that I'm following her, she starts walking again. I wish she'd wait for me to catch up, so she could at least tell me what's going on.*
*When I cross the treeline, the wind gets noticeably better, although the cold is no less bitter.*
*Tara doesn’t seem phased. She’s still forging ahead, always in the distance no matter how fast I go. Close enough to see, but not close enough to hear.*
*After a few more minutes, I finally start to inch closer to her. I can’t feel my hands or my feet and my teeth won’t stop chattering. I need to stop her now.*
Madison: "Ta… ra…"
*My mouth is shaking too much for me to form any sort of coherent phrases. If I wasn’t already genuinely scared, I am now.*
*Then, without warning, Tara stops. She stands completely still, back towards me. I do my best to push forward, finally making my way to her.*
Madison: "Tara… we have to…"
*I give up on words, instead reaching out towards her. My fingers are numb and heavy.*
*Before I can touch her shoulder, though, she turns around.*
*And my blood runs as icy as my skin.*
*Her face is completely smooth and featureless, like a mannequin. Her clothes, her hair, everything else are a perfect replica. But her face is an empty void.*
*This isn’t Tara. This isn’t even human.*
*The sharp taste of bile rises in my throat as I stumble back, repulsed. As I stare in horror, she begins to flicker like a lightbulb going out.*
Madison: "No. No no no no."
*This isn't real. This can't be real. This isn't happening. This can't be happening.*
*Still flickering, the thing begins to move. Its shoulders start to shake as it rocks back and forth, tilting its head back slightly. It doesn’t make a sound, but its intention is clear.*
*It's laughing at me.*
*I fall backwards, hands scrambling through the snow that I can no longer feel. I scuttle away from it as best I can, though it doesn't even try to follow me.*
*It just rocks, celebrating its triumph over me.*
*My shoulder smashes into something hard, sending a stinging pain through me.*
*It's a tree.*
*I try to stand up, but I've lost all sensation in my legs. My fingers scrabble desperately, uselessly at the hard earth and slick bark, but I can't find any purchase.*
*I try to stand, and my feet slide from beneath me as I crash back to the ground.*
*Each effort is harder than the last.*
*I try several more times to stand, but I can't. Exhausted, I lie there, cheek pressed against the frigid ground. I can't even feel it.*
*My face burns as it's buffeted with more and more snow. My thoughts run ragged.*
*I know how hypothermia works. How fast it can set in. 'Surprisingly quickly,' I'd once read. My body temperature only needs to drop a couple degrees to start shutting down.*
*I can't muster the effort to move.*
*The fake Tara is gone now. The space it occupied is empty. I'm alone.*
*The realization crosses my mind, surprisingly lucid: I'm going to die.*
*This is the end. There is no 'after this.' I've reached the end of my life.*
*Snow accumulates on my body. What parts I can feel burn. I'm grateful for those that I can't.*
*My breath is barely even a wisp of vapor.*
*I try to tell myself that I'm wrong.*
*Someone will find me. I can't die out here. I’m not some mountain adventurer. I'm not the kind of person this happens to. I can’t freeze to death. I can’t.*
*But I'm going to.*
*What about Tara? And BT? And my family? I can’t just leave them all behind because I ran too far into the woods. That’s just not how this works.*
*I try to push myself back up, but I can’t move my arms. I can’t move anything.*
*I'm sure that if my body allowed me, I'd be crying. I don’t want to die.*
*I don’t want to die.*
*I don't want to die.*
*My consciousness fades.*
*A piercing light, that should be unpleasant but isn't, shines in front of me.*
*I will my body to reach for it, but I can't. Then I understand: it isn't meant for my hands to touch.*
*I let go of it all, giving myself up.*
*But then.*
*A sound.*
*Imperceptibly loud. Deafeningly quiet.*
*Something vibrant and full of life fills my tired, tired eyes.*
*It blocks the light.*
*And then…*
*Abigail appears, a look of shock on her face, as she reaches out a hand to Maddie.*
*An angel appears.*
*I’m not cold.*
*I’m not warm either. I’m not… anything.*
*I’m not even sure I’m awake.*
*All I can see is vast, profound, blackness.*
*I realize my eyes are shut.*
*Are they frozen shut? I try to move my arms to rub my face, but those feel frozen too.*
*With great effort, I manage to wrench my eyes open.*
*I’m flat on my back. Right above me is a familiar curved wood ceiling. The grey smudges in my peripheral vision are the pews where I sat and read with Abigail just days ago.*
*I'm in the church.*
*I try to remember how I got here. But all I have are glimpses of what happened.*
*-waking up alone.*
*-the mayor dropping by unannounced.*
*-following Tara.*
*Not Tara.*
*Even nearly immobile, a shiver creeps up my spine at the thought of that… thing.*
*And after that…*
*It comes back to me as an avalanche. Running. Stumbling, and falling.*
*And being so, so cold.*
*I can recall the dread as it happened. As I gave up. When strength left my body and my eyelids couldn't bear being open any longer.*
*But how did I get from there to here?*
*There’s the sound of rustling cloth from beside me. I force my head to turn to look.*
*And there she is. Warm. Radiant. Just an arm’s length away.*
*Abigail.*
*I blink, expecting her to vanish. To be a figment of my imagination.*
*But when my aching eyes open once more, she's still there. Worry wrinkles her face.*
*She saved me, didn’t she? There’s no other explanation.*
*I muster the strength to smile up at her. She smiles back, though it doesn't reach her eyes.*
Abigail: "Hello, Madison. I’m glad you’re awake."
*In the otherwise silent church, her voice rings out like a bell. The first words I've ever heard her say.*
*I can hear her. We can speak to each other.*
*Understanding pierces through my exhaustion. With that understanding comes a whole cascade of realizations.*
*A million feelings rush through me, and I don’t know what order to process them. Two concepts stand out among everything else:*
*Her voice is lovely; soft and gentle like she is. I must be dead.*
Madison: "Abigail."
*I mumble out the only coherent thought that I have.*
*She slides from the bench she was sitting in to the ground, kneeling on the floor beside me.*
Abigail: "That’s right. I’m here."
*She leans over me, the serene portrait of a heaven-sent angel.*
*She's here.*
*That can only mean one thing.*
*My head doesn't hurt. My back isn't sore despite spending lying on the cold stone floor for hours. I can hear and speak with Abigail.*
Madison: "I died… didn’t I? And you saved me and brought me back here?"
*She rubs her face, a pained look on it that she can't brush away.*
Abigail: "You’re not quite dead. But you're not alive either. Rather, you’re somewhere in between life and death."
Abigail: "Like me."
Abigail: "That’s the best way I can put it."
*I can tell that her tone is supposed to be optimistic. But to me, she sounds entirely too cheerful for someone narrating my own demise.*
*Still, I suppose this is better than the alternative.*
Madison: "Then… thank you."
*Her smile gets just a little bit smaller.*
Abigail: "Please don’t mention it."
Abigail: "Really, I’m sorry that we hadn’t been just a little bit faster. If we had arrived sooner…"
*She looks away, guilt twisting her face.*
Madison: "It’s okay. It’s not your fault."
*Even in my addled state, I know that much.*
*I shut my eyes, hoping that this might be a dream. And that when I open them, I'll be safe and sound in my bed at the cabin.*
*I'm not, of course. There's nothing above me but stone.*
*I force myself to sit up. The motion catches her attention and Abigail lunges forward, ready to catch me if I fall.*
Abigail: "Oh! Don’t strain yourself!"
*But there's still no pain or soreness. Instead, it's like each individual part was switched off, and I have to reactivate them.*
*Maybe that’s not far from the truth. Once I’ve sat up, I’m able to stand without any issue. The heaviness falls away like a blanket.*
Madison: "I think I’m okay."
*To demonstrate, I pace back and forth a couple steps, feeling like I'm just stretching my legs. I reach out and flex my fingers, staring at their strange, glimmering transparencies.*
*The whole time, she watches intently, poised to intervene if anything goes wrong.*
*Nothing does. I'm fine, relatively speaking.*
*I look towards Abigail for approval with the most earnest smile I can muster. She visibly relaxes.*
*I then sit down on one of the pews. Abigail sits down beside me.*
*It’s bizarre. Despite years of existential dread, I don’t know how to feel about my own, past-tense death.*
*Well, Abigail did say that it’s not quite death. It’s just not exactly life, either.*
*Obviously it’s got to be a bit more complicated than that. But right now it’s helping to soften the blow a little bit.*
*I look down at my hands. They’re normal and clean. Not bloating and turning purple like in the last few moments.*
*The rest of me seems similarly plain. Apparently I was well-preserved.*
*There’s one difference I wonder about, though.*
*I press my finger to the stone that we’re sitting on.*
*Or at least I try to.*
*But my hand just passes through. It doesn’t feel like anything.*
*Right.*
*Next to me, Abigail does the same thing, pressing her hand in up to her wrist. She giggles, and my heart skips a beat.*
Abigail: "I’d forgotten how it feels at first. It’s kind of wondrous."
Madison: "Yeah, that’s a good way to put it."
Abigail: "Once you get used to it, it’s really quite convenient."
Abigail: "It’ll just take a bit of time!"
*Abigail stands and gives me a huge smile, probably not realizing just how scary that thought is to me.*
*I don’t really want to spend however long it takes to ‘get used to it’ like this.*
*But she’s clearly excited to talk. I am too, regardless of the circumstances.*
*So I won’t spoil the moment. Yet.*
Madison: "How long has it been since then? Since I… you know."
*Even thinking of the word 'died' causes my throat to constrict with terror.*
*It’s not death, I remind myself. If I can feel afraid, then it can’t be death.*
Abigail: "It’s been over a day. You’ve been unconscious this entire time."
Abigail: "I was afraid that perhaps I'd done it wrong. That you might not ever wake up."
Madison: "But here I am."
*I can see her eyes sparkle with tears. Despite the fact that I’m standing right here, even the thought of my demise almost makes her cry.*
Abigail: "Yes. Here you are."
Madison: "How did you do it? I remember thinking that I was going to die, for sure…"
*She looks away from me, fidgeting with her dress.*
Abigail: "As I said, we arrived too late to… to save your life. The cold had already set in, and you were so far from home…"
Abigail: "But we were able to save your spirit. Your soul. The important part of you. And that's what you are now."
Madison: "A spirit?"
*She nods.*
Madison: "That's what you are too, right?"
*Another nod.*
Abigail: "Exactly!"
*Her voice is gleeful. I can tell she's excited by this. I can't blame her. After 200 years of solitude, I'm sure that I would be too.*
*But for me, well…*
*This is a nightmare, wrapped in irony.*
*I came to Eysenfeld as a skeptic. And now, I'm… this. It's so overwhelming.*
*What will my parents say? Or my friends back home? And what about everything else I left behind? I never prepared a will or anything like that.*
*I don't know what to think. What to feel. I suppose I should be grateful that I'm not truly dead but, at the same time, I don't know what I'm missing out on. Maybe it would've been preferable.*
*I wish I could've at least said goodbye. Especially to Tara. There's so much left unsaid…*
*Wait. Tara.*
*With a jolt, I turn to face Abigail. She flinches at how suddenly I move.*
Madison: "Do you know if Tara's safe? My friend, the one who came here with me. I thought I was following her into the woods, but it was-"
*The image of that fake Tara comes to mind, and with it a wave of dread. The memory of it is repulsive simply in how false it was. How unnatural.*
Abigail: "She's fine. As far as I know, she was never in any danger at all."
Abigail: "I'm so sorry, Madison. You were tricked."
Madison: "Right."
*I relax a little bit.*
*Tricked.*
Madison: "Tricked by… by whom?"
*Abigail looks uncomfortable once more.*
Abigail: "I'm not sure what name she uses now. The mayor of the village. Whatever you know her as."
Madison: "Evelyn."
*So she's responsible. I'm not surprised in the least. The way she always seemed like a snake, coiled and ready to pounce. The bad feeling I got every time I interacted with her.*
*She must have come by the cabin just to scope me out. She had probably been planning this all along.*
*I shudder.*
Madison: "Who is she, really? Why would she want to kill me?"
Abigail: "I'll explain it later. But I promise, you don't have to worry about her anymore!"
*Clearly that's supposed to make me feel better. But it doesn't. Yeah, she's not a threat anymore. But only because she succeeded.*
*I want to understand. I need to understand. Why would she want me dead? I guess I'll find out in time.*
Madison: "I just have one other question. Earlier, when you were talking about saving me, you kept saying 'we.' Who else?"
*Now her eyes regain that previous, mischievous gleam that I've started to recognize on her.*
Abigail: "Someone special. I'll take you to meet them, if you feel you're ready for it."
Madison: "I think so."
*There's nothing to be accomplished by sitting around feeling sorry for myself right now. I've spent enough of my time in Eysenfeld doing that as it is.*
*Plus, I'm curious to see who might have helped Abigail. Who else could've known about her this whole time, yet kept her a secret?*
*I stand up, and absentmindedly offer a hand to Abigail to help her up, too.*
*She takes it, *
*She takes it, then drops it immediately.*
*Eyes wide and awestruck, she stares as if I'd sprouted a tentacle from my arm.*
*Tears spring to her eyes, seeping out to form a thin river down her face.*
Madison: "What is it? What’s wrong? Did I hurt you?"
Abigail: "Your hand… I can feel it."
*She’s right. It hadn’t even registered as different, to me. But for the first time since we've met, I can actually touch and feel her there.*
*She grabs my hand again, not standing. She closes her eyes as her fingers roam over mine, taking in the sensation.*
*With anyone else, anyone on earth, it'd be awkward. But somehow, with Abigail… I don't mind. It's a little endearing, in fact.*
*After a moment, she seems to realize what she's doing. With a yelp and a sheepish expression, she lets go.*
Abigail: "I'm sorry, Madison! I just… I just got so carried away for a moment…"
Madison: "It's fine. Really."
*My skin still tingles with the feeling, vivid because of the absence of anything else.*
