Morgan: "Maybe you'll learn enough while you're here to make a few cooking videos yourself."
Tara: "Are you asking because you actually think that'd be good, or do you just want to see another video where I almost burn my house down?"
*Morgan shrugs and gives Tara a small smirk. When Tara chuckles in response, I feel another surge of sadness.*
*I knew I shouldn't have expected this to work right away, but… I'm still crestfallen.*
*Having them this close hurts in a way I couldn't have imagined. All the time I spent feeling ignored while I was alive seems so trivial compared to this.*
Tara: "So, what's the plan for today? Got anywhere in town we might be able to hit up for local lore?"
Morgan: "Most of the official records of town history are sealed up in Evelyn's office, so those are off the table."
Morgan: "But we might have some luck checking out older buildings around town. She's done a good job of cleaning up evidence of what Eysenfeld used to be, but maybe you'll notice something I didn't."
Tara: "Sure. I mean, it's worth a shot."
*Her voice barely contains a trace of the fire I'm so used to. I have to hope that at least the remaining trace is a sign she'll regain the fire someday.*
*At least she's not alone. Morgan reaches out and puts her hand on Tara's shoulder, squeezing gently. When she speaks, her words are soft and strained.*
Morgan: "Are you going to be okay?"
Tara: "What do you mean?"
Morgan: "After yesterday. If you need some time to rest, nobody's going to blame you."
*I still don't feel any kind of power within me that I could use to manifest myself. Abigail's my only hope. I turn to her, trying to keep my voice level despite my desperation.*
Madison: "Abigail, do you think you could manifest? Even if I can't reach them, maybe you can."
*Abigail nods, but she's frowning. My heart wasn't exactly soaring, but I can feel it sink even further.*
Abigail: "I'll try, but… whatever power I had that allowed me to appear to you has felt much weaker since I used it to save you."
*Abigail closes her eyes and starts to lift higher off the ground. As I watch, I feel a strange feeling in the air around me.*
*As if it's bending around her, being pulled aside so she can step through the border between our worlds. For a minute, it seems like it's actually working.*
*I'm trying to figure out what exactly I'm going to say when I feel the energy in the air start to fade. Abigail seems to be struggling, furrowing her brow as she sways in the air.*
*Her face scrunches, and guilt stabs me like a knife. Is she in pain?*
Madison: "Abigail, stop. It's okay."
Abigail: "Maybe if i just… try a little harder…"
*I reach out and gently take her hand, lowering her to the ground. She opens her eyes, breathing hard. As much as I want to see Tara, it's not worth making her feel like this.*
Madison: "It's okay. I promise. We can try again some other time."
*Abigail gives a faint nod and stares down at her feet. Her hands ball into fists as her shoulders rise and fall.*
Abigail: "I'm so sorry, Madison. I don’t understand why this is happening."
Madison: "There aren't any rulebooks for this sort of thing. I don't blame you for not knowing about things most people think are impossible to begin with."
*The disappointment I'm feeling takes a backseat to concern for Abigail's well-being. I've already put her through a lot by making her come here in the first place.*
Madison: "We knew beforehand that this was a long shot. I'm just happy I got to see them again."
Abigail: "Are you sure?"
*I'm not, but I know that if I tell her that she'll want to try again. It's better this way.*
Madison: "I'm sure. You've done more than enough by coming with me."
*I look back to Tara and Morgan, taking what I pray isn't my final glance at them.*
*To my surprise, Tara is leaning against Morgan's shoulder while Morgan's arm is wrapped around her back in a loose hug.*
Tara: "Thank you. As messed up as all this is, I'm glad that you're the one helping me get through it."
*Tara's voice is soft, and it carries a vulnerability I don't think I've ever heard from her before.*
Morgan: "Same here. We make a good team."
*Tara raises her head to look at Morgan, their faces inches apart.*
Tara: "We should have a team name. Like… "The Dream Team" or something."
Morgan: "We'll work on it."
*I already had a feeling that the two of them had something going on before I… *
*Before all this happened.*
*I'm not that surprised, if I'm being honest. Stuff like this has happened before with her. But it's always a one night stand, or some kind of quick fling. Never the kind of thing that would merit real vulnerability.*
*Then again, she's never been in a situation like this before. Maybe losing me brought out a part of her I never knew she had.*
*As if in response to my thoughts, Morgan leads forward and kisses Tara. It's a lot more than a simple peck on the lips. This is something deeper. So deep in fact, that I have to turn away. It seems wrong to spy on something that's intimate.*
*Next to me, Abigail has also turned away. Her face is a bright crimson, and her hands are close to her chest.*
*I didn't stop to consider that this is the first act of intimacy she's seen in two centuries. For all I know, this could be the first time she's ever seen two women kiss.*
Madison: "You okay?"
*She looks up at me, slightly alarmed.*
Abigail: "Ah… yes. I'm okay. I just wasn't prepared for something like that."
Madison: "Trust me, I wasn't either."
*Abigail pauses for a moment, closing her eyes and taking a calming breath.*
*A small smile crosses Abby's face as she blushes, her wide eyes softening.*
Abigail: "… It is nice though, isn't it? A love like that can give someone incredible strength."
Madison: "Yeah. I'm happy for them."
*I really am. Maybe this will help her cope better now that I’m… gone.*
Madison: "But we should probably go. I feel really weird being here for this."
Abigail: "Of course. Maybe we can try this again another time."
*Her words lack conviction, but she doesn't protest. I'll have to think of some ways to cheer her up once we get back to the church.*
Tara: "Hey, do you want to maybe-"
*The front door creaks open. All four of us freeze as we face the entrance.*
*Evelyn stalks in and slams the door behind her.*
*She doesn't say anything, but neither do Tara and Morgan. Tara's shaking, and Morgan places a protective arm in front of her as she glares at her mother.*
*I shiver too, desperately wanting to look away but feeling unable to. A part of me screams to do something, anything, to get her away from Morgan and Tara.*
*Yet. There's a stronger part of me screaming, "I need to leave. I need to get out of here." But I feel just as frozen as the day I died.*
*I feel Abigail on my arm, pulling me backwards. She pulls my body through the connecting wall, and only when Evelyn is out my vision can I breathe again.*
*Abigail presses against me, just as stunned as I am. I try to keep calm, telling myself that she doesn't even know we're here. That she can't hurt me anymore.*
*Evelyn passes through the door and by the corner where Abigail and I are lurking.*
*And she stops.*
*Slowly, her head turns to look where we are. Where there's otherwise just empty space.*
*She's so close that I can see her face contort in recognition and shock.*
Abigail: "Madison!"
*I jerk my head towards Abigail to see that she's already started to head for the wall, waving for me to follow her.*
*It's enough to snap me out of my trance. I run to her, casting a backwards glance at Evelyn to make sure she isn't following us.*
*She doesn't move, but her eyes track us as we run. Right before I pass through the wall, her mouth opens.*
Evelyn: "How-"
*I don't stick around to find out what she says next.*
*Abigail and I run as fast as we can, phasing through buildings and people with reckless abandon.*
*After what we just saw, all other fears feel inconsequential.*
*By the time we're back in the forest, it's clear that Evelyn has no interest in pursuing us. For now, at least.*
Abigail: "Madison, are you okay?"
*She puts her hand on my shoulder, pulling me closer to her. My head throbs painfully, and my breathing is shaky, but her touch grounds me.*
Madison: "I'm… I'm not sure. But I'm a lot better now that we have some distance between us and her."
*I look backwards towards the village, half-expecting her to come flying at us like some kind of demon.*
Madison: "She saw us, Abigail. How is that possible?"
*Abigail looks down at the ground, and I realize she's shaking as hard as I am.*
Abigail: "I wish I knew."
Abigail: "Madison, she said something to you before we left, didn't she?"
Madison: "Yeah. I didn't catch all of it. But I think I know what she was going to say."
Madison: "She looked shocked when she saw us. Confused, even. I think… I think she didn't know about us being out here."
*Abigail's voice is barely above a whisper when she speaks. Her face is drained of color.*
Abigail: "But now she does."
*The message is clear. As long as Evelyn is in Eysenfeld, we can't be.*
*I'm sorry, Tara. I'm sorry, Morgan.*
*But it looks like you're on your own.*
*The midday sun sits high in the sky, setting everything aglitter. The vibrant music of the forest - the pitter-patter of dripping snow, the chitter-chatter of birds, and the accompanying breeze - is especially loud today.*
*The events of yesterday are a forgotten nightmare, and so today… Today must be a dream come true.*
*Madison stands with her back to me and to the church, facing the forest. Her head is tilted back ever so slightly. For the first time, she looks truly at peace.*
*For the briefest moment, she’s framed by the sunlight. Then, perhaps sensing me watching, she turns around and smiles at me, and I can’t help but smile back.*
*Without a doubt, the past several days have been the happiest of my extraordinarily long existence.*
Abigail: "Good morning. Did you sleep well?"
Madison: "Yeah, I did. It’s getting a bit easier."
Abigail: "That’s wonderful."
*I know that that isn’t true. That she tossed and turned and sighed until early in the morning.*
*I know, but I understand.*
*Whatever it takes for her to feel more comfortable, more at home. I wish that I knew how to help. For me, sleep was the last thing on my mind after I passed.*
*I explored endlessly, backtracking over and over again until I knew every step of these woods.*
*I made the most of my situation as best as I could. And rather well, if I do say so myself.*
Abigail: "What would you like to do today?"
Madison: "Let’s go back to that forest clearing. The one where the fairies live. I’d like to see it again."
*My soul sings.*
*That out of everywhere we’ve been, that would be the place she wants to return.*
*I rush to her side, drawn to her presence like a magnet. Simply being near to her makes me happy. A part of my mind screams at me that I don’t deserve it.*
*The same way that she doesn’t deserve to be so miserable.*
*Still, I put on my brightest smile as I touch her arm.*
Abigail: "That sounds perfect. I’d like nothing better."
Abigail: "Shall we go now?"
Madison: "Yeah, sure."
*As is tradition, I lead the way out of the church and towards the forest. The trees and branches part invitingly, familiar and old.*
*I ask the forest to take us to the clearing, and I can feel it ripple in response. Not physically, but in my heart. I know that the forest is listening.*
*It’s difficult to explain. So much of this existence has become habit and routine for me, that I keep taking for granted the difficulties Madison must be going through.*
*The emotional turmoil, coupled with the way her own understanding of the universe itself keeps changing. I feel another pain in my heart at that. The stab of guilt once more, for enjoying her presence.*
*Madison accompanies me unhesitatingly, her steps more confident than they used to be. We walk side-by-side. I relish the closeness. I wonder if it even crosses her mind.*
*When we reach the clearing, Madison sighs contentedly, relaxed and easy. A tranquil expression settles on her face. Like a masterpiece of art, I’d spend the whole day appreciating that look if I could.*
*The grove’s serenity is contagious - I also feel a little more at peace here.*
*We head for the shore of the pond, as still and glassy as ever.*
Madison: "I really love it here. It’s so calm."
*I nod. Of course we’ve discussed it before, but I don’t mind. I could listen to her talk about anything, for any amount of time.*
Madison: "It actually reminds me of somewhere back at home. There’s a little lake near where Tara and I live. Sometimes I like to go and sit down there to think."
Madison: "There are a ton of geese and ducks around, too. Sometimes they won’t leave you alone, so you have to just ignore them."
*I envision a flock of birds surrounding Madison, all quacking and vying for her attention.*
Abigail: "Maybe they want you to sing to them, too."
*Madison laughs, sweet and subtle. It’s the most beautiful sound in the world.*
Madison: "Nah, they just want food. It’s really popular to go down and feed them. Moms do it with their kids a lot. But then the birds get so used to it that they think every human has frozen peas for them."
Abigail: "They must be disappointed quite often, then."
Madison: "Yeah."
*She trails off, looking wistful. I’m sure that she’s thinking of home.*
*I try not to ask her much about her life before I met her, because I know that the memories hurt.*
*To my surprise, though, Madison clears her throat and continues talking.*
Madison: "I wish that you could see it someday. My home, I mean. In America. I think you’d like it there."
Abigail: "I’m sure that I would. I'd love to see any place that you care so much about. All of the buildings, and the lights."
*I try to envision what it might be like, but it’s futile. There’s so much that I don’t know; so many things that I haven’t seen.*
*Trying to imagine it is like trying to imagine a color that I’ve never seen before. No matter what she says, it’s impossible to visualize.*
*Even the memories I have of the few times I left Eysenfeld have long since faded. To me, the only world that exists now is the forest.*
Madison: "I keep thinking… In your time, what would I have been like? Would I be the same person if I never fell in love with films?"
Abigail: "Films… ?"
Madison: "Oh, uh… It's, it's like a play that you can watch anytime! They're really compelling."
Abigail: "I see."
*Madison moves her arms a little, looking like she wants to say more but eventually just shakes her head.*
*She smiles a bit sadly.*
Madison: "There are so many things like that - films, or events in history - that are so normal to me but that I keep forgetting you don’t know about."
Madison: "So if I ever say something that makes no sense at all, I can try to explain."
Abigail: "There are a lot of things I’ve wondered about, it’s true. For instance, sometimes I’ll see great, noisy machines up in the sky. Do you know what I’m talking about?"
*Madison giggles again, but I don’t know why.*
Madison: "Yeah. Those are called airplanes. They’re kind of like giant carriages, that carry people around. That’s how we got from America to here."
Abigail: "What? Don’t tease me like that. There’s no way something like that could fly!"
Madison: "No, it’s true. It’s really true. They’re the fastest way to get around the world. There are also these things called cars, which are much smaller and only fit a few people."
Madison: "But most people own their own car, and use it to get around."
Abigail: "Do you have one?"
Madison: "I do."
Abigail: "Why not use that to cross the ocean?"
*She laughs again, and I don’t know why. At first I feel embarrassed, the gulf of knowledge that I lack nearly as wide as the ocean that she claims to have crossed.*
*But then she steps closer, placing her arms around me in a light hug.*
*Again, I don’t know why.*
*But it’s lovely. I feel myself relaxing, leaning against her arms and just enjoying the closeness. The comfort. The affection.*
*Far too soon, Madison relaxes and I regretfully pull away from her. She gazes up at the sky, and for a brief moment, I anticipate another lesson about the modern world.*
Madison: "I've been thinking a lot about what you said the other day. About how you've never wanted to leave this place."
Abigail: "Yes. I'm far too cowardly for that."
*And in many other ways, too.*
Madison: "I don't think that's cowardly at all."
Madison: "It’s difficult to leave behind what you know and what feels safe. In fact, it’s really scary."
Madison: "I know that the two don’t really compare, but before I decided to quit Tara’s show, I felt the same way. Like I was afraid to leave, just because I was so used to it being part of my life."
Madison: "But I decided that if I wasn’t happy with how things were, that I should just take the risk and change something."
Madison: "Everyone has those worries, though. That doesn’t make you a coward."
*Madison half-smiles, the side of her mouth curling up just a little bit.*
Madison: "If we ever do find a way out of here, maybe you could come back to America with me."
*She’s joking. I’m sure that she doesn’t mean it, or at least not fully. And yet my heart flutters like a bird taking flight.*
*Even after everything that’s happened, that she’d even have concern for someone like me…*
*She truly is the most extraordinary person I’ve met.*
*And as happy as her offer makes me, it hurts like a knife in my heart because I know that it could never be.*
*I wouldn’t deserve that kind of happiness, anyway.*
*Still, I smile back in kind.*
Abigail: "That might be nice. Would I have to ride on an airplane?"
Madison: "Oh, definitely."
Madison: "People don’t really take boats across the ocean for travel anymore. Not nearly as much. Pretty much everyone flies."
Abigail: "Isn’t that scary? How does something so heavy manage to stay up in the air?"
Madison: "It’s kind of scary the first few times, but you get used to it. As for how it’s possible, well… There’s all sorts of science behind it, but that would take too long to explain."
Madison: "Anyway though, you could stay with me and Tara. We’ve got plenty of space. Or we could help you find your own apartment."
Madison: "There’s a lot you’d have to learn about the modern world, of course, but it’d be worth it, and we could help you learn."
*Madison continues on, explaining this and that procedure we’d have to go through and how we’d overcome each obstacle.*
*I play my part, smiling and nodding along. It’s an easy role - it does sound wonderful. In fact, there’s nothing I’d like more.*
*And though at first I thought she was joking, she’s being completely serious.*
*That makes it hurt even more.*
*Because I know it's impossible.*
*I close my eyes to help hide the tears that have sprung there, unbidden. Fortunately, Madison doesn’t even seem to notice.*
*Her voice trails off, and when I open my eyes again I see that she’s looking past me, her eyes wide with awe.*
*I spin around.*
*Fluttering about, far too early in the day…*
Madison: "Are those the fairies you were talking about?"
Abigail: "Yes."
*A handful of them flit amongst the trees and lights, paying us no mind. They move like dancers, their motions fluid and in sync with each other.*
*As one rises up to adjust a hanging light, another fairy weaves between branches, carrying something in its hands that I can’t see.*
*Though I only spy a few of them right now, I’m sure that many more are just out of sight, deeper in the grove or otherwise nearby.*
*Madison watches them with unabashed awe.*
Madison: "What are they doing?"
*I shrug.*
Abigail: "I'm not sure. I've never seen them out during the day like this. Usually they stay hidden in their hive until the moon is out."
Abigail: "They aren’t like humans, they’re something entirely different. Even though I’ve spoken with them before, I don’t really understand them."
Madison: "Wait, they can talk? Like, they can speak human language?"
Abigail: "Yes. They aren’t the most eloquent, but yes."
Madison: "I see."
*The only time I’ve talked with them was the first time I came to this grove, all on my own. At first, they had tried to drive me out, but eventually realized that I meant them no harm.*
*Since then, we haven’t interacted.*
Madison: "They’re part of the forest too, right? In the connected way that you were talking about before."
Abigail: "Yes."
*She makes a sound in acknowledgment, but doesn’t say anything more. Whatever implications or meaning that might have to her goes unspoken.*
*Soon, the fairies disappear once more into the trees, whatever business that called them here finished. They go silently, not even the hum of their tiny wings reaching us.*
*After only seconds, we’re left all alone again.*
Madison: "They weren’t anything like I expected."
Abigail: "Really? How so?"
Madison: "In stories I’ve heard, fairies are always smaller and look like little people with wings. Not like what we just saw."
Madison: "And they usually live in flowers and fields, not the snow."
Madison: "But those are just stories, of course. I keep having to remind myself that this is real life. And that real life doesn’t care what happens in stories."
*Madison looks down at the pond. Gently, she places the tip of her foot into the water. Of course, the water remains still, like she isn’t even there.*
*She takes a tentative step forward, still standing upon the water. It supports her effortlessly.*
*Her hands are slightly outstretched, as if she might lose her balance or footing and fall in.*
*She turns around to face me.*
Madison: "I had an idea, actually."
Madison: "About getting out of here… Do you think that the forest spirit could help us? It's powerful, right? Maybe it could use its magic to make us humans again."
Madison: "We could even talk to it together. You’d seriously be welcome to go back with me and Tara. I’d like that a lot, actually."
*I can’t look at her, can’t meet her eyes. All I can do is nod, trying to smile, and hoping that she doesn’t see my tears.*
Abigail: "I’d like that too."
Abigail: "Perhaps it could help. There's certainly a chance."
*There isn't.*
*Her face lights up. Even the prospect of leaving makes her happy.*
Madison: "Great! Maybe we can plan together what the best way to ask it would be?"
*I just nod again. It’s all I can do.*
Abigail: "Sure."
*I can't bring myself to tell her the truth. That no matter what she asks; what she says, there's no way the spirit can grant her wish.*
*It's not just something that can be reversed. Her mortal form was destroyed by Evelyn, just shortly after I saved her spirit.*
*As far as the rest of the world is concerned, Madison Raines no longer exists. Neither the forest spirit nor I have the power to remake her.*
*There's no escape.*
*Another morning. Nothing’s changed.*
*No progress. No hope.*
*The only difference this morning is that Tara’s still asleep. It’s the first time I’ve woken up before her since we started sleeping together.*
*I’m glad she’s still sleeping. I can tell she needs it.*
*I slip out of bed and dress silently. My next move should be heading to the shop. Sticking to routine is what's kept Evelyn's off our backs.*
*For now.*
*The instant I start thinking about Evelyn again, I'm hit by a powerful urge to climb back into bed and wrap my arms around Tara. Hold her for a little longer.*
*I don’t. She needs her rest, and there’s still some time before she leaves.*
*Somehow, thinking about that makes me feel even worse. I try to push the thoughts away, but her voice does that for me.*
Tara: "Oh shit, what time is it?"
*She’s sitting up in bed and rubbing at her eyes. She’s still wearing her clothes from the night before. Or at least the top half.*
*But her sitting up doesn’t mean she’s actually awake.*
Morgan: "It’s still early. You can rest more."
Tara: "No, no, it’s fine… I got this…"
Morgan: "I’m just going to go spend some time in the shop so nobody comes to check in on us later today. Get some sleep. I’ll be back soon."
Tara: "You sure?"
*I move back to the bed and kiss her forehead before lowering her onto her pillow.*
Morgan: "I’m sure. I’ll wake you up when I’m back."
Tara: "Okay, cool. Thanks, babe."
Tara: "I mean, uh… thanks."
*Dead silence.*
Morgan: "I know what you meant."
*I’m out the door before either of us can say a word.*
*I don't want to think about that right now. I just need to get to the shop.*
*I’m so focused on that I almost slam into Evelyn the second I step outside.*
Evelyn: "No need to rush, dear."
*My brain jumps to panic mode. Why is she here? She's never here in the morning. Did she come for us?*
*The door isn’t shut all the way. I could run back inside. Grab Tara, get out.*
*I know deep down that it’s pointless to try. If she wanted us dead, we'd be dead.*
*I don't know why she's sparing us. But if she changes her mind, we won’t get a warning.*
Morgan: "What do you want?"
*Her mouth curls into a frown.*
Evelyn: "Am I not supposed to take an interest in my daughter's life?"
Evelyn: "I thought you had outgrown that phase."
*I grit my teeth. The sight of Tara's body being flung into the wall. Maddie's room in the cabin, hauntingly empty.*
*She doesn't have the right to even pretend to be my mother.*
Morgan: "I'm not your daughter."
Evelyn: "Morgan!"
*I flinch as she raises her voice. I hate letting her get a rise out of me. But there’s too many memories making me afraid of that sound.*
*She just sighs and her features sink. She almost looks visibly hurt.*
*Only almost. There's still something inhuman about her expression. Something I've never been able to understand.*
Evelyn: "You can be so cruel sometimes."
Evelyn: "You weren't always. When you were young you were the sweetest thing. You would never imagine the things you say to me so casually now."
*I clench my fists. Have to keep it together.*
*My nails push slightly against the skin of my palm. As always, my fingers freeze before they draw blood.*
Morgan: "I’m going to work."
*My voice comes out as a rasp, and I cringe at the weakness of it.*
*She puts her hand on my shoulder as I try to walk past.*
Evelyn: "Work can wait, dear. I’m sure your customers will understand. I have something to show you."
Evelyn: "I thought since you were so desperate to see in my office, I could give you a little glimpse of what I keep in there."
*Evelyn drops her hand, her lips twisting into a sickeningly sweet smile.*
Evelyn: "If you're not interested, however, I'm sure your American friend would be eager to join me."
*My blood freezes.*
*I want to hit her. I want to scream at her.*
*I'm not sure if it's fear or the curse she's placed on me that keeps me rooted to the spot.*
*I can't even look at up her. She knows she's won, as she always has. I can feel her eyes on me as she waits for me to follow her.*
*The moment passes, and she makes a sound of affirmation. She turns away.*
Evelyn: "Now, come with me dear."
*I figure out where we’re heading after a couple minutes of walking. The forest.*
*If she’s taking me in there, then…*
*This could be it.*
Evelyn: "Ah, here we are."
*I look back to the house for what I hope isn’t the last time. What’s going to happen if Tara wakes up and can’t find me? What if she never sees me again?*
Morgan: "Tara… "
*I mutter her name under my breath. Some dumb childish part of me hopes that she heard it somehow.*
*It’s better that she’s not here. But I still wish she was.*
Evelyn: "What was that, dear?"
Morgan: "Nothing."
Evelyn: "I ought to thank your friend. She's made motivating you so much easier."
Evelyn: "It's nice to see you caring about someone other than yourself for once. I'd almost think you were finally growing up, if you weren't so stubbornly holding on to your teenage rebelliousness."
*I don’t say anything. I keep my face blank. One, two, breathing. She pays me no mind.*
*We walk into the forest. Past the initial treeline, into its depths. At least I know where we are.*
*Or I thought I did. As we keep on walking I realize that I don't know where we are at all.*
*I don't recognize any of this. The trees are different. These aren’t the ones I know. They aren’t even the ones I saw when we started walking.*
*I’ve walked this path dozens of times. There’s no way I’m making a mistake. The branches blot out the sun even more than usual, and the shadows seem to blend around Evelyn.*
*I can hear the creaking of old wood in the darkness. It almost sounds like the trees are moving.*
*I've never seen the forest behave like this. I've never seen anything behave like this. As we walk, Evelyn keeps turning her head to glance at me. Probably trying to see how scared I am. I don't let my expression waver. I won’t let her in. She won’t win this one.*
*The things around me don’t even look like trees anymore. They’re too gnarled and rotten. They twist into shapes that should be impossible.*
*They form walls around us as we walk, pushing us forward. I can’t help drawing in on myself. It's like the whole forest is closing around us.*
*It’s getting harder to breathe. I feel like I’m about to be crushed by the forest itself.*
*And just when I think I can't take it anymore, we break into a clearing.*
*And in the center of the clearing is a beautiful radiant tree.*
*It’s actually green. Real, brilliant green needles. The trunk is so thick and sturdy that the bark makes it look armor-plated.*
*The rich scent of pine fills the air. The the shadows around us are kept at bay by a shimmering golden light.*
*I can breathe again. Crisp, clear air fills my lungs. The awful sensations of the twisted forest are all but forgotten.*
*I reach out my hand to it. But only reach. Something inside me tells me that I'm not supposed to touch it. That I'm not even supposed to be here.*
Morgan: "What is this?"
*She ignores me. Or at least I think she does. I don't turn around to look at her, I'm too transfixed by the tree ahead of me.*
*I don't know how long I stand there staring, but after some time I notice the air around me change. My limbs begin to numb almost immediately.*
*When I look back at Evelyn, she's… glowing. An oily shimmer all over her body.*
*And she's smiling at me. Smiling.*
Evelyn: "You were so peaceful. So calm. I can't remember another time when you were so relaxed."
Evelyn: "I hate to interrupt it. Really I do. But I only have so much time to spend here."
Evelyn: "Do understand."
*She starts walking towards me.*
*That's when I see the knife in her hand.*
*I try to open my mouth to speak. I try to move my feet away from her.*
*I can't.*
*Completely still. Impulse crashes against nature. My heart hammers in my chest.*
*Every muscle in my body is frozen. I can't even shake.*
Evelyn: "This forest spirit… it's so young. As small as it is, the power within it is enough to melt the ice in this place. To draw you in."
Evelyn: "You love it so much, even though you'd only just met it moments ago."
*A rush of understanding fills me. The warmth, the peace in this place.*
*A haven from the cold, and we are the intruders.*
Evelyn: "I always knew you were part of this forest. Much more than any of the others. This confirms that."
*She grabs my hand and holds it up, examining my palm, running her fingers over it's creases, as if it's like nothing she's ever seen before.*
Evelyn: "I thought it would make you dangerous. It didn't."
Evelyn: "It made you weaker."
*She presses the blade to my hand. My mind screams at my body, desperately trying to get my muscles to respond.*
Evelyn: "Your one chance at success. Of escape. And you're weakened by it."
*She laughs to herself, like she knows a joke that I'm not in on.*
*With a swift motion, she drags the knife against my palm. I stare in horror as blood trickles and pools from the open gash.*
Evelyn: "Since you loved it so much, I wonder how it will feel for you to be the one to kill it."
*My strings are cut in that moment, and I fall over. The rush of sensations rush over me all at once. The pain. The cold.*
Morgan: "AAAAHH!"
*I scream out, clutching my palm. I turn and try to crawl immediately, my instincts shouting at me as I fall to my knees over and over. Get up. Run. Run.*
*But it's too late. Evelyn grabs my wrist so hard I feel like the bones are about to break, and she swings me around and presses my bloody palm into the forest spirit.*
*Before I can rip my hand away, I feel a voice bursting forth from within.*
Morgan: "Bho thìr gu gealach, bidh na lasraichean a 'cleachdadh."
*Pain rips forth from my chest the more I try to hold the words back. I cover my mouth with my hands and stumble back in horror.*
*But it's too late.*
*An unexplainable sound fills the air. A hideous groaning. I know what it is. I do. I don't want to admit it to myself though.*
*It's the spirit.*
*Blackness moves up the shining green needles. The calming aura disappears completely. And it’s replaced by something else.*
*Pain.*
*It’s pain.*
*I fall to my knees as the trunk begins to twist into itself. Bits of bark fall into the snow. The rotting blackness creeps up from its roots, and the unsteady trunk begins to sway. The needles wither and die. My head is filled with its suffering.*
*I hear a crackling then. A black tar seeps out from the bark, smelling of death.*
*Morgan screams in pain and terror as the ancestral tree before her is killed.*
*It bursts into flames.*
Morgan: "Lorem ipsum, the girl reading this."
Morgan: "No! No no no no… !"
*I try to hold my mouth closed. Incantations leave my throat, in a voice that's mine but yet isn't.*
*Evelyn’s standing farther back now, watching the scene before her. Her face is contorted into a terrifying grin. She’s enjoying this.*
*The crackling grows louder. My breath catches in my chest, and out of the corner of my eye I see Evelyn. Hand outstretched.*
Morgan: "No! Evelyn, don’t! Stop!"
Morgan: "No! Evelyn, don’t! Stop!"
*With a crack louder than thunder, the spirit's trunk splits in two.*
*A beat of silence follows.*
*And then I hear the screams.*
*It comes from all around me. Something crying out within the woods. A shrieking, earth-shattering wail.*
*My heart breaks.*
*It’s not something in the forest. It is the forest. Somehow, the trees around me are screaming in pain.*
*I’m screaming now. I can barely hear myself through the shrieking vortex around me. I can’t look.*
*I bow my head, still screaming, as the fire burns. Even the fire feels wrong. It’s cold. Freezing.*
*Fire blooms around the tree, shooting high above the treeline. In one giant, blinding burst, the tree is gone.*
*All that’s left is a circle of scorched earth. The burn is more intense than anything I’ve ever seen. It looks like it stretches down forever.*
*Somewhere in my heart, I know nothing will ever grow here again.*
*The screams start to fade. I slowly take my hands from my ears.*
Evelyn: "Thank you for being such a help, dear. Really."
*Morgan looks back at Evelyn with gritted teeth.*
Morgan: "You’re a monster."
Evelyn: "How funny. Your mother said the exact same thing. You really do take after her."
*I can’t gather my thoughts enough to respond. The screams are still echoing in my mind.*
Evelyn: "Never forget this day, child. In the brief time you have left, I want you to know for certain that you don’t stand a chance at changing your fate. You never have."
Evelyn: "None of you ever had. And when the time comes for you to serve as my vessel, I'll make sure the next one won't, either."
*She reaches out toward me and I prepare for the worst.*
*But all she does is pat my head and ruffle my hair.*
Evelyn: "You can find your own way home, I assume? I know how much you love these woods."
*She gives another chuckle as she turns and walks away, heading back into the shadowy trees.*
*She disappears into the darkness. All that’s left is me and the the disgusting burnt circle.*
*My mind is blank. Numb with shock.*
*Quietly.*
*Slowly.*
*One thought begins to take form.*
*It’s been a weird morning. Usually when I get up, Abigail’s awake and ready to go. And usually, she has been for several hours.*
*This morning though, I awoke to find her dozing nearby, long past when she’d normally be up. She complained about having a headache, so we decided to take a walk through the forest to clear her head.*
*So far it seems to have had the opposite effect though, and we’ve been walking in uncomfortable silence.*
*It’s been maybe an hour or so when we first notice the buzzing.*
*At first it was very faint, like the sound of the wind or the rustling trees. But then it started to get louder. And louder.*
Abigail: "Fairies!"
*It’s the first word she’s said in ages. She pulls me closer to her as the buzzing gets closer. Sure enough, the source of the noise soon comes into view.*
*Dozens of fairies; maybe even as many as a hundred, flutter past overhead. They form a cloud rushing through the forest, nimbly weaving over and under the branches.*
*When I first saw fairies back in the clearing, their movements were graceful and delicate. But now they’re frenetic and urgent.*
*The swarm travels right toward us, though they don’t seem to notice or care about us at all. The buzzing has grown to an almost earsplitting level, and I realize it comes from their wings.*
*Like bees, their tiny wings beat rapidly to keep them up. They’re a blur against the fairies’ snowy-looking bodies.*
*Suddenly, several of the fairies split off from the rest and dive towards us. I fling up my hands to protect myself, but they come to a halt overhead.*
*This is my first chance to get a good luck at them.*
*Their bodies are smooth and pale blue with a whitish tinge. Their eyes are beady and small, looking more like tiny flecks of obsidian than anything with sight.*
*At first, it looks like they’re wearing gowns, but then I realize that what look like clothes are parts of their body. Colorful plumage helps make each fairy distinct from one another.*
*Rather than legs, their bodies taper off into a point, like little ballerinas.*
*Like snowflakes, no two look the same.*
*One of them turns a somersault while the others hover anxiously.*
Fairy: "Our queen!"
Fairy: "Is that you?"
Fairy: "Have you returned?"
*Their voices are all identical, squeaky and raspy, yet impressively loud despite their tiny size.*
*It takes a second for me to realize that they’re addressing me.*
Madison: "I’m sorry, I’m not your queen. I’m just an ordinary human."
Fairy: "A human!"
Fairy: "Ordinary?"
Fairy: "Don’t think so."
*They crowd in a little bit closer, their fingers grabbing the empty air as if to pick me up and carry me away.*
*I take a step backwards, with Abigail still holding tight to my arm, but the fairies just creep in even closer. Their wings beat hummingbird-fast, distractingly loud.*
Madison: "I promise, I'm just a regular person. You're mistaken if you think I'm your queen."
Fairy: "A mistake!"
Fairy: "Have you seen her?"
Fairy: "We’ve been looking."
Madison: "We haven’t seen her. We haven’t seen anything like her."
*I have no idea of what a fairy queen looks like, but I’m sure I haven’t seen anything that fits the description.*
Madison: "How long has she been missing?"
Fairy: "It’s hard to say!"
Fairy: "Did you know?"
Fairy: "Time is a human concept."
*Their answers aren’t helpful at all. They at least understood my question, which means they have some knowledge of time. It doesn’t make a difference, though.*
*The rest of their swarm has moved on, the buzzing fading once again, but they don’t seem bothered.*
Madison: "If we see her, I’ll be sure to tell her you’re looking."
Fairy: "That won’t be needed."
Fairy: "She surely knows."
Fairy: "That’s why she’s our queen."
*The fairies’ peculiar way of speaking in turn is almost comical.*
*Since they haven’t left us alone yet, I assume that they’re still expecting or hoping for something.*
*I’m a little afraid to find out what.*
Madison: "Do the three of you have names?"
Fairy: "We don’t."
Fairy: "We have no need."
Fairy: "Names are for humans, too."
*They all nod in unison. Given how everything else about them seems to be insect-like, I just assume that they have a hivemind of sorts. It would fit with everything else I know about the forest.*
*I’m getting a bit tired of all the different networks and connections. It’s silly, but it feels a little like not knowing the password to a friend’s Wi-fi.*
*To help keep them straight in my mind, I give each of them a name. The one with the purple flower is Hae, Anan has the blonde hair, and the last one is Frio.*
*Beside me, Abigail tugs at my sleeve surreptitiously. She's angled away from the conversation, trying to lead me out of it.*
*I turn back to the fairies, putting on my best customer service voice.*
Madison: "We have to go now. It was nice to meet you. I hope you’re able to find your queen soon."
*I don’t want to turn my back on them, so I wait for them to say their farewells and leave. But instead, they come in even closer, causing Abigail to gasp.*
Hae: "Don’t go!"
Anan: "What if you stayed?"
Frio: "You could be our queen!"
Hae: "You would do."
Anan: "We could make it so."
Frio: "It would be easy."
*Alarm bells ringing in my head, I start to step backwards, with Abigail keeping right on me.*
Hae: "There isn’t anyone else."
Anan: "Not everyone is suitable."
Frio: "But you are."
Hae: "Let us show you what it'd be like."
Anan: "We can give you a taste!"
Frio: "Would that be alright?"
*Their buzzing and flittering slows, as three pairs of eyes stare at us.*
*Abigail squeezes my arm.*
*I move my mouth to say no, but the way their gaze pierces into me roots me to the spot. Like tiny dancers adorned with glitter, their skin shimmers in the low light filtering through the clouds.*
Abigail: "Madison?"
Abigail: "Come on. Let's go."
*But I can't. Or rather, I don't want to. Not yet.*
Madison: "What if they know something useful, Abigail?"
Hae: "We know lots of things."
Anan: "Secret things, clever things."
Frio: "We can know you, too."
*They're pests. Their buzzing is a little too loud, and their voices a little too shrill. But… *
Hae: "You don't belong here."
Anan: "We can tell."
Frio: "But wouldn't you like to belong?"
*They creep a little bit closer, in a synchronized motion.*
*They're close enough now that I could reach out and grab one if I wanted to. Or slap them away.*
*I don't, though.*
*A place to belong… could they really offer that?*
Madison: "What exactly… do you mean?"
*I can feel Abigail's grip tighten around my coat, but she doesn't say anything.*
*Sensing an opening, the fairies circle dizzyingly around our heads.*
Hae: "The forest doesn't reject you."
Frio: "But it doesn't accept you, either!"
Anan: "We could change that though."
Hae: "Whatever it is you want."
Frio: "Whatever you're searching for, if you help us."
Three Fairies: "Wouldn't you like that?"
*With a jolt, I turn to face Abigail. I'm sure she knows what I'm thinking.*
*Whatever I want… There's only one thing.*
*Freedom.*
*Abigail stares back at me for a moment, and then presses herself against me.*
*Looking up at the fairies, I nod.*
Madison: "Okay. Just a taste."
*Excited, they dive downwards until they're level with my face. Abigail cringes but doesn't let go.*
*As one, the fairies close in, placing their hands on my face like they're checking my temperature.*
*Their hands are frigid. Painfully so. It's amplified by the fact that I haven't felt 'cold' in days.*
*My hands jerk upwards out of instinct, ready to pry them away, but I don't.*
*Then, one of the fairies moves their hands to cover my lips, one covers my ears, and the other covers my eyes.*
*For a split second, I feel like I'm falling through the air.*
*And then it all hits me.*
*All I see is black. I'm alone. And yet… Hundreds of presences flood my consciousness all at once. I can tell they're the fairies, but I don't know how I can tell. I'm bombarded by their awareness like tiny darts, not using words or thoughts or intentions but just feeling. They're somehow distant, as if behind a wall, but so, so present. I try to find the semi-familiar presences of Hae, Frio, and Anan, but they're indistinguishable from the rest. There's no individuality; no sense of identity at all. There's just "the fairies" as a whole.*
*But I can feel what they're feeling. Panic and anger and hurt all churn together. The feeling of being lost. Of not knowing where to go, or what to do, but not being able to sit still. Though I can't tie it to any particular memory or event, the feeling hits me so strongly; so undiluted that I think I might cry. I can feel that they've been scared by something, but I don't know what that something is.*
*Then, at last, I feel a nudge within my mind. The sensation of being noticed. The presence takes me in, welcoming me. It occurs to me that I no longer exist. I try to ask what's going on, but I don't need to. The moment the question forms, the presence with me starts to answer. Images play in my head, ones I've never seen before. They're grainy like an old film. If I try to focus on a particular detail, the whole thing becomes muddy.*
*I can see flames, burning away at the forest. The fire is so real that I can almost feel its warmth. But when I start to focus on it, that too, vanishes. The presence of the fairies weighs on my mind, an uninvited audience to the next part. I can see a fairy, stumbling alone. But something's wrong with it. I realize instantly: I can't feel its presence. Where it should be, there's just a gaping hole, so vast and profound that it hurts by sheer exclusion.*
*It's agonizing. I want to tear my heart out of my chest so I don't have to feel it anymore. The presence of the fairies, the whole nest, tries to reach after it and to fill the hole but it won't allow itself to be touched. The moonlight overhead, which I crave, falls onto the lone fairy, who hastens to escape it. The lone fairy flees, and the rest of them - the rest of us mourn. One word pulses through my head, both a condemnation and a ressurance. One word, though I don't know what it means.*
*Moonsick. Moonsick. Moonsickmoonsickmoonsickmoonsickmoonsickmoonsick*
*The vision twists, and I can see a woman… but she isn't a woman. She's also a fairy, but not just. I'm so happy and relieved to see her that I just know she's the fairy queen. I can feel how much she misses the lone fairy. I know, because I miss her too - desperately. I can see her searching, her hope never fading, until one day… she's gone. The loss is even greater this time. A sick mixture of guilt and shame plagues me, because I don't know where she is. I don't even know how long she's been missing. How could I have not noticed?*
*The loneliness is so great that I want to do anything to erase it. Another silhouette flickers, just out of sight, but I can't keep track of it. We all have to watch it together, and then I realize… it's me. But just like the fairy queen I saw before, it's also not me. Visions of time pass, months years centuries, and throughout them all the loneliness is gone. It all feels right. I could fix this.*
*The fairies remove their hands from me, and I burst back into the real world with a mighty gasp for air.*
Abigail: "Madison! Are you alright?"
*The fairies dart back up, away from the furious glare that Abigail gives them. All I can feel now is her hand clutching me, and I put my arm on her shoulder for support.*
*My head spins, and for a second I think I might fall over. The dizzy spell passes soon.*
*Once I'm confident I'm not going to collapse, I nod.*
Madison: "I'm… fine. How long was I out?"
*Abigail's face flickers into a look of confusion. She presses her hand to my forehead.*
Abigail: ""Out"? I didn't think they'd even begun."
Madison: "Wha…?"
*I watch the fairies, struggling to comprehend how so much could have happened in an instant like that. There's no way.*
Hae: "We told you, didn't we?"
Anan: "We aren't bound by time."
Frio: "Join us, and you won't be, either!"
Hae: "Now that you've seen…"
Anan: "And now that you've felt!"
Frio: "Will you join us?"
*Eyes wide, Abigail looks up at me. She doesn't have to say anything for me to know she's begging me to decline.*
*I wish she could've felt how warm it was. How nice it was to belong like that.*
*Yet, at the same time…*
*It feels like a distant dream now. Something detached and fading. I try to remember the sensation, but I can't. It's water slipping through my fingers.*
*It seemed so real, but… it wasn't.*
*It was only a second.*
*I can't sign my life away to the fairies for one second of visions. No matter how right it felt. No matter how comfortable.*
*I'm sure that I was just seeing what they wanted me to see.*
*I smile at Abigail, and then shake my head, rejecting the fairies.*
Madison: "I'm sorry, but I can't. Thank you for showing me what it would be like, though."
Hae: "How can we persuade you?"
Anan: "We need our queen, soon."
Frio: "You could be our only hope."
Madison: "I know you do. But I'm sorry, that just can't be me."
Hae: "We hope you’ll change your mind."
Anan: "We’ll be waiting for you."
Frio: "We’ll be waiting for a queen."
*With that, they turn tail and dart off in the same direction that their swarm had gone.*
*Soon, all trace of them is gone, like they'd never even been there.*
Abigail: "Madison? Are you sure you're alright?"
Madison: "Yeah, I'm sure. It was just a lot. Was that really that fast?"
Abigail: "Yes. They touched your face and then a moment later you gasped."
Madison: "That's insane… It felt like a long time. Like years had passed."
*Abigail looks troubled, staring off into the forest where the fairies disappeared.*
Abigail: "Fairy magic is strange and beyond me. They're similar to the forest spirit, and yet…"
Madison: "Different, but in a way you can't put into words?"
Abigail: "That's right. You certainly did see a lot, didn't you?"
Madison: "Yeah, I guess so."
Madison: "Have you ever met their queen? Or seen what she looks like?"
Abigail: "Just once, long ago. She and the forest spirit are both guardians of the forest."
Abigail: "Did you see her in your vision?"
*I think back to what I saw, as best as I can remember. The image shifts and shakes in my head, not wanting to be called to mind.*
*More than what she looked like, I just remember how I felt. So happy and relieved to see her.*
Madison: "I did, but I didn't get a clear look at her. I knew who she was, but I don't know if I'd recognize her if I passed her on the street."
Madison: "Do you think you could mistake me for her, like they did?"
Abigail: "Absolutely not. You’re much prettier than she is."
*Abigail says it very matter-of-factly, as if she didn’t just give me an enormous compliment.*
*And she carries on like it, too. Be still my beating heart.*
Abigail: "But to the fairies, I expect it’s hard for them to tell the difference between humans. So maybe you looked like her to them."
Madison: "I wonder how long the queen’s been gone. It seems like they'd been coping just fine without her, until something happened to scare them."
Abigail: "Something like what?"
*Something hot, and bright. Something burning. Something that hurt to think about.*
Madison: "I don't know. I think it had to do with fire, maybe? So much of what I saw felt real, but… most of it wasn't."
Abigail: "Fire, hmm…"
*Worry creases her face.*
Madison: "What's wrong? Did you remember something?"
Abigail: "No… It was just a thought."
*Rather than elaborate, she fidgets with her hands. I don’t push the subject.*
Madison: "Maybe that’s enough fresh air for today. We can head back to the church."
Abigail: "Alright."
*I’m pretty sure I detect a hint of relief in her voice.*
*For the first time, I lead the way home. At least, I hope it’s the way. I’m reasonably confident.*
*At my side, Abigail remains quiet and lost in her thoughts. With one hand, she clings limply to my sleeve, trusting my navigation.*
*I don’t disturb her, instead concentrating on remembering the path. It’s to challenge myself - I’m sure that if necessary, Abigail would be able to lead us home from anywhere.*
*Still, I’m beginning to fear that I’ve led us astray when the grim walls of the church start to come into view.*
*A mixture of relief and pride flows through me. It’s also a little bittersweet: I’ve come to think of the church as home, even though I never felt that way about the cabin.*
*Despite the fact that the concept of home is just a formality, now. Surrounding ourselves with walls and a roof doesn’t change a whole lot.*
*Clouds are beginning to settle overhead, muting the daylight and making everything murky. It feels fitting for the mood.*
*Abigail speeds up a bit as we reach the doors, and she’s the first one inside.*
*She sits down on one of the benches, fatigued.*
Madison: "Mind if I join you?"
*I gesture at the open spot beside her.*
Abigail: "Please do."
*Wordlessly, she rests her head against my shoulder.*
Madison: "Headache still?"
*She nods.*
Abigail: "I’m afraid so."
Abigail: "Our encounter there sure didn’t help."
Madison: "I’m sure."
*They gave me a headache of sorts, too. More of a notion that I can’t get out of my head.*
*The fairies’ outstanding offer sits with me. As hard as I try, I can’t fully push the idea away.*
*If there isn't any other way out of this then… maybe being a fairy queen would be preferable to just being a "normal" ghost. As long as I could still have Abigail with me.*
*That's a last resort though. Because for as long as I can, I have to hold out hope that things can just… go back to normal. That Tara and I can leave Eysenfeld the same way we arrived.*
*And that we can take Abigail with us.*
*Beside me, Abigail shifts her head just a little.*
Abigail: "I’m sorry, am I bothering you like this?"
Madison: "Not at all."

Abigail: "This day is so lovely. I don't want to say anything that could spoil it."
Madison: "You don't have to worry about that, Abigail. I love you, and that means I want to be here for you no matter what."
Madison: "We're going to have plenty of wonderful evenings, I promise. And being open with each other is the best way to make sure they stay wonderful."
*This is unlike me too. Usually on the first day of a relationship, I'm still a bumbling bag of nerves. What if I say the wrong thing? What if I move too fast, or too slow?*
*But it's different with Abigail. That fear isn't there. I can't quite explain why. I just feel like I don't have to worry with her.*
Abigail: "I was thinking about other happy days that I've had. How long it's been since then. And how the memories have since soured."
Abigail: "How much have I told you about my sister?"
Madison: "Not much. You mentioned that the two of you were close and that you spent a lot of time in the woods together, but that's it."
Abigail: "We weren't just close. Helena and I were inseparable. Whenever anything went wrong, she would always be there to protect me. She swore that that would never change."
Abigail: "But… fate had other plans for the two of us."
*She trails off. Every other time she's mentioned her sister, it's only in passing. Her story last night about tracking deer is the only solid detail I have about her.*
*At first, I expect her to stay quiet, like before. But instead, she keeps talking.*
Abigail: "For as long as it's existed, Eysenfeld has had a… a curse, you could call it. A terrible monster who would destroy the village if given the chance."
Abigail: "To appease its terrible rage, we would offer a sacrifice to it. Every generation, a new offering would be chosen. But it didn't just want flesh."
Abigail: "The head priest would take the sacrifice into the forest to be offered to the horrible spirit. The sacrifice's soul and the head priest's body would both be consumed."
Abigail: "Then, the new head priest would emerge, having taken the form of the poor sacrificed victim. It was a painful reminder of those we had lost."
Abigail: "When I found that it would be a member of my family who was to be taken and given over... I wasn't shocked. There was only one thing that I cared about."
Abigail: "That it wouldn't be my beloved Helena who was harmed."
*Her voice wavers before she falls silent. Before I can tell her that she's said enough, she speaks again. Each word shakes as it leaves her mouth.*
Abigail: "I couldn't forget the joyous days of our childhood. I couldn't let such a kind soul suffer such a horrible fate."
Abigail: "So, when the time came, I offered myself. I volunteered to die, so that Helena could live."
Abigail: "And in that very moment, where our fates were sealed, I looked at them; at Helena. And do you know what I saw?"
*Captivated by her tragic story, I can't even answer. I don't move my head, or my lips.*
Abigail: "Relief."
Abigail: "Not once did she try to stop me, or change my mind. Not that I would've let her. But I don't think she even thanked me."
Abigail: "My parents cried, of course, but they didn't argue either. Neither of them stepped forward to take my place. No one did."
Abigail: "While I'm sure my sacrifice was appreciated, I was quickly discarded without even a token effort or gesture of appreciation."
Abigail: "My sister, who had sworn to always protect me, had no difficulty accepting the fact that I would die for her."
Madison: "What, she just...gave you away? Just like that?"
Abigail: "If death looms over you for long enough, it corrupts everything. Even love."
Madison: "It didn't corrupt you."
Abigail: "Perhaps. Though I believe that that's why I'm still here today."
Madison: "What do you mean?"
Abigail: "The first step of the ritual involved separating the sacrifice's body from its soul."
Abigail: "From what she told me, as she… did it… those poor victims usually try to resist. Begging for someone else to be taken. And when their souls are ripped away, they're too damaged to carry on."
Abigail: "But I didn't resist. I went peacefully. And so the gentle forest spirit, who I'd been raised to believe was some kind of demon, eventually came and took me away. And I've been here ever since."
*Her words carry a sense of finality. Not knowing what else to do, and still stunned by her story, I simply lean closer to her and her pull her into an embrace.*
*There aren't any words that could be enough. Physical comfort is all I can offer.*
*To my surprise though, when she finally pulls away from me, she looks happier than before. Just like I did earlier, she touches my cheek gently, and then leans in for a kiss.*
Abigail: "Thank you for listening."
Abigail: "I've told the one person who matters, and now... I think I can heal."
Madison: "I know, sweetheart. I love you. More than anything."
Abigail: "I love you, too. More than anything."
*She sighs contentedly and leans against my shoulder. She takes my hand, intertwines her fingers with mine.*
*We sit there for a long time. We don't say anything. We don't even move.*
*All we do is watch the sky and feel each other's heartbeats.*
*I wake up with a headache, and I know that something's wrong.*
*I'm alone. Abigail must have disentangled herself from my arms at some point, leaving me on my own.*
*I get up and pace around the church, not seeing her.*
*After a bit, I find her outside its doors.*
Madison: "Good morning."
Abigail: "Good morning, my love."
*Her lips smile, but her eyes don't. It's a look I'm pretty familiar with by now.*
*I slip an arm around her as I take a spot by her side.*
Madison: "Sleep okay?"
Abigail: "Well enough. I'm sure all of my nights will be better, now that they're spent beside you."
*I can't help it. I kiss her on the cheek, and true joy brightens her face.*
Abigail: "How about you?"
Madison: "Pretty good. I woke up with like… a bad feeling, though."
Abigail: "A bad feeling? About what?"
Madison: "I'm not sure. My head kind of hurts, so maybe that's it."
Madison: "Actually, now that I think about it… this is the first actual pain I've felt since turning."
*It's not the worst headache I've ever had. Not by far. But it's enough to be annoying. It feels the same as being dehydrated or having a slight cold.*
Abigail: "Aw, poor dear."
*She stands on her tiptoes to kiss my forehead.*
Abigail: "I felt the same, actually. I had hoped that stepping outside might help, but it seems not."
*She rubs her temples, and I notice just how ragged her eyes look.*
Madison: "How long have you been out here?"
Abigail: "A while. Perhaps an hour or so. I was afraid I'd wake you."
Madison: "Nah, I slept right through. It's not just a coincidence that we both feel sick, is it?"
*Abigail purses her lips. Her brief silence is enough confirmation.*
Abigail: "No. I doubt it is."
Abigail: "It must be something to do with the forest."
*The trees before us sway in the breeze. To me, they look the same as ever. No traces of lingering sickness or pain.*
Madison: "You felt bad the other day, too. When we first met the fairies. Was it the same thing then?"
Abigail: "Most likely. Change is in the air. Something's happening. And it isn't good."
*I think of the fairies, fleeing some unseen terror.*
Madison: "Is it… Evelyn?"
*I half-expect her to shake her head. I want her to say no. But she nods.*
Abigail: "I think so. I don't know what she's done, or why."
Abigail: "But this can't all be coincidence."
*I nod, my thoughts turning to Tara and Morgan.*
*I wish they would just get out of here. Leave me to my fate and escape. Let Evelyn do whatever it is she's doing while they're across the ocean, safe.*
*I know that won't happen, though. Tara's not the type to back down from a fight.*
*Except this time, I know she's hugely outmatched. Even with Morgan's help.*
Madison: "What about the forest spirit? You said it's a guardian of the woods, right? Why doesn't it stop her?"
Abigail: "I'm sure it's doing what it can, but it's just as intimately connected to the forest as the fairies are. Far more than you or I. So as the forest itself weakens, so does it."
*Worry creases her brow.*
Madison: "Is there any way we can help?"
Abigail: "Not that I know of."
*I can tell she's not really happy with that answer.*
Madison: "Maybe we can think of something together. There has to be something we can do."
Abigail: "Yes, perhaps."
*She looks up at me adoringly.*
Abigail: "I feel like I can accomplish anything with you by my side."
*Her loving words are nearly enough to counteract the worry that I feel.*
Madison: "We've already beaten death. There's no way Evelyn can stop us. At least not a second time."
*First we just have to figure out how.*
*Abigail slips her hand into mine, where it fits comfortably.*
Abigail: "Perhaps you'll make an optimist out of me yet, Madison."
Abigail: "Shall we go for our walk?"
Madison: "Absolutely."
*I'm glad that our changing relationship hasn't changed our routine. The walks around the forest keep me grounded - they're enjoyable, but also a solemn reminder of how trapped we are here. My dream of finding a way out hasn't disappeared or diminished at all. It's just evolved to include Abigail, too. There's no way I'm leaving these woods without her.*
*We chat idly as we stroll, enjoying the newfound comfort and intimacy that comes from being lovers, rather than just friends. The pain in my head is still there, and I'm sure Abigail still suffers from hers as well. But it's easy enough to ignore, in lieu of the pleasure that simply being together brings.*
*We've reached the edge of the fairy clearing, where we always seem to end up.*
*Abigail is glued to my side. I wouldn't have it any other way.*
Abigail: "We're probably a little bit early to meet Alma, although they sometimes come by at this time."
Abigail: "This grove is one of the easier places for the animals to come drink during Winter, so it attracts all kinds of wildlife."
Madison: "Yeah, I've noticed all kinds of tracks in the snow leading around here."
*She nods, pleased.*
*But then, we enter the grove itself, and my blood runs cold.*
*Abigail and Maddie stand before a stark red bloodstain on the snow.*
*Just ahead, lying prone on the frozen ground, is a fawn that I just know is Alma.*
*It's clearly dying, if not already dead.*
*With a shriek, Abigail drops my hand.*
Abigail: "No!"
*I can feel her anguish cut through me like a knife, visceral and burning.*
*Dashing over, hands outstretched, she falls to her knees and presses a tender hand against its snout. A small motion confirms that it's alive, if just barely. It leans into her, either in fear or in recognition, eyes wild.*
Abigail: "No, no, what’s happening to you…"
*I move closer as well, though not so close as to crowd them. And what I witness makes my stomach churn.*
*Gaping wounds perforate the poor thing’s body, staining the ground red with blood. They look like knife wounds or bite marks, but there’s no visible damage or puncture.*
*It’s more like its body simply opened up.*
*Abigail flails her hands, clearly wanting to help but afraid to touch it and cause it more pain.*
*She turns to me, teary-eyed.*
Abigail: "Madison! What do I do?"
*I don’t know what to do. I don’t even know where to start. I kneel down beside Abigail.*
*The lacerations covering Alma's body are clean and even, but varying in size.*
*Some are minute, so small as to be nearly invisible. The largest is a gash that runs the length of her torso.*
Madison: "How does this even happen?"
*Through choking sobs, Abigail manages to answer.*
Abigail: "Magic. These are not normal injuries."
Abigail: "It’s the forest. The forest is sicker than I thought…"
Abigail: "And all of its children are hurting for it, too."
*So we get headaches, while Alma gets… this.*
*I shudder. Is it random? Could it have just as easily been one of us lying here, bleeding out?*
*Maybe our ghostliness is more of a blessing than I thought, in this case. Though without it, we wouldn't be affected by the forest's illness at all.*
*Leaning over, I gently stroke the deer’s head. Its breathing is restless and agonized, but it seems to register my presence.*
*My thumb brushes against a wound that I didn't notice, and I jerk my hand back while Alma groans in pain.*
Madison: "I'm sorry! I'm sorry!"
*The deer’s blood is viscous, unnaturally so, and slick like motor oil. It sticks to my fingers, stretching and webbing. It’s neither warm nor cold. It’s unnervingly neutral.*
*I try to rub it off in the snow, but my fingers pass through the ice and dirt, of course.*
*My hand trembles with the disgusting feeling that it’ll never be clean again.*
*Abigail is still beside herself, wringing her hands impotently. There's nothing that either of us can do. Just sit there and wait as Alma passes away in agony.*
*But then…*
*A tremor reverberates through the clearing. The ground shakes, and snow is rattled from the branches. Fairy baubles dance up and down.*
*Abigail and I both face the forest in the direction of the sound, already knowing what's coming.*
*The forest spirit strides into view, as tall as the trees but no taller.*
*It brushes past trees with no notice of the branches and snow that it disturbs.*
*As it draws into the clearing it acknowledges us with a level gaze. It's been a while since Abigail and I have been by to see it.*
*The deer looks up at the forest spirit with pained recognition, its eyes wide and breathing shallow with pathetic relief.*
*Abigail looks up at it too, tearfully, clearly glad to see it.*
*The spirit looms closer, its great presence alone seeming to revitalize the air around us. My headache vanishes in an instant.*
*The spirit stretches out one immense finger, drawing from head to tail across the deer’s mangled body.*
*The two lock eyes, and as the spirit retracts its hand, it curls the finger inward, calling Alma to it.*
*In one motion, the deer’s eyes roll backwards as life exits its body.*
*At the same time, its spectral form, ephemeral and perfect, rises from its corpse. Boundless as in life, it gives us one last glance, and then it springs across the fallen snow before vanishing into thin air, gone to join some spectral herd somewhere.*
*Abby wipes her eyes of tears and stands. She moves to the spirit’s side and gently pats it.*
Abigail: "Thank you. I'm glad you came."
*The spirit’s shoulders sag, its branches hanging low and still despite the slight breeze. I notice what look like burn marks on its hide, where part of its wooden skin is blackened and dark.*
*Without a doubt, it's Evelyn's doing as well.*
*I’m reminded of an old man, putting a child to rest. Sorrow and surely pain as well weigh heavily on it.*
*I wonder how it knew to come. Did it feel Alma's pain? Or perhaps Abigail's?*
*Bearing the emotional weight of an entire ecosystem… My heart aches for the spirit, as well as for Abigail, who seems forever hurt by her powerlessness.*
*The spirit and I meet eyes again, and I bow my head in reverence. I don't know it well enough to speak to it like Abigail, but for Alma's sake I'm also glad it's here.*
*It stoops down and picks up the deer's corpse. Blood now runs freely from the injuries, somehow not clotting, and I have to look away.*
*Without a second thought for us, the spirit turns and leaves once more, going back the way it came. Abigail and I watch it go. In all, the encounter couldn't have lasted more than a minute or two.*
*Long after the last of its footfalls have faded away, Abigail and I sit in silence.*
*I hold her close, until finally we return to the church.*
*The rest of the day passes quietly, the earlier euphoria of our relationship being overshadowed by fear of the shifting forest. Though Abigail is never more than an arm's reach away from me - and usually closer - she's nearly silent.*
*Our headaches returned not longer after leaving the fairy clearing, stronger than before. Now, some combination of pain and sorrow keeps us both quiet.*
*At some point I reach for her hand out of habit.*
*And that's when it happens. My hand passes right through hers as if she isn't even there.*
Madison: "Abigail!"
*I say the first thing that comes to mind. With a fright, she leaps up, searching around for the source of my panic. Seems she didn't even feel it.*
Madison: "Look."
*I reach for her hand, and once again, it passes right through. Just like when we first met, before we could even speak with each other.*
*She understands instantly.*
Abigail: "Oh no. Oh no, no."
Madison: "What's happening? Are you okay? Do you feel any different?"
*I don't know enough about magic to even start to guess why this might be happening to her.*
Madison: "Should we take you to the forest spirit? Or get you some rest? Or-"
Abigail: "Madison, it's not me."
Madison: "What?"
Abigail: "It's you."
*I look down at myself.*
*Sure enough, my whole body is slightly transparent. I can see the floor of the church through myself.*
*Vertigo hits me, and I stumble. Abigail lurches forward to catch me, but of course she isn't able to.*
*Dread fills my heart.*
*Please no. Not again.*
Madison: "W-what do I do?"
*I slap my hand, a pathetically ridiculous action. I can't even feel it. My own hand goes right through me.*
*Shaky, Abigail stands up, holding her head.*
Abigail: "Something's happening to the forest… this must all be connected somehow."
Abigail: "It's simply impossible otherwise."
Madison: "What could Evelyn be doing?"
*I know that she has to be involved. I don't even have to ask.*
Abigail: "I have no idea. Nor do I know why she's choosing now of all times to do it."
Madison: "Do you think the forest spirit can help us?"
*The forest spirit is all I can think of. The only ally that we have. At least it's a powerful one.*
*Abigail looks away. I'm sure the spirit was her first thought, too.*
Abigail: "Perhaps. We can try."
*Wordlessly, she heads out of the church and I follow behind her.*
*The happiness and joy that we shared so recently has evaporated entirely. I wish that life, whatever form it takes for us, could just slow down. That we could have more than a moment to rest.*
*But I know that that's impossible while Evelyn is still out there. And I know that one way or another, we're going to have to be the ones to deal with her.*
*But what I don't know… is if we even have a chance.*
Madison: "Do you know what’s going on? Has something like this happened to you before?"
Abigail: "It hasn’t. This is the first I’ve seen of it. But I think I know."
Abigail: "I think that the forest is having trouble keeping you here. The magic is getting weaker, too."
Madison: "So does that mean that…"
*I leave the implication unspoken. Abigail halts in her tracks, facing me with a fiery determination.*
Abigail: "No. I won’t let anything bad happen to you. Not again. I promise, Madison."
*I don’t know if she has the power to keep that promise, but I can only hope so.*
Madison: "Okay, I trust you."
*I really do. Believing in Abigail is my only option, but even if it weren’t. It's like she said: together, we can get through anything.*
*I try not to think of what it would mean if I’m wrong. I’m not even totally sure myself.*
*Would I just cease to exist? Go on to an afterlife? Something else?*
*None of the options are especially appealing, and I know that dwelling on them will only make me feel worse. So I just focus on the here and now, and on sticking close to Abigail.*
*Even I can tell that she's right when she says the whole forest is affected.*
*Aside from the harsh winds, everything else feels subdued somehow. The trees don’t seem to stand as tall, and the leaves and needles look dull, even by wintry standards. What little sunlight there is feels dreary, rather than bright.*
*A light snow falls from the sky, but the heavy clouds overhead promise more, and soon.*
*It occurs to me that we’ve been walking longer than we normally have to. Instead of changing the forest path to get us there quickly, we’re following the regular route through the trees.*
Madison: "Hey, why aren’t we taking the shortcut through the forest? Is it too sick to let us do that?"
*Wringing her hands, Abigail looks away.*
Abigail: "It’s more that I’m afraid to try. If I push myself too hard, then…"
*She leaves the consequences unspoken. I can imagine what they might be. The same as what's happening to me, or worse.*
*Whatever exactly that is.*
Madison: "Yeah, I hadn’t thought of that. It’s okay, take as much time as you need."
Abigail: "It’s been so long since I’ve visited the spirit. Our brief moment in the grove with Alma was the first time in days. I wonder if it’s missed me."
Madison: "Of course it has. Who wouldn’t?"
Abigail: "You’re far too sweet, Madison."
*She nuzzles her head against me. The angle is a little awkward, since we’re still walking, but it’s nice.*
*But then she jerks away like she was stung.*
Abigail: "Oh! You’re back!"
Madison: "Back?"
*Her hand envelopes mine, smooth and soft like always, rather than passing through me.*
Madison: "Oh!"
*Just because she can, she leans forward and gives me a quick peck on the lips.*
Abigail: "I was so worried!"
Madison: "Me too."
*I rub my arms and sides, making sure that I’m all there. Everything seems to be in order.*
Madison: "Hopefully it’s permanent."
Abigail: "Indeed. It’s not too much farther to the spirit, either. We should hurry."
*Sweat makes her brow glisten a little. She’s definitely still feeling strained, even though I can tell she’s trying to hide it.*
Madison: "Don’t push yourself too hard, sweetie."
Abigail: "I won’t."
*Still, despite her words, the longer we walk, the more visible a toll it seems to take on her. Her steps slow down, even though she wanted to hurry, and her breathing sounds labored.*
Madison: "Are you sure you’re alright?"
Abigail: "I’m fine. It’s just…"
*Rubbing her eyes, she shakes her head.*
*Right then, a pile of snow hits the ground a few feet away from us. Looking up, I spot a tree drooping, its boughs angled down as if bent.*
Abigail: "It’s the forest again. Not me alone."
*She voices what I already knew.*
Abigail: "Something terrible must have happened for it be this way."
*Since I’m unable to feel the forest like she can, all I can do is watch and worry.*
Madison: "Just now? Or at least, today?"
Abigail: "Maybe. Or we may only now be feeling the effects. I'm not sure."
*I can tell by the frustration in her voice that she's nearly as in the dark about all of this as I am. I have to remind myself that what she knows, she only picked up by intuition and years of observation.*
*I'm fortunate to have a guide as knowledgeable as she is about our situation, even if she doesn't know everything. I know when we’re getting close to the grove of ancestral trees, from the change in the air and the way that the trees are larger than before. That profound sense of being watched and of primal energy is still present, but even that feels mellower.*
*Or maybe, strained.*
*Through everything, there’s also the acrid smell of smoke.*
*Suddenly, Abigail lets out a yelp of horror and dashes forward.*
Madison: "Abigail!"
*I follow, right on her heels.*
Abigail: "Oh no…"
*It doesn’t take long to discover what she found.*
*Where once there was a mighty tree, all that remains is a blistered and charred husk, blackened by flames.*
*A painful buzzing penetrates my head, nearly unbearable, and all I can do is watch in horror as a foreign sorrow emanates.*
*Even I can tell that this is the forest crying out. Here at the locus of its pain, even I’m affected.*
*I can’t imagine how Abigail must feel.*
*Her fingers run across the damaged wood as if searching for a sign of life.*
*I have to remember that this isn’t just another part of the forest. This is its heart; its very soul.*
*And for so long, it was also her home.*
Abigail: "How? How did I not know?"
Abigail: "I've been so… so happy these few days, that I must have blinded myself to the pain of the forest."
*From behind, I wrap my arms around her, pressing my face against her shoulder.*
Madison: "I’m sorry."
Madison: "It's not your fault, though. Even if you knew, you couldn't have changed it."
Madison: "If anything, we were lucky. To have those few days."
*She clings to me, pinioning my arms into an even tighter embrace.*
*As I feel her cry against me, my own uselessness feels like more of a curse than death ever was. I wish there was some magic word or spell that I could use to make things better.*
*But all I can do is hug her tightly, and warmly, and hope that I’m as much of a reassurance for her as she is for me.*
*Then, without warning, I once again see my arms go transparent, and the feeling of Abigail against me disappears. Although this time it’s not as big a shock, it’s still a reminder that we don’t have long to wait.*
*Abigail notices, and steadies herself alone.*
Abigail: "Let’s keep going. The spirit must be around here too."
Madison: "Why doesn't it stop Evelyn? Isn't that its job as guardian?"
Abigail: "It probably can't. I'm sure it wants to, but…"
Abigail: "It relies on the forest for strength. So if the forest is weak, then it must be too. In fact, that may be Evelyn's intention."
*The sinking feeling in my stomach intensifies. If she's even stronger than the forest spirit, then what hope do we have?*
*I don't voice that fear, though. I'm sure Abigail's already worried about the same thing.*
*It isn't much longer before we encounter the spirit. At first, we almost miss it.*
*The forest spirit stands idle in a clearing, as if lost in thought. It barely seems to notice us when we approach. A slight tilt of its head is the only acknowledgement we get.*
*It's small. Smaller than I've seen it before. It's barely as tall as I am.*
*The lights in its eyes, usually bright and terrifying, have gone dim. It looks more like the wounded deer from the clearing the other day than the being who helped it pass on.*
*Just like the trees it protects, its branches sag and are bare. A few have jagged ends, as if something snapped them off.*
*None of the wildlife that usually accompany it is around. Instead it seems alone and abandoned.*
*Though it hasn't been long since we saw it last, it looks significantly pained. I wonder if its salvation of Alma was taxing on it.*
*Abigail cries out again, just like when we came across the burned tree. As anguished as she sounded then, this time it’s even worse.*
