Tara: "I wanted to talk to you about something. Or ask you about something. I dunno. It can wait if you still wanna work on getting your stuff sorted."
Morgan: "Is something wrong?"
Tara: "No! I mean, I don't think so. I've just been thinking about some stuff."
Morgan: "Like?"
*Tara takes a deep breath and scoots back on the bed. She curls her legs up towards herself, taking on a surprisingly subdued posture.*
Tara: "It's… well… "
Tara: "You know how Abby's been calling us a couple all day? Even though we told her we're not?"
Morgan: "Yeah. She's not very good at taking hints."
Tara: "I'm not sure if that's it. I think she's just, like, direct. Kinda like you."
Tara: "Runs in the family, I guess."
Tara: "I know we agreed that we were going to stay… friends, or whatever you'd call this, until this was all over. I get that."
Tara: "But after seeing the two of them, I'm not sure if I feel like that anymore."
Tara: "We've still got a lot of shit left to deal with. We still don't have a plan. But… I think that's okay?"
*She shrugs halfheartedly and looks down. I want to give her a hug, but I can't move.*
Tara: "Abby put a name on this thing we have when we couldn't. And maybe she's wrong. But I don't want her to be."
Tara: "I really love you, Morgan. I don't want to deny that. I want to be closer than just friends that love each other. I… "
Morgan: "Don't."
*The word leaves my mouth before I have a chance to think about it.*
Tara: "What?"
Morgan: "Please don't ask."
Tara: "I'm sorry, I… I thought… "
Morgan: "It's not that I don't want to. That's why I'm asking you not to say it."
Morgan: "Because I know I'll say yes if you do."
*She looks up at me. I can practically hear the cogs turning in her head.*
Tara: "Why don't you want to say yes?"
Morgan: "People get hurt when they get close to me. I don't even like having friends because of how guilty I feel when Evelyn gets in the way."
Morgan: "I'm already so close to you. Closer than I've ever been to anyone. But if I put a label on it… A part of me just feels like it would put you in more danger."

Morgan: "Besides, I've never had a girlfriend before. I don't know what it could mean for me. Or for you."
Tara: "What do you mean? What about the one that taught you how to knit?"
*Oh no. How could I have forgotten about that lie?*
*It was just so easy to say back then. It made me seem like I knew what I was doing. Made it easier to ignore what happened with those girls.*
*Tara's understood everything about me. But I still don't want to tell her about what I did with them. Or why I did it.*
Morgan: "I… um… "
Tara: "Hey, it's cool. I've had plenty of girls lie about having girlfriends before. It's just a thing people do."
Tara: "We can just forget about it. We're past lies now, right?"
*Her words stab at me like needles. It's not like she's trying to force this out of me. I could even tell myself that this isn't a lie. That it's just not telling her about something she never asked about.*
Morgan: "There's more to it than a simple lie."
*Wait, what am I saying? What am I doing?*
Morgan: "There are still things I haven't told you about me. About what I've done. Things that could make you change your mind."
*Tara manages a weak smile. It's hard to be reassuring when you don't know what you're reassuring someone about.*
Tara: "If you don't want to date, that's okay. I get it. But nothing you can tell me is gonna change how I feel about you."
*She sits forward again and pats a spot on the bed next to her. But right now, I’d rather be miles away from her, not closer.*
*I hate feeling like this. I’ve finally learned how to get close to people, and now I’m back to pushing her away.*
Morgan: "Do you remember what I called myself the first time we talked to Geladura?"
Tara: "… A shell."
Tara: "But you know that’s bullshit now, right? We’re gonna make sure Evelyn can’t get in your head anymore."
Morgan: "Evelyn isn’t the only one that made me feel that way."

Morgan: "I didn't learn how to knit from an ex-girlfriend. I learned from Hannah."
Morgan: "I had feelings for her. We were close. But she cut off all contact with me when I told her how I felt."

*I clench my fists, digging my nails as far into my palm as they'll go before my limbs freeze up. As far as they'll go until the icy hex within me takes over again.*
Morgan: "Nobody talked to me for a month."
Morgan: "You told me that you spoke to some girls our age back when you were doing interviews. Did you ask them about me?"
Tara: "We, uh… yeah. We did."
Morgan: "What did they say?"
Tara: "Nothing really bad or anything. One of them seemed closer to you than the other two, and her friend was kinda teasing her about it. Just typical stuff."
*As if any of them have the right to mock each other. They’re all the same.*
Morgan: "I probably slept with her."

Morgan: "They always had some kind of excuse. They'd start dropping "hints." Talk about how they’d always been curious about what it was like to be with a girl. How they didn’t have anywhere to be that day."
*I still can’t look at her. Any rational person would judge me for this. I know I’ve judged myself plenty.*
Morgan: "… They always left right after. Most of them wouldn’t talk about it. They’d just go back to their normal lives."
Morgan: "I guess some of them talked, though. Soon enough, I got a reputation."

Morgan: "Evelyn never let me feel like this body belonged to me. She chose my clothes. She chose my style. She even chose what I do with my hair. It was her body, and she wanted it how she liked it."

Morgan: "But things have been different with you. For the first time, sex started to mean something. Being open started to seem possible. Being myself started to seem possible."
Morgan: "But all I know about myself is how easy it is for other people to use me."
Morgan: "If I go out into the world and live for myself… I don’t know what kind of person I’m going to become."
*The shaking has started to subside, replaced by a painful knot in my gut. Almost there. I can make it.*
Morgan: "What if I end up becoming someone you can’t love? What if my shell is the only worthwhile part of me?"
*Without warning, Tara throws her arms around me and pulls me close. I wrap my arms around her waist without even thinking.*
Tara: "You're not a shell, Morgan. You're the furthest thing from that."
Tara: "Just because someone tried to make you something other than yourself… or because people took advantage of you… "
Tara: "That can't destroy the real you. That you has always been there."
Tara: "And I know it a lot better than you might think."
*She nuzzles my neck, and I can feel the dampness of her cheeks. She's crying.*
Tara: "You’ve got passion. You’ve got personality. You’ve got conviction. You’ve got so much awesome stuff in you that I couldn’t name it all if you gave me a whole week. All of that is the real you."
*Tara grabs me by the waist, and gently leads us over to the bed. I press my forehead against hers and - for once - allow myself to be comforted.*
*She strokes my hair. Time slows to a standstill. We kiss.*
*I hold Tara close in my arms. She's nestled close to me. The sun is long gone but the moon is bright enough to illuminate her smile.*
*I'm smiling too.*
*I close my eyes and let myself relax. I think I might actually sleep well tonight.*
*But as always, luck isn't on my side.*
Tara: "Hey."
Morgan: "Whuh?"
Tara: "Do you hear that?"
*I strain my ears and see if I can sense anything out of the ordinary. But apart from the usual sounds of the forest, it’s silent.*
Morgan: "I can’t hear anything. Maybe you dreamed it?"
Tara: "Unless this is a dream, it’s definitely real. I can hear it right now, I swear."
*I sit up in bed and look over at her. She’s already most of the way up, head swaying as she looks for the source of whatever she’s hearing.*
Morgan: "Well, what does it sound like?"
Tara: "Kinda like, uh…"
*She makes a strange whooshing noise and twirls her arms around. I don’t see how arm movements are part of a sound, but I doubt I’d understand what she’s talking about no matter how she put it.*
*She looks back at me for confirmation, but all I can do is shrug. In response, she jumps out of bed and starts pulling her clothes on.*
Tara: "I’m gonna go see what it is. You coming?"
*Whether or not this sound is real, I’m not about to let her go off on her own. I promised I’d protect her, and that includes protecting her from mysterious noises.*
Morgan: "Right behind you."
*Besides, as powerful as Maddie is… there’s still a chance Evelyn could be trying something.*
*I follow her out of bed and pull on my shoes. She gives me a quick grin in response. Excited to have me along for the ride again.*
*Tara bolts out the door as I finish tying my laces. A second later, I hear her banging on the door to Maddie and Abigail’s room.*
Tara: "MADS! ARE YOU GUYS MAKING THAT NOISE?"
*I pull on my sweater and walk into the hall in time to see Maddie slowly open the door of her room, covering herself with a blanket.*
Madison: "Tara, it’s nearly 2 in the morning. Whatever this is, can’t it wait until morning?"
Tara: "Come on, not you too! I thought it was coming from your room!"
*Abigail’s voice calls out from the room, along with the rustling of sheets.*
Abigail: "What was coming from our room?"
Tara: "Are you kidding me?"
*She turns and storms away from the door, moving out into the kitchen.*
*I nod apologetically at Maddie before following Tara.*
Morgan: "Babe, come on. Let’s just go back to bed. I’m sure that whatever it is can wait until morning."
Tara: "It keeps getting louder, though! What if it’s something dangerous? Maybe Evelyn has her own evil forest spirit and she’s riding it towards the cabin or something?"
*There's a pang in my heart. It's cold and familiar. If the sound is that loud, but none of us can hear it… maybe it’s only meant for her to hear.*
*As I try to figure out how to get her back to bed, Maddie and Abigail join me in the hallway.*
Madison: "So what’s she doing this time?"
Morgan: "She woke up hearing some kind of whooshing noise that I couldn’t hear. I thought she’d just imagined it, but she can still hear it."
*Maddie rubs her chin, puzzled. Abigail looks out at Tara with a worried expression. I can't shake the unsettling feeling in my chest either.*
Madison: "This isn’t the first time something like this has happened, so I’d be inclined to dismiss it. But with everything else that’s happened this month-"
*Before she can finish her sentence, Tara turns towards the door.*
Tara: "There! It’s right outside!"
*Abigail realizes it first. She steps towards Tara, reaching out her hand.*
Abigail: "Tara, don’t! What if it’s-"
*Oh no. No no no.*
*I’m an idiot. Why didn’t I figure it out sooner?*
Morgan: "Tara, stay away from the door!"
*She doesn’t look back. As she takes another step towards the door, I will myself to start moving again.*
Tara: "Now you guys are actually gonna be able to see what I’m talking about."
Tara: "You’re gonna feel so dumb for doubting me."
*I’m running towards her, but it’s too late. She grabs the handle and throws the door open.*
Tara: "Wait, what?"
*There’s nothing outside. Maybe that sight will be enough to bring her back to her senses.*
Tara: "The sound’s gone! It was right there!"
*She turns back to look at me. I’m only a couple steps away, and I can see the expression on her face.*
*She’s terrified.*
Tara: "Babe, what-"
*A flash of light fills the room. I’m stopped dead in my tracks as I raise my arm to cover my eyes.*
Morgan: "TARA!"
*The light dissipates, and my eyes slowly readjust. In the haze, my fears are confirmed.*
*She’s gone. Tara’s gone.*
*I'm frozen. I can’t process what’s happening.*
*I'm not sure how long the tears start rolling down my face before I notice them.*
*Maddie and Abigail look at me in silence.*
*And then I start screaming.*
*I've never made a sound like this. It's an angry roar that fills the cabin.*
Morgan: "NO!"
Abigail: "Morgan, I…"
Morgan: "IT WAS SUPPOSED TO BE ME!"
*It's happening again. Just like everyone before her.*
*I love her. And now, because of me… *
*I collapse to the ground.*
Morgan: "Not her… please… "
*Hot tears fall from my cheeks to the floor below.*
*I feel a pair of arms wrap around me, and a voice in my ear.*
Abigail: "Morgan, it's okay… It'll be okay."
*I slowly turn towards her. She and Maddie both stand close to me. I can see how worried they are. For Tara, and for me.*
*Maddie puts her hand on my shoulder. I flinch, but I don't pull away.*
Madison: "She's still alive. I can feel it."
Madison: "Whatever just happened didn’t kill her, but it did move her. I can't figure out where yet. But I can sense her, somehow."
Morgan: "You're sure?"
Madison: "I'm sure. We can find her."
Abigail: "I think I know where she might be."
Morgan: "Where?"
Abigail: "If it was Evelyn who took her, she most likely brought her to the church. That's where she performed the ritual to…"
*She trails off. But I know what she was about to say.*
*The transfer ritual.*
*She wants Tara's body.*
Morgan: "But why her? That's… what she was raising me for. Why would she take her?"
Madison: "I'm not sure. But I've got a feeling there's only one way to find out."
Madison: "I'd hoped we'd have time to come up with an actual plan before we had to face her, but we can't just let her have Tara."
*I'm already heading for the door. Maddie and Abigail follow close behind.*
Morgan: "We can figure something out on the way. Let's go."
*We'll think of something. We have to.*
*This time is going to be different. I'm not going to let Evelyn win again.*
*I'm getting Tara back. No matter the cost.*
*And this time, I'm going to make sure Evelyn never hurts anyone again.*
*For better or for worse…*
*This is the end.*
*I don't even have time to be disoriented before I hit the stone ground, hard.*
*I struggle to pick myself up and stand, but before I can, footsteps approach - Evelyn's, of course - and I'm hauled to my feet.*
*She does it with one hand. She's way stronger than she looks.*
Evelyn: "Hello, Tara."
*As expected, we're in the church. She drags me over and throws me onto the altar.*
*I try to resist, but my limbs won't move.*
Tara: "Oh hey, thanks for the ride."
*I'm scared. More scared than I've ever been in my whole life, but I don't want her to have the satisfaction of knowing it.*
*She walks away, out of sight, although I can still hear her close by.*
Tara: "They're gonna come for me, you know. We figured out your whole plan. They're probably already on their way."
Evelyn: "Let them. I'll kill them, too."
*Her tone is casually disinterested. She can't even give me the courtesy of pretending she cares?*
*I wish I could move my head to see her. But I can't. I can't move any part of me, other than my mouth.*
Evelyn: "It’s always such a nice feeling to return here. Such a sense of renewal and energy. Although I imagine right now you don’t feel the same way."
Tara: "I dunno, I’m digging the ambience."
*I’m about to die and I’m making jokes. I mean, what else am I supposed to do?*
*We had a plan, one with a good chance of working. I know that Mads and the rest won't abandon me. They'll just switch it up a little, same as if she'd taken Morgan like we expected.*
*Honestly, it's probably better for everyone that Morgan's the one who's still free right now. She's less likely to screw something up than I am.*
*Still, it’s all I can to to suppress the little voice inside me telling me to be afraid, to acknowledge that I’m going to die. Or worse.*
Evelyn: "Really, you should be thanking me."
Tara: "For what? I must have missed it."
Evelyn: "Because by dying here, you've given poor Morgan another chance. If she runs away now, I have no more need of her. I'll let her live."
Evelyn: "Of course, if she turns up here with any delusions of grandeur, she'll die along with the rest of them."
*A chill runs up my spine.*
*Because I know, beyond a shadow of a doubt, that there's no way she won't come for me. That she won't run away.*
*Still, the way that Evelyn can talk so cruelly about her own daughter makes it hard for even me to keep up my attitude.*
Tara: "You really don't feel anything, do you? You raised her, you lived with her! And even after all that…"
Evelyn: "Finally, you get something right. I don't feel anything for her. You got something wrong, too."
*Evelyn halts, and then stalks closer to the altar. Close enough that that I can see her wildly dilated pupils. The razor sharp points on her fangs.*
Evelyn: "I didn't raise her. I cultivated her."
Evelyn: "From the minute she was born, I prepped her for this fate. To be a worthy vessel."
Evelyn: "How could I have known that a much more useful one would arrive at my doorstep, just days away from my ascension?"
*Now she takes a step back, letting the moonlight fall across her. She arches her back like a cat, enjoying it.*
*She raises an arm, and I'm lifted into the air like a circus performer, suspended from nothing.*
*My arms are snapped to my sides. I struggle as hard as I can, but I still can't move at all. I can't even flail around.*
*Still, her words pique my curiosity, and I'm dying to know what she meant. Since I might be dying anyway, I may as well ask.*
Tara: "More useful how?"
*She grins.*
Evelyn: "Because. This night is very, very special."
Evelyn: "For years - centuries, even - I've whiled away my time here in Eysenfeld. It's amused me to no end."
Evelyn: "But… I've grown bored of it. I desire to see more of the world."
Evelyn: "And while any human body would do, I suppose, there's merit in one such as yourself. One with influence, and fame."
*I don't even know the words to convey how disgusted I feel. It's not enough for her to steal my body, but she also wants to pretend to be me?*
*I think the horror I feel must show on my face, because she smirks. As she does so, her skin starts to shimmer and shake, like there's light just underneath it.*
*A thin thread of ethereal matter, like the spell that she used to bring me here, ekes into existence. It stretches between her hand and my chest.*
*The second it comes in contact with me, I feel like all of the strength is sapped out of me. Like I'm being sedated.*
*I hope I'm only passing out, and not dying.*
*I'm not sure if the two feel different.*
*It would suck extra hard if I was dying, though, because then my last sight would be Evelyn, sneering cruelly.*
*Just before it all goes black, she manages to get in one final taunt.*
Evelyn: "What an ironic fate for you, don't you think? You came out here seeking magic, and now..."
Evelyn: "You found it."
*We're up and ready to go nearly the instant that Tara disappears. It's a shock, of course, but not one that messes up all of our plans.*
*I walk to the front door, which is still open and banging in the wind.*
Morgan: "I'm going to fucking kill her if she hurts Tara."
Madison: "Tara will be fine. She's tough. Besides, we've got a plan, right?"
*After a long moment, I nod.*
Morgan: "Right."
*Maddie turns back to face Abigail, who's half-hidden by the bedroom door.*
Madison: "Are you sure you want to go, sweetheart? You can stay here…"
Abigail: "Of course not. There's no way you're doing this alone."
Abigail: "I'm just… a little nervous, that's all."
Madison: "Me too. I'm a lot nervous, actually."
Madison: "We'll be fine, though."
*If she keeps saying that, it'll be true, right?*
*I wish I could believe it. I wonder if Maddie even does.*
*I scoop up Geladura, and wait outside. We need to leave, now, but I force myself not to rush them along.*
*A minute later, they follow me outside.*
*They run together, passing familiar scenery.*
*We hurry out into the woods. Despite some wind and fog, the night is relatively clear.*
Madison: "Are you sure you don't want me to just take you to the church? I could have us there in a second."
Morgan: "I know. But she can't know about your powers yet. If we arrive too fast, then Evelyn will know something's up."
*It would be nice to take the easy way like that. But we can't do anything rashly. Not if it might hurt Tara in the long run.*
Madison: "Right."
Morgan: "We have to wait until her soul is out of its body. That's when we'll send the signal."
Madison: "Do you know how long that'll last? Like, how long it'll be exposed."
Morgan: "I have no idea. I hope it won't be instant."
Madison: "You hope?"
*I nod.*
*I wish that I knew more. I really do. But it's magic, and magic is rarely predictable. That's the only thing that I can say with confidence about it.*
*I'm not sure what more I should say. This is where we'll have to part. Even though I'm making the rest of my journey on foot, Maddie and Abigail will need to use magic to get to the fairy clearing.*
*Before we do though, there's one last thing I need to say.*
Morgan: "Um, Maddie."
Morgan: "I know there isn't really time, but I need to say this. Since I might not get another chance to."
*Geladura nuzzles my chin, reassuring me.*
Morgan: "I'm sorry."
Morgan: "That doesn't even come close to describing it, but it's all that I can say. For bringing you out here. Getting you involved in all of this."
Morgan: "I never imagined any of it would turn out this way."
Morgan: "So, if you still hate me, I understand."
Morgan: "I just wanted you to know."
*It feels good to have the words out there. I've spent so much time apologizing these past few weeks. But it's still a bit of a relief every time.*
Madison: "Morgan."
Madison: "Well, first of all, I accept your apology. But I promise, I never hated you."
Madison: "It was my fault for acting like such a bitch when we first got here. And as for everything else… Well, some of it might have been Tara's fault, but a lot of it was my fault too."
Madison: "So let's get this over with. We'll save Tara, take care of Evelyn, and then we'll all go home."
Madison: "Simple, right?"
*I just nod again. I'm not sure I can talk right now. Not without crying. And I hate crying.*
*I wish it were that simple. I hope it will be that simple.*
*But it rarely is.*
*I swallow the lump in my throat. I have to say something.*
*This is hardly a plan. It's barely more than an idea. It relies far too much on luck, and everything going our way.*
*But it's all we have.*
*The furious winds tell me that I'm in the right place. As if there were any doubt.*
*When I approach the doors to the church, they blast open. I've been expected.*
*I don't waste any time. I rush in.*
*Tara floats in midair, suspended by invisible magic. She lies prone, but with her arms and legs apparently locked in place.*
*She looks dead.*
*A colorful cloud floats above her, with a long tail spiralling back towards her body.*
*I don’t even need to be told - I know that it’s her soul.*
*It thrashes about, as much as a cloud can, but is seemingly locked out.*
*Below, Evelyn stands, hunched, with her back to me. A trail of malevolent white magic tethers her to Tara.*
*Evelyn's appearance has changed drastically, becoming more monstrous. Her body begins to take on icy qualities, while her face becomes disfigured. Her hair is an icy white.*
*Moonlight pours through the window behind them, piercing Tara and illuminating Evelyn. When she hears me enter, she stands up straight, her back cracking obscenely.*
*She's disgusting.*
Evelyn: "Morgan, you came."
*Her voice isn’t the voice I know. It’s raspy and inhuman. It echoes around the church, louder than it should be, dragging itself from corner to corner.*
Evelyn: "I knew you would. You always do."
*Her body spasms and twitches as if electrified. Still, her voice remains steady and flat.*
Morgan: "Of course I came. You're not taking Tara. You're not taking anyone else, ever again."
*She laughs. But it sounds like a choke. There's no humor in it. It's inhuman.*
Evelyn: "Do you know how many times I've heard that before? Countless."
Evelyn: "And yet… here we are."
Evelyn: "Predictable. You humans always are. Curiously so."
Evelyn: "You have all this freedom, but don’t know how to use it."
Evelyn: "Pity. Pity, pity, pity."
Evelyn: "Soon, I'll have taken the body of your friend, and you will be dead, and I will be far, far gone."
Evelyn: "You see, I've grown tired of playing pretend out here in the woods. I've decided it's time for something new."
Evelyn: "And Tara… is going to give that to me."
Geladura: "You'll still be a pretender."
*Evelyn seems to notice Geladura for the first time, and she licks her bloody lips.*
Evelyn: "You… so you're here, too."
Evelyn: "Fitting."
*Evelyn's gaze narrows, then her eyes widen once again. She points imperiously at Geladura and I, and the stone walls of the church rumble in response.*
Evelyn: "I can tell, you're still no queen. No more than that brat who stands beside you. You're still powerless."
Evelyn: "Tell me, who's the pretender now?"
*A brick detaches itself from the ceiling, plummeting down straight towards us.*
*I dart out of the way, barely managing to avoid it. Geladura yowls as I squeeze her harder than intended.*
Evelyn: "You're just as pitiful as she is. What did you think you could do? How did you think you had any chance?"
Evelyn: "Or is it just human nature to try, even if you're doomed to fail?"
*The moon is at its peak, or close to it. If she waits much longer, she won't be able to complete the ritual, and she'll have done our job for us.*
*Maybe she knows this, because she turns away from us once again.*
*I have to stop her.*
*I push my legs forward, but some invisble force pushes back. Like moving through quicksand, I can barely inch forward.*
*Something's protecting her. Inch by inch. I have to keep moving.*
*A pulsing ball of magic starts to form in front of Evelyn, and she focuses intently on it. The light from the moon falls directly on her.*
*Her skin reacts to it, bubbling and boiling like hot wax. It must be agonizing, but she doesn't react. Maybe she's beyond pain.*
*Geladura leaps out of my arms, making her own way beside me. Her breathing is ragged and labored. Neither of us waste energy on more words.*
*I don't know what I'll do if I reach Evelyn. I just know that I can't sit still.*
*The coalescing ball of magical energy continues to grow and grow, taking on a sinister, dark hue. The color of a moonless night. The color of an empty void.*
*It starts to spin, crackle and shimmer.*
*That's when I realize this isn't for the soul transfer. It's too strong. Too unstable.*
*Wha-*
*Before Morgan can finish speaking, a tendril of malevolent energy suddenly shoots out straight towards her. *
*It moves too fast for me to react. It pierces me like an arrow.*
*Godless, fiery pain burns through my veins. My whole body stiffens, refusing to move. I can't even cry out.*
*Each individual atom of my body feels like it's being tortured. Like a burning needle is being driven through them.*
*I want to scream. I want some sort of release. But I'm not even granted that mercy.*
*Behind the pain there's a rising, mounting horror.*
*I'm going to die here after all, aren't I?*
*My vision starts to go black. I welcome oblivion. At least then, I won't have to hurt anymore.*
*I try to move my eyes to fixate on Tara. If I'm going to die, I at least want my last sight to be of her. Even like this.*
*I can't even manage that. All I can see is Evelyn. She says something, her lips moving, but I'm deaf to it. All I can hear is a deep, resonating ringing sound in my ears, like some perverse bell toll.*
*Then Evelyn's eyes go wide. Wider than before.*
*The air behind her shimmers and shakes, just like her magic spell. Empty space tears itself open, and she's sucked backwards like a ragdoll.*
*In an instant, the magic and the pain is gone, leaving behind no trace.*
*I fall forward, my momentum having been locked in place with me. And just ahead, Tara crashes to the ground, limply.*
*I stagger to my feet, not trusting my body to support me, but it does. The complete absence of pain is a shock in its own right. I feel like I should be experiencing the aftereffects, but I'm not.*
*All that lingers is the memory.*
*Geladura scurries over to me.*
Geladura: "Morgan! Are you alright?"
Morgan: "I think so. I feel… fine, actually."
Morgan: "What happened to Evelyn?"
*She licks the back of her paw for a second, then stops.*
Geladura: "I… I'm afraid that I panicked, and sent the signal to Madison. I tried to help you, but I couldn't. My own power is still weak. Not nearly enough to match Evelyn's."
Geladura: "Of course."
*We rush over to where Tara lies prone on the cold floor. Her soul is nowhere to be seen, which I hope means it returned to her body.*
*I cradle her as tenderly as I can, checking for a sign of a pulse or breath.*
*I gently prop her against my chest, stroking her hair.*
Morgan: "Please be okay. Please."
*Two tears drip down my face, landing on hers. I didn't realize I was crying.*
*Geladura lingers beside us, nuzzling her face against Tara's cheek.*
Geladura: "She's alive. I can feel that much."
Morgan: "Is she okay though?"
Geladura: "I don't think she should experience any lasting effects from the magic. It's the fall to the ground that worries me more."
*That's true. She fell from pretty high up, onto a stone floor. I wipe away the tears that fell on her, steeling myself to prevent anymore.*
*I trace my finger across the curve of her jaw, down to her lips.*
*They press together, just a bit, to kiss my finger.*
Morgan: "Tara! Tara, can you hear me?"
*I'm afraid to let hope spring into my heart again.*
*Her lips move, just barely.*
Morgan: "I can't hear you."
*There's a soft, blessedly warm gust of air against my face as she sighs, and forces her eyes open.*
Tara: "I said, 'I'm dumb as bricks, babe.' It'll take more than head trauma to hurt me."
*I pull her up and into a hug, and she pats my back.*
Morgan: "You're okay? You're sure?"
Tara: "Yeah, I'm sure."
*I wipe my eyes as we part to face each other once again.*
Morgan: "I was so scared…"
Tara: "I wasn't."
Tara: "I knew you'd save me."
*I don't deserve someone like her.*
Tara: "Is there any way we can get to Mads? She might need our help."
*We both turn to face Geladura.*
Geladura: "I can take us there. But I don't know how much use I'll be once we arrive."
*Tara moves to question, but I place a hand lightly on her arm. Geladura's barely holding on as-is, and her guilt at her inability to do more is palpable.*
*It's all up to Maddie.*
Morgan: "Let's go. There isn't much time."
*I help Tara to her feet, and she immediately winces, hissing in pain.*
Morgan: "Are you okay?"
Tara: "I'm fine, I'm fine. Guess I just hurt my ankle in the fall."
*She's able to stand on her own, giving me a shaky smile.*
*I don't like it, but there's nothing I can do right now. At least it's only her foot, and not her head or back.*
*With a graceful jump, Geladura hops into my arms once again. I stand next to Tara who leans against me for support.*
*With one hand, I support the cat. With my other, I take Tara's other hand in my own. Then I nod.*
Morgan: "We're ready."
*My heart pounds as Abigail and I arrive in the fairy grove.*
*This place has been the setting of most of my favorite memories here in the forest. I could have never guessed that it would be where everything ends.*
*I can feel the fairies - my subjects - watching from close by. They don't show themselves, but their presences are like tiny little pressure points on my consciousness.*
*They're just as tense as I am.*
*Closing my eyes. I reach out to the forest, asking for its aid. It still hurts, badly. It laments the terrible pain that it's suffered.*
*I promise that it'll be okay. It's my duty as queen to ensure this. I hold the forest in my heart, and promise to rid it of the moonsick one.*
*Abigail and I walk to the water's edge. Nervousness tears at my stomach. Geladura's signal could come at any time. I have to be ready.*
*High above, the moon offers its light to us. The whole lake is dyed in its silver glow, shimmering and refracting and amplifying.*
*Moonlight caresses my face like a lover, warm and invigorating. I indulge in it, letting it sink in through my skin, my roots, my forest. Every little second could be crucial.*
Abigail: "How do you feel?"
*She holds my hand loosely in hers.*
Madison: "Nervous. In a way I've never felt before."
Madison: "Nothing else in my life has ever been this important. There's never been life or death consequences like this."
Madison: "What if I screw up?"
*I expect to see fear reflected in Abigail's face as well, but when I turn towards her, she looks serene and unbothered.*
Abigail: "I have complete faith in you, Madison."
Abigail: "The fairies said that not just anyone could be queen, but that you could. They saw something in you, too."
Abigail: "We're all putting our trust in you. The whole forest is, and our friends, too. We wouldn't do that if we didn't believe in you."
*I wish I could use a magic spell to boost my confidence. For all the power that I hold right now, it sure feels insignificant.*
*So far, everything has come naturally. Moving through the woods or interacting with its denizens is easy. But to use my magic against another being… That's something else entirely.*
*But I can't let that kind of attitude overwhelm me. I can't be defeated before it even starts.*
*Abigail's right: everyone is counting on me. I can't let them down.*
Madison: "Thank you."
Abigail: "For what?"
Madison: "For believing in me."
*She leans forward, and kisses my cheek.*
Abigail: "Always."
*Always.*
*That word has meant a lot of different things to me lately.*
*My concept of 'forever' keeps shifting and changing. Even now, I'm not entirely sure what it means.*
*I suppose that, after tonight, I will.*
Madison: "You know, when we were here before, there were times that I wished it could last forever. Where it was peaceful, and just the two of us."
Madison: "But now, more than anything… I'm ready to go home. I'm ready for us to make new memories, together."
Abigail: "Nothing would make me happier."
Abigail: "When you came into my life, I thought that it would be fleeting. Like a nice dream, that I might someday look back upon and smile. I didn't dare imagine, nor hope, for anything more."
Abigail: "But now…"
Abigail: "Well, perhaps you've made an optimist out of me, after all."
*I kiss her forehead, and then her nose, and then her lips.*
Madison: "I love you. More than anything."
Abigail: "I love you too. More than anything."
*Together, we face forward.*
*And that's when I feel it.*
*A cold flash of foreign fear.*
*It stabs through my head and the idyllic scenery. I recognize the origin immediately.*
*Geladura's panicked signal.*
*I step away from Abigail, clearing a space. I force myself to breathe evenly as I reach within myself, mentally, searching for the magic.*
*I call upon the power within me. Unlike before, when it blossomed and flourished at my request, now it lies dormant and afraid. I understand. I'm afraid too. But there's no time to be afraid.*
*Using my authority as fairy queen, I coax the magic forth. The church's presence aches like an infection, and I promise the forest that I'll help heal it. The magic ripples through the woods. It sets upon the church, finding Evelyn easily. I can't see her in my mind's eye, but I can feel her. Feel her hatred and scorn and… fear.*
*So I'm not the only one who's afraid, then. That's more reassuring than anything else.*
*If she detects the magic that's coming for her, then I can't tell. At my will, the tendrils of magic wrap around her like vines. Then I stick my hand out - my physical, actual hand - and I pull.*
*The result is immediate. Just above the surface of the lake, a ripple disrupts the air as a massive rift opens up. Within seconds, Evelyn falls forward through it, collapsing in a heap to the shore.*
*It's wrong, though. She's still in her regular body. It's not just her vulnerable soul like it should be.*
*I take a fearful step back.*
*Why did Geladura send the signal early?*
*My blood runs cold. What if something happened to her and Morgan?*
*What if she wasn't too early? What if I was too late?*
*I try to rally my thoughts, and concentrate on what to do.*
*She quickly picks herself up, wild eyes casting back and forth. Her appearance is monstrous. It finally fits her personality.*
*Her gaze falls on me, and her lips curl in hatred or disgust.*
Evelyn: "So it's you."
Evelyn: "I see that even death didn't want you."
Evelyn: "And now here you are. Stinking of snow and pine. Stinking of them."
Evelyn: "You wear their clothes. You bear their crown. But you are not one of them."
*She lumbers forward, her steps seeming pained. She's either dazed from being pulled her or from the effects of her own spell. But I can't count on that to last.*
Madison: "You're right, I'm not. Just like you'll never be a human."
*I need to buy myself time while I try to think.*
Evelyn: "Human? Is that what you think I want?"
Evelyn: "I will be more than human."
*A gust of wind stirs up, likely at her calling. The trees shake, and I plant my feet firmly to keep from losing my balance.*
*I feel the barriers in my mind freeze and break away.*
*She's gone. As the world spins around me, the moon shines down on the lake.*
*I search for her presence with my mind, but I can't find her, like a fish that slipped off the line.*
*I need to calm down.*
*My heart races as my head begins to ache and throb.*
Evelyn: "You're so fragile. So easily damaged. So easily broken."
*The biting wind intensifies, threatening to sweep my crown away. Abigail shields herself with one arm. Evelyn, however, remains unaffected.*
Evelyn: "What's wrong, little child? Why not make this easy on yourself? I can make it painless for you this time."
*Reality and illusion blur and blend, and the fabrics of my vision tear.*
*She takes another step forward in my mind, shattering what little focus I had. She's almost close enough that if she lunged forward, she could grab me by the throat.*
*Instead, Evelyn scratches at her own neck, her nails drawing blood, but she doesn't seem to notice.*
*High in the sky, the moon watches over us, indifferent. I can feel its light sustaining me, the same as it bolsters her. She turns to look up at it, shivering.*
Evelyn: "Do you know why you were the first to go? Because you were the easiest. All alone, off in the woods. Tragic accidents happen all the time out there."
Evelyn: "Really, it's my daughter you have to blame for all this. It's a shame you won't get another chance."
*I try to find her again with the magic, but I can't. It's like she isn't even there. Where her consciousness should exist, there's nothing but a gaping hole.*
*A void that feels familiar somehow…*
*Her eyes glow red as she faces me again.*
Evelyn: "Looking for me?"
*My consciousness is immediately assaulted. My head pounds as I feel Evelyn enter it like an intrusive thought.*
*With every push and pull she rips at my soul. My magic quakes under her power, flickering and fearful.*
*I try to reach out and grab it, pull my own consciousness and strength into me, but every time I get close it's yanked away.*
*Unrelenting. Overwhelming. "Die, die, DIE!" It echoes and reverberates in my skull.*
*I choke, momentarily forgetting how to breathe.*
*Every single thought. Every single idea. Snatched and destroyed.*
*I can't even see anymore.*
Evelyn: "I knew it. Your gifts are wasted on you. Queenship is wasted on you."
Evelyn: "What good is power if you don't know how to use it?"
Evelyn: "All of you are the same."
*A searing pain in my head forces me to my knees.*
Evelyn: "Your friends couldn't save you then, just as you can't save them now."
*An unseen force shoves my head upwards, making me look at Evelyn.*
*A peculiar ringing blocks out all other sound, except for her words. I'm deaf to the wind, to the forest, to Abigail.*
Evelyn: "See what you've done, and despair."
*She gestures imperiously towards the empty space between us. Just like the portal I used to bring her here, another hole in space rends itself it open.*
*Through it, I can see a glimpse of the murky, stained glass window of the church.*
*Then, it upends, unceremoniously spilling out my friends.*
*Or at least, their bodies. Nausea hits me at the sight.*
*Tara's there, her body limp and broken, arms and legs splayed at an obscene angle. Blood trickles from her mouth, beneath her open, sightless, eyes.*
*Morgan is also there, in a heap, though her body isn't as mangled as Tara's. A grisly wound stretches from her ear, across her face and down her neck. Blood stains the sand beneath her.*
*Even Geladura lies there, motionless. Though her back is turned to me, a small puddle of water forms beneath her, spreading as she seems to melt.*
*I scream.*
*I want to run towards them or clutch at my heart, but I'm pinned in place.*
*How? What went wrong?*
*Was this why the signal came too early?*
*I shouldn't have left them alone. I should've gone with them. This is my fault, isn't it?*
*Evelyn's attack on my mind ceases in order to let me truly comprehend the horror before me.*
*As much as I fight it, my heart gives way to what Evelyn asked of me.*
*I despair.*
*Our feet touch solid ground, and Tara immediately loses her balance. I grab her shoulder to keep her from falling.*
*Geladura pants heavily, the magic having taken a heavy toll on her.*
*Maddie, Abigail and Evelyn are on the far side of the lake. Only Abigail seems to notice us. Even from a distance, the terror on her face is clear.*
Morgan: "Come on, they need our help."
*Tara takes one step forward, and then falls to the ground, clutching her ankle.*
Tara: "Shit."
*Just then, Maddie screams, in pain or in horror. A sick feeling in my stomach, all three of us turn to look, although there's nothing new to be seen.*
*I know from experience that doesn't mean she isn't in pain.*
*Abigail is tugging on her arm, but Maddie doesn't react at all. She must be beyond even feeling it.*
*And just behind Evelyn, a familiar looking ball of sinister light is forming, sparkling in the moonlight.*
*Its tendrils jolt and jag with a familiar hunger.*
*Maddie doesn't even know.*
*Evelyn turns back and looks at us, a sadistic grin on her face. The tendrils of magic shift, and a feeling of impending doom fills my lungs.*
*I look back at Tara, who scowls at her foot as she tries to move. There's no way she'll be able to.*
*I don't have long to act.*
*If I run for Evelyn, she might hurt Tara before I can get there. If I don't, there's no telling if Maddie will be able to stop her.*
*Of course, it might already be too late to help her, anyway.*
*Tara's gritting her teeth through the pain. If I leave her on her own, she'll be helpless.*
*I can't let that happen.*
*I face towards Evelyn again. I have to stop her somehow. That's all that matters. Even if I…*
*The realization that it might cost me my life rushes through me.*
*All along, I've been aware of that as a vague possibility. I'd always known it might come to that.*
*The future with Tara I've been pushing at the forefront of my mind. The pain I've been pushing aside to move forward.*
*The memories and emotions of the past overflow and hit me all at once. All the things I thought I could forget.*
*But now in this moment, faced with that decision…*
*I freeze. My whole body seizes up, and I fall to the ground.*
Morgan: "No."
Morgan: "No, no…"
*Not here. Not now. I can't afford be paralyzed like this right now.*
*But the cracks of my psyche freeze over and hold, and the curse within me grips my every muscle.*
*Years. Years of feeling nothing. Weeks of pushing forward. And still, fear triumphs.*
*Is this really how it will end? Am I really not strong enough to do anything?*
*Something soft brushes against my arm, and I look over to see Geladura hurrying towards the lake.*
*She turns back towards me, meeting my eyes.*
Geladura: "Take care of Tara."
Morgan: "What do you mean? What-"
Geladura: "Goodbye, Morgan. And thank you."
*With that, she dashes off into the lake, quickly disappearing. I don't even get the chance to yell her name.*
*I'm able to stand, knowing now that whatever happens is out of my hands.*
*If I'm going to die, at least the curse gives me the courtesy of letting me face it on my feet.*
*Just then, Evelyn's ball of magic crackles noisily, and I fling myself in front of Tara, shielding her with my outstretched arms.*
*She cries out in fear, and I brace myself for the end.*
*But it never comes.*
*A warmth spreads throughout the clearing instead. A bright light blinds me. And I hear Evelyn, vocalizing something between a gasp and a snarl.*
*I turn, just in time to see the silhouette of a beautiful woman leap from the lake.*
*Though I can't see her face, I just know that it's Geladura. The real fairy queen.*
*For a brief moment, she hangs there, suspended and framed by moonlight.*
*She waves her hand in a slow, elegant gesture like a conductor directing an orchestra. Light glitters at her fingertips.*
*Then she hurls the light towards Evelyn.*
*Two things happen simultaneously.*
*The shards of light shred through Evelyn's form, leaving glistening, luminous trails where they cut her.*
*She remains on the shore, frozen in a position of pain, as her whole body shakes and shudders like a glitched image on a TV screen.*
*Then she starts to melt. Starting from her feet and working quickly up her body, she collapses inward on herself, leaving behind nothing but a thick, black goo that stains the sand.*
*At the same time, Geladura limply crashes back to the water, her lifeforce spent. Her fragile form dissipates into starlight that's scattered across the rippling surface, her brief return as queen already over.*
*Everything is quiet.*
Tara: "Is it over? Is she… gone?"
Morgan: "I think so."
*Melting away into sludge is a fitting, and hopefully permanent end.*
*Tara and I separate, and she leans on me for support.*
*And then, we start to hear a buzzing sound.*
*It comes from all around us, spreading through the trees.*
*Fairies burst forth from their hiding spot, swooping over our heads and across the lake.*
*They don't pay any attention to Tara and I, nor to Geladura's sacrifice.*
*Instead, they flock straight to Maddie and Abigail, forming a thick cloud over their heads.*
*I'm sorry, Geladura.*
*I push thoughts about her in the back for now. I help Tara over to where our friends are, as quickly as we can manage.*
*Maddie looks distraught, while Abigail tries in vain to comfort her. As we get closer, I'm able to hear just what it is the fairies are saying.*
Fairy: "Our queen! Our queen!"
*They chant the phrase over and over. I feel nauseous as I realize the consequences of what's happened.*
*Without Geladura, there's no fairy queen to take Maddie's place. By her own agreement, she has to stay here.*
*Forever.*
*A loud buzzing fills the air, and we watch the swarm of fairies as they rush toward us from the trees.*
*I step back to stand beside my friends as the fairies arrive, joyously fluttering above Geladura's head. A bright smile illuminates her face as her people welcome her home.*
*They don't speak, their need for human language now gone. They don't acknowledge me at all.*
*I strain to try to spot Hae or Frio or Anan, but I can't. I'm sure they're there, amongst the rest of the hive, but they don't pay me any mind at all. Maybe that's for the best.*
Tara: "So this is like… really cool."
*She and Morgan are both watching, somewhat awestruck.*
Tara: "When we get home, Mads, you've gotta write an autobiography. Either that or the best fantasy novel ever."
Tara: "I'm sure you could make millions off the book deal."
*I wrap my arm around Abigail.*
Madison: "I'm good. I've got everything I need right here."
*Abigail grins, putting her arm around me in turn.*
*Tara groans and makes an exaggerated gagging noise.*
Tara: "Okay, if you're gonna be that sappy all the time then-"
*Morgan interrupts whatever the consequences will be by leaning forward and kissing Tara on the lips.*
*That shuts her up fast.*
Morgan: "Then we'll just have to outdo them, right?"
*For maybe the second or third time ever, Tara looks like she doesn't know what to say.*
*But in a good way.*
*As we all laugh, Geladura approaches us, the rest of the fairies forming a cloud over her head.*
Geladura: "So. It's over."
Geladura: "There's much to be done. The forest will need time to heal, and recover."
Geladura: "It will take years. But it will happen."
Geladura: "Thank you, all of you. Mere words cannot express how grateful I am. How grateful the forest is."
Geladura: "Especially to you, Morgan and Abigail. Your family has suffered so much over the years."
*That's right. Geladura was there with Morgan throughout everything. She's the only one who can even come close to really understanding what Morgan's been through.*
Morgan: "It's over now, though. Just like the forest, it'll take time to heal, and recover."
Morgan: "But we'll be okay."
Geladura: "I know you will."
*Geladura faces away from me, looking out towards the forest at large and towards the waiting cluster of fairies. Her domain and her people.*
Tara: "What about the folks back in Eysenfeld? What'll happen to them?"
Geladura: "I imagine they'll recover from the glamour rather quickly. I wouldn't be surprised if they don't even remember Evelyn's existence."
Geladura: "There might be some difficulty as they figure out who will manage their affairs…"
*She turns back to us now, smiling. In particular, she looks at Morgan.*
Geladura: "But I don't imagine any of you will be around to concern yourselves with that."
Morgan: "Right."
*At the same time, Morgan and Tara reach for each other's hands. They smile at each other, meeting halfway.*
Geladura: "And you, Abigail. Are you looking forward to leaving?"
Abigail: "Certainly. I've heard so much about the rest of the world from Madison and from Tara already, that I can't wait to see it myself."
Abigail: "Although… I'm a little nervous about um… What were they called?"
Madison: "Airplanes."
Abigail: "Yes, those! I do hope they're safe."
*She looks to me for support, and I put my arm around her.*
Madison: "It'll be fine, I promise."
*She'll have a lot to get used to in the modern world, but it seems like she's just as excited to experience as I am to show it to her.*
*Even Morgan will have some acclimating to do, after spending her whole life with Eysenfeld's limited amenities.*
*It's fine, though. As Morgan mentioned… We have so much time.*
*The fairy queen smiles at us again.*
Geladura: "I see. If you don't mind, I'd like to show you just one last bit of magic. Something long overdue."
*The four of us look at each other, then nod in assent.*
Madison: "Lead the way."
*Geladura takes us around the pond to the far side, right to the boundary of bare ground and covered snow. She kneels down, scraping together a mound in her hands, while the rest of the fairies watch from above in reverent silence - their usual keening muted for now.*
*The ball of snow in Geladura's hands is shaped and smoothed out as she alternates between scraping and patting it.*
*Soon, I realize the form it's taking: that of one of the fairies.*
*Geladura then gathers several other materials from the forest debris: pine needles, a soggy leaf, a couple tiny pebbles, and even more objects that I can't see. She arranges the pebbles on her snow sculpture to form a face. Then, she deftly weaves together a few sodden pine needles and ties them around the back of the head.*
*I realize that they're supposed to be glasses, or at least an imitation of them.*
Abigail: "Madison! It's you!"
*As Geladura wraps the leaf around the snowfairy, I realize that Abigail's right. It's not an exact replica of course, but it's definitely supposed to be me.*
*Once it's complete, Geladura lifts the tiny model from the ground, holding it in her cupped hands like a treasure.*
*For a second, I think she's going to hand it to me, but she walks right past and heads to the shore of the pond.*
*With her back to us, she lifts her arms, holding the tiny sculpture aloft.*
*The moonlight shines down on her, illuminating the snow in her hands.*
*The air around them starts to glimmer and sparkle, the light of the moon refracting off of nothing.*
*Geladura's whole body glows with a soft white aura that's brightest in her hands.*
*Then, the little fairy in her hands flutters to life and flies into the air. After just two beats of its fragile wings, it turns and joins the rest of the swarm, where it's immediately accepted into the fold.*
*The fairy queen turns back to face us, the sparkling magic now calm once more.*
Geladura: "A tribute in your honor. This was the first birth of our people in over a century, ever since I was first bound."
Geladura: "Through this little one, your memory will live on in these woods."
Madison: "Thank you. It's… it's an honor. Really."
*It's a bit strange to me that she'd choose me as the model, rather than Morgan, whom she knows much better, or Abigail, who's far more intimately connected with the forest.*
*After all, it's not like I did a whole lot as fairy queen.*
*Still, the gesture is touching. I watch the little fairy for as long as I can, until it's assimilated into the rest and impossible to keep track of.*
*Tara's positively green with envy.*
Geladura: "Before you leave tomorrow, will you come join me for a final time? We have one last farewell to make."
*She looks at Abigail as she says it. Abigail's eyes widen, and then she nods.*
Tara: "We've got a late train, so it's easy to do."
Madison: "You already got us tickets home?"
Tara: "Hell yeah! One of us has to be on top of things around here."
*She smirks, and I playfully smack her shoulder.*
*Going home.*
*Just the thought of it is exciting. The prospect of returning to our normal lives, without having to fight for them… By now, it feels kind of like a dream.*
*But for once, one that I know will come true.*
*Once again, I wake up before Abigail does.*
*The bed isn't even close to big enough for both of us, but it didn't stop us from getting a full night's sleep.*
*She lays nestled against my side, breathing peacefully, while I contemplate the sunlight streaming in.*
*No matter where Tara and I travel to, I'm always looking forward to going home. That's especially true now. It feels weird to imagine that this time tomorrow, I'll be on a plane over the sea.*
*We still have a long day ahead of us, though. Our train doesn't depart until the afternoon, and then it's hours to the city. At least it'll be much more cheerful than my trip out here, when it was just Tara and me.*
*For as long as I can, I just remain in bed, cuddling close with Abigail. It's so nice to be able to just turn off my brain and relax. No deadlines, no threats, no flickering in and out of existence.*
*Peace.*
*It isn't too long before her eyes flutter and drift open. She yawns, cuddling even closer to me. Without moving the rest of my body, I turn my head to face her, and our noses brush together.*
Madison: "Good morning."
Abigail: "Mm. Good morning, Madison."
Madison: "How'd you sleep?"
Abigail: "It was the best sleep I've had in 200 years."
*She smirks, and I laugh too.*
Madison: "That joke never gets old. Fitting."
*Abigail moves in even closer, draping herself across my arm and shoulder like a cat. Her hair falls across my face, and I blow it away with a playful puff of air.*
Abigail: "What about you?"
Madison: "Better than I have in months."
*It's true. We both passed out pretty quickly after getting back to the cabin last night. Even the excitement of the day's events couldn't keep us up.*
Abigail: "Will our bed in America be this comfortable?"
Madison: "Oh, it's even better. Although BT, our cat, really likes to sleep up there. So you might have to fight him for the spot."
Abigail: "BT will have to find somewhere new to sleep, because I don't want to share you."
*She shoos away an imaginary cat with one hand. I love her.*
Madison: "Maybe he'll like Morgan. She's gotta be used to having cats around by now."
Madison: "Geladura counts. Technically."
Abigail: "Our home will be so lively, won't it?"
Madison: "Absolutely."
Abigail: "I can't wait."
Madison: "Me either."
*The sounds and smells of Morgan and Tara preparing breakfast wafting through the bedroom eventually stir us back into gear. I pack what little clothes I brought while Abigail gets dressed.*
Morgan: "Good morning."
Tara: "Meowdy, ny'all."
Madison: "Please don't ever say that again."
Madison: "But good morning."
Abigail: "Hello! What are we eating today?"
Tara: "Wow, you don't waste any time, huh?"
Abigail: "I've wasted a lot of time not eating, and I intend to fully make up for it."
Morgan: "You're in luck. I brought over all the food I had at home, so there's plenty."
*She sets a platter of pastries on the table, and Abigail immediately dual-wields a blueberry muffin and an everything bagel.*
Madison: "Wait, you already went all the way to town and back, and then made breakfast?"
Morgan: "Yes."
Tara: "Isn't she amazing?"
*Morgan blushes. That might be the first time I've witnessed that.*
Abigail: "She ish!"
*Crumbs litter the table, the aftermath of Abigail's enthusiastic eating. I've never met someone so delighted just to be eating food.*
Morgan: "Have as much as you'd like. I'll bring the rest with us on the train."
Tara: "I'm not sure we're gonna have to worry about leftovers."
*Abigail grins in agreement.*
*After we finish, I help take all the dishes back into the kitchen and set them by the sink. Morgan starts washing them right away.*
Morgan: "I'm not sure who I'm washing these for. But it feels wrong to just leave them dirty."
Madison: "What'll happen to this place once we're gone?"
*She shrugs.*
Morgan: "I'll leave the key in my shop. I don't really care beyond that."
Morgan: "It's nice to not have to."
*She wipes the plates dry with a towel, and then we go back into the living room.*
*The rest of the cabin is already immaculate.*
*The blankets are folded, the furniture is realigned. Everything is in its place. I'd even say it's tidier than the day we arrived.*
*I give my bedroom a final onceover, looking for anything I may have left behind.*
*Of course, there isn't anything.*
*I shut the door behind me. The action has a sense of finality to it.*
*Everyone else is waiting for me in the living room.*
Tara: "So we're going out into the woods one last time, right?"
Abigail: "Yes. I need to say goodbye to…"
Abigail: "A friend."
Tara: "Whoa! You know someone else in the forest?"
Tara: "Is it Elvis?"
Madison: "You're never gonna let that go, are you?"
Abigail: "Who's Elvis?"
Madison: "Don't worry about it, Tara's just being dumb."
Tara: "Aren't I always?"
Madison: "You've got me there."
Madison: "Anyway, you'll see when we arrive."
*I don't want to spoil the surprise. Plus, I want Abigail to be able to share a peaceful farewell. Especially remembering what she told me before about the fairy queen and the forest spirit.*
*We head outside and into the forest.*
*For the first time since we got to Eysenfeld, it's not that cold out. In fact, it's even a bit warm.*
*I wonder if that's coincidence, or if it also has something to do with Evelyn being gone. Neither would surprise me.*
*We haven't gone very far when Geladura suddenly appears, popping into sight just a few feet away.*
*Her arrival is silent - no smoke or sound or anything like that. Just… one second it's empty, and the next, she's there.*
Geladura: "Good morning."
*Morgan and Tara both flinch at her sudden arrival, but Abigail and I are unaffected.*
Geladura: "I've been waiting for you. I could feel the four of you enter the forest."
Tara: "That's awesome! So your fairy sense started tingling?"
*Geladura tilts her head, perplexed. Tara really needs to learn her audience out here.*
Geladura: "Anyway, if you're prepared, I'll take you all the rest of the way. It would take far too long to walk."
*The five of us join hands, Geladura standing between Morgan and me. Her hands are cold, but not unpleasantly so.*
*In the time that it takes for me to blink, we're teleported to the grove of ancestral trees. Even without being one with the forest, the magic in this place is palpable. It soaks into the earth and trees, and the air is sweet.*
*We approach the ancestral grove with careful, quiet steps.*
*The destruction that Evelyn caused is more prevalent here. Streaks of ash still blemish the surrounding trees, painful reminders of the one she slew.*
*Just ahead, I can see the clearing where I know the forest spirit will be. Geladura steps aside, gesturing for Abigail and I to continue on.*
*We do.*
*When the spirit comes into view, I can hear Tara gasp from behind me.*
*The spirit looks even weaker than the last time I saw it. If previously it looked old, now it looks downright ancient.*
*Its once terrifying eyes have lost their luster. Its wooden body is even more knotted and warped than before. And it's so, so small.*
Tara: "Is this… your friend?"
*Without turning around, Abigail nods.*
Abigail: "For all these years, we've been together. It was the forest spirit who saved my soul, when Evelyn came for my body."
*Abigail reaches out a hand, going to the forest spirit's side. Like every time before, I also keep my distance.*
Geladura: "Abigail…"
Abigail: "I know."
Abigail: "It's time, isn't it?"
*Geladura nods.*
*The forest spirit peers down at the girl by its side. I'm sure that it loves her back.*
*Abigail takes a single step backwards, and Geladura moves to her side.*
*Then, the fairy queen raises a palm, and presses it against the forest spirit.*
*The sensation of magic gathering is familiar to me by now, even though this time, it's flowing past me, not into me. Geladura calls life from the surrounding trees - here, where life is more vibrant. The ground ripples under our feet, surging with unseen power. The sunlight beaming onto us feels warmer. Everything is gloriously, happily, alive.*
*The forest spirit's twisted form starts to change and morph. Not in a painful way, like how Evelyn died, but much more peaceful. Jagged shards of wood soften and retract. Its two legs start to stitch themselves together into one solid trunk. Its arms stiffen - reaching outward and upwards - slowly regaining the collossal height that it once possessed. Roots appear and bury themselves underground, as the forest floor itself rises a little to accommodate.*
*I remember now that Abigail mentioned before that one of the fairy queen's duties was to help the forest spirit pass on. And with Geladura missing for so long, it was impossible. I wonder how many decades or centuries this one had existed before meeting Abigail. No wonder all the trees here have such vibrance. No wonder this is the center of life in the forest.*
*Just like the process that made me fairy queen, there's little pomp and flair to the transformation. But instead, it feels intimate and calm. When Geladura retracts her hand, and the magic dissipates once more, everything is still and silent.*
*Abigail says goodbye to the forest spirit, which has turned into a tree. Abigail kneels on the ground before it, with the spirit's eye being the only recognizable part of it.*
*Abigail falls to her knees at the base of the newborn tree. The spirit's great eye slowly slides down the trunk, a final magical motion, until it's level with her face.*
*Despite the tears in her eyes, Abigail manages a smile.*
Abigail: "Hello, old friend."
Abigail: "Thank you for everything you've given me. Not just company, but another chance at life itself."
Abigail: "I'll miss you. I really will. More than I'll miss anything else here."
Abigail: "I'm sorry I wasn't around much lately. But I hope that you know I was very happy."
Abigail: "Even when it was just us. Even when there was no one else."
*She reaches up, touching her hand to the tree just like the fairy queen did. Except it isn't a regal, powerful touch.*
*It's delicate and soft.*
*She presses her forehead against the tender bark, not bothering to wipe away the tears.*
Abigail: "And I hope that you know that I'll be even happier now. You don't have to worry about me."
Abigail: "I'll be okay."
*Abigail remains there for a long moment. The forest spirit's eye watches her, as best it can, for as long as it can.*
*Slowly, it goes dim. The red light fades to grey, and then to the same plain brown as the rest of the tree.*
*Within moments, it's indistinguishable from the rest of the forest.*
*Abigail looks back up, and notices the change. She brushes her hand across the sightless eye, and then she rises to face Geladura.*
Abigail: "Thank you."
Geladura: "I'm sure you made its life that much more wonderful, as well."
Abigail: "I hope so."
Abigail: "Tara, do we still have time before we have to board our train?"
Tara: "Couple hours, yeah."
Abigail: "If it's no trouble, I'd like to return to the fairy grove one final time."
Geladura: "I'd be happy to take you."
*Tara and Morgan nod in approval. They've both been keeping pretty quiet - although that's only unusual for Tara.*
*Still, I'm glad that she's being considerate of the solemn mood.*
*Once more, Geladura takes our hands and transports us to the fairy clearing.*
*There's no evidence here of what took place last night.*
*While there aren't any deer this time, a few birds are in their nests. All's right in the grove.*
*It's a little strange to not instantly feel the presence of the fairies in my head. I'm sure they're close by, but like usual, they keep their distance.*
*As soon as we regain our balance, Abigail wordlessly starts to head for the shore.*
*Tara taps me on the shoulder.*
Tara: "Hey, me and Morgan are gonna take a walk, give you two a couple minutes."
Madison: "Sure. Thanks."
*The two of them set off at a leisurely pace around the water. Geladura goes with them. She and Morgan will surely want to say goodbye as well.*
*Abigail's already down by the water, and I move to join her.*
*Her hand grasps mine, and she squeezes tightly.*
*She breathes in deeply. I do the same. The air is crisp, but refreshing. This place has been so special to both of us that I'm a little bit sad to leave it behind as well.*
*I know that's nothing compared to how Abigail must feel.*
*I don't speak. I'm sure that in her heart, Abigail's saying her final goodbyes to this place. I'll let her be the first to speak.*
*We stand there in comfortable silence for a while, hand in hand.*
Abigail: "I said before that I had come to love the forest as much as I possibly could. But now, when we're about to leave, I realize that I was wrong."
Abigail: "Because I love it more now than I ever have before."
Abigail: "It isn't that I want to stay. But rather, that I never had the opportunity to miss it. And now, on the eve of my departure… I'm sure that I will."
Abigail: "I think, there's a certain kind of love that comes from having something. But there's a different kind of love that can only come from losing it."
*To my surprise, she isn't crying. In the past, when we've talked like this, it's been accompanied by tears, both joyful and sad.*
*But now, when I look over at Abigail, she looks determined. Determined to be happy.*
Madison: "Just because you leave something behind doesn't mean you've lost it."
Madison: "It just means that you have to learn to love it in a different way."
Madison: "When you look back on something, and it makes you smile… That counts, too."
Madison: "Love isn't just about moving forward. Sometimes it's about looking back."
Madison: "This place will always be special to you. And to me. In different ways."
Abigail: "What if I forget? What if someday, I can't look back, and remember what it looks like? What if I can't remember how it felt the first time we kissed? What if I forget how happy I am, right now?"
Madison: "Even if you forget, you'll still carry it with you. It's still a part of who you are. It made you the person you are today."
Madison: "The memories aren't what's important. What's important is how they help you grow."
*We go quiet again after that. I surprised even myself with those words.*
*When I look back, five or ten or even fifty years from now, I wonder how I'll feel about this time in my life.*
*Not just the forest and all the magical things that have happened, but everything that led up to it.*
*Working on Taranormal, and all that we accomplished there. All the dreams that I put aside for it, but also the new ones that it instilled in me.*
*I still have a lot to figure out, and feelings to sort through. But now, the future doesn't seem too ominous and scary.*
*In fact, I'm looking forward to it.*
*From behind us, I can hear the sounds of Morgan and Tara approaching once more from their walk. I squeeze Abigail's hand, and she starts to lead me back toward our friends.*
Abigail: "Come on. Let's go home."
*It wasn't supposed to be like this. Just Tara and me, without Maddie and Abigail. And it's my fault. Something changed about the fairies' magic the instant Geladura and Evelyn died. Maddie and Abigail both faded away from sight. We didn't even get to say goodbye.*
*"It's not like that they're actually dead, y'know? So we don't really have to worry about them." Tara's been saying stuff like this since we got on the train. Since before then, in fact. Her wording makes it sound like she's trying to reassure me, but I know it's more for her own sake. Not that I mind. I'm willing to indulge anything that makes it easier for her.*
*"That's true. And with Maddie's magic, I'm sure they'll never be bored or lonely."*
*"Yeah, for sure. Plus I'm sure Mads can like, tell her all about film theory, recite the script to her favorite movies a bazillion times. Stuff like that. That… nerd…"*
*She's in tears before the sentence ends, and I pull her into a tight, guilty hug. The rumbling of the train makes it uncomfortable, its motions slamming her against my chin repeatedly, but I don't mind. As far as I'm concerned, I exist right now just to make things better for her. As much as I can. No matter how inadequate.*
*We haven't talked about what we'll do when we get back. The prospect of a future together is equal parts bright and ominous. I know we'll get through it, together. But I also know how hard it will be. Still, we've been through worse. Much worse.*
*Everything I own is here in this train car. The belongings that I wanted to take with me fit in a suitcase. I didn't say goodbye to anyone. I'm sure the village will be lost for a while without Evelyn, as they recover from her influence. But just like the rest of us, they'll be better off in the long run.*
*We left most of Maddie's things behind. Just in case she comes back for them, Tara explained. We also left two train tickets on the table in the cabin. Tara had printed four before we had even reunited with them. Just in case.*
*For so many years, I dreamed of a train ride like this. A one-way trip out of Eysenfeld, and towards a new life. There are still hours to go before we get to the city, and then hours of waiting for our flight, and then hours to be spent in the sky. I wish I could sleep through them all. Neither Tara nor I got any rest last night, even though we tried. We just spent the whole time tossing and turning.*
*In my mind's eye, I kept replaying those final moments. As I hesitated, and as Geladura sacrificed herself. It was only due to the rippling moonlight that she was able to stop Evelyn. To save Tara and I. If I had done something differently, then I'm sure it wouldn't have ended that way. Maddie and Abigail could be here on the train with us, Geladura back with the fairies.*
*Except, this is real life. My choices brought us to this point, and now we have to live with them. All I can do now is make the best of what we have left. Even if it takes years, I know that we can heal. Together. I'm looking forward to that day. I'll become the kind of person that Tara deserves.*
*I'm happy for them. I really am. I think Mads has tried to hide it from me. I can hear the smiles and excitement in her voice. Even when they don't show up on her face. And the way she looks at Abby. The way they hold hands. She's just really delicate in a way I've never seen her before. The change is obvious. I've gotta be supportive, right? Any good friend would be.*
*It's just…*
*Our last few days in Eysenfeld feel like they're gone in an instant. I guess I'm also kinda zoned out during them. It's hard to describe but… none of it feels real. It's like I'm watching myself in one of my own videos. I can hear my voice, hear myself laugh and joke, but that's not me.*
*I'll be sitting in the living room and look at a clock and it'll be midnight. I won't even remember doing anything else that day. And then I'll just go to sleep, right there on the couch. I… can't go back into that room. And it's like that for… however long we have left. It's all hazy. I don't even remember packing.*
*And then we're at the train station, getting ready to board. Abby is pointing at everything and asking all kinds of questions. I don't think she's ever seen a train before. Mads is so patient with her, she even seems to enjoy explaining all this stuff.*
*They're good together.*
*Before we get on board Mads grabs my arm and pulls me aside.*
*"Hey… Listen," she says. "I just want you to know that–"*
*I interrupt her. "I'm ok Mads. Really. You don't have to worry." I flash her the old grin and finger guns. I've done it so many times on camera when I was sick or tired that I don't think even Maddie can see my heart isn't really in it this time. At least I hope not.*
*She wraps her arms around me anyway. "I just want you to know that I'm here for you."*
*I can feel the tears want to come out. I'm not gonna let them. I wrench my eyelids shut and take a deep breath. That's all I've got. My pride. Mads steps back, hands on my shoulders. She smiles at me. Abby smiles at me. Both of them have this look of pity in their eyes, that they don't even try to hide. That's the last thing I want.*
*"Come on, let's get out of here," Maddie says, and she leads me onto the train.*
*Abby's a big bundle of giggles and excitement. You'd think it was already Christmas by how she's acting. Then again, it's probably better than Christmas for her.*
*"Ahh, I can't believe we're finally leaving. I never thought this day would come. I never even dreamed about it. And now… it's a little scary!" Abby says, her eyes shining. She's literally vibrating in her seat.*
*It's scary for me to leave too. Just not in the same way.*
*I realize I'm staring at her too late. Our eyes meet and I look away towards the window, but before I do I see that look on her face again: pity, mixed with guilt. She doesn't even say much after that. At least not that I can hear. She and Mads kinda just whisper to each other while I stare out the window, watching the forests and mountains pass by.*
*I don't know what I'm gonna do. I was just starting to have all these plans. Things were starting to feel certain again and now… There had to be some other way. There had to be. I'm not sure who to be angry at. I don't know who to blame.*
*Hours pass. It's hard to tell. Abby falls asleep with her head on Maddie's shoulder, and Maddie just keeps shooting me these concerned glances every time she looks up from the book she's reading.*
*She doesn't say anything though. She probably doesn't know what to say. I sure as hell don't. I lay my head against the glass. It's cool and the way my head bounces off it as the train goes along is kinda soothing.*
*I wonder how this train ride would have gone if…*
*Would I be the one with my head on Morgan's shoulder, sleeping, like Abby is with Mads? I'm glad they're getting their happy ending. They deserve it. They really do. But what about what Morgan deserved? What about what I deserved? It.. It doesn't feel fair. It'd doesn't feel right.*
*But it does feel real. I wish it didn't feel so real.*
*I sigh. They look so content, the two of them. A good friend would be happy for them.*
*God I wish I could be.*
*"All aboard!"*
*The train conductor yells out his iconic line, and there's a loud hiss as the train begins to move. It feels like something out of an old-timey film, but I suppose that's more than appropriate for how our journey has gone so far.*
*Beside me, Abigail sits with a white-knuckled grip on the seats, alternating between staring out the window and making sure the ground is still beneath her. Across from me, Tara and Morgan sprawl across each other, all pretenses of "not a couple" abandoned. As for me? I drink it all in, with a smile on my face. Every one of us deserves this.*
*We pull out of the station, and I gaze out the window alongside Abigail. On the way in, I remember how scary and foreboding the forest looked, and how it felt like entering another world. Now, on the way out… I certainly won't miss it, but I'm not afraid anymore. I wonder if Geladura is watching us, somewhere out there.*
*"Hey Mads." Of course Tara's the one to break the silence. "How proud do you think my folks will be that I finally got around to adding 'forged government documents' to my criminal record?"*
*I just have to laugh. I can't even be mad at her for this one, because there's no way we're getting Abigail into the country otherwise. I'm not sure how she did it. Maybe that's a brand of magic of her own.*
*"What will be the first thing we do when we get back?" asks Abigail.*
*"Oh, you know we're gonna party." From where her head rests on Morgan's lap, Tara still manages to strike a pose. "We've got new year's coming up, plus Maddie's birthday is the day before."*
*Both Abigail and Morgan's eyes widen. I try to brush it off. Every year, Tara swears we're going to go "all out" for my birthday. Every year, she takes like two drinks and passes out, or falls out of her barstool. Whichever comes first.*
*"Morgan, Abby, both of you drink, right?"*
*"I do."*
*"It's been quite a long time since I have, of course, but I'm certainly partial to it."*
*"You both know what that means then, right?"*
*Tara's question is met with three blank stares.*
*"DRINKING CONTEST!"*
*"Oh God, Tara. Not again."*
*"Come on, you're okay with Morgan and Abby watching you drunkenly sing MCR, right?"*
*"No, I'm not okay."*
*While Tara launches into an extremely exaggerated version of my last birthday party for Morgan and Abigail's benefits, I find myself looking out the window, watching the world go by once again. There's so much that I'm looking forward to when we get home. Teaching Abigail about the world, going back to school… I'll also do my best to help Morgan take over the duties on the show. Maybe I'll even come back to guest star in an episode or two.*
*For so long, I wanted the world to slow down, just a little bit, so I could catch up. Figure things out. Figure myself out. If there's one thing that being dead, and hearing Abigail's stories taught me, it's that I don't want to look back later with regret. Just like I told her at the lake, we have a lot of good memories left to create. I intend to savor every minute of them.*
*I listen to the conversation, laughing along even while I daydream about the future. My heart is happy here, even if my head's in the clouds right now. Abigail leans into my side, giggling at some joke whose punchline I missed, but I smile all the same. She'll make an optimist out of me yet. If there's one thing that we have now, it's time.*
*And as for everything else, well… I'm sure the world won't mind if I keep it waiting just a bit longer.*
*It takes a week or two before Tara is able to go into Maddie's room. She only stays in there for a few minutes before the tears start flowing.*
*After three weeks, she calls Maddie's family. I hold her hand while she listens to them sob. They don’t speak to her again after that.*
*It takes two months for her to be ready to start clearing out Maddie's room. BT keeps scratching at the door, waiting for Maddie to open it and let him in.*
*It takes a long time for her to make her video about what happened. She keeps it vague. She moves on to something new.*
